'''Abel Tesfaye'''{{Efn|{{langx|am|አቤል ተስፋዬ}}}} (born '''Abel Makkonen Tesfaye''';{{Efn|{{langx|am|አቤል መኮንን ተስፋዬ}}}} February 16, 1990), known by his [[stage name]] '''the<!-- lowercase per [[MOS:THENICKNAME]] --> Weeknd''', is a Canadian singer, songwriter, record producer, and actor.<!--Keep notable occupations in lead per [[MOS:ROLEBIO]].--><ref name="Fantasy3">{{Cite news |last=Eells |first=Josh |date=October 21, 2015 |title=Sex, Drugs and R&B: Inside The Weeknd's Dark Twisted Fantasy |magazine=[[Rolling Stone]] |url=https://www.rollingstone.com/music/music-news/sex-drugs-and-rb-inside-the-weeknds-dark-twisted-fantasy-176091/ |url-status=live |url-access=limited |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20180615145745/https://www.rollingstone.com/music/features/sex-drugs-and-r-b-inside-the-weeknds-dark-twisted-fantasy-20151021 |archive-date=June 15, 2018|mode=cs2}}</ref><ref>{{Cite magazine |last1=Donnelly |first1=Matt |last2=Sharf |first2=Zack |date=May 22, 2023 |title='The Idol' Scandalizes Cannes With Five-Minute Standing Ovation for Lily-Rose Depp's Masturbating Pop Star, Explicit Nudity and the Weeknd's TV Acting Debut |url=https://variety.com/2023/tv/news/the-idol-cannes-weeknd-standing-ovation-lily-rose-depp-nudity-1235621084/ |access-date=June 6, 2023 |magazine=[[Variety (magazine)|Variety]]|mode=cs2|archive-date=October 29, 2023 |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20231029161015/https://variety.com/2023/tv/news/the-idol-cannes-weeknd-standing-ovation-lily-rose-depp-nudity-1235621084/ |url-status=live }}</ref> Regarded as an influential figure in [[popular music]], he is known for his light-lyric [[tenor]] vocal range and [[falsetto]], [[alternative R&B]] sound, and dark aesthetics, as well as the cinematic visuals and storytelling of [[The Weeknd videography|his music videos]]. [[List of awards and nominations received by the Weeknd|His accolades]] include 4 [[Grammy Awards]], 20 [[Billboard Music Awards|''Billboard'' Music Awards]], 22 [[Juno Award]]s, 6 [[American Music Awards]], 3 [[MTV Video Music Awards]], and a [[Latin Grammy Awards|Latin Grammy Award]].

Tesfaye began releasing music [[anonymity|anonymously]] in 2009. After co-founding the record label [[XO (record label)|XO]], he released three mixtapes—''[[House of Balloons]]'', ''[[Thursday (album)|Thursday]]'', and ''[[Echoes of Silence]]''—in 2011. He signed with [[Republic Records]] to compile the mixtapes into the compilation album ''[[Trilogy (The Weeknd album)|Trilogy]]'' (2012), before releasing his debut studio album, ''[[Kiss Land]]'' (2013). Following collaborations and film soundtrack contributions from 2013 and 2014, Tesfaye blended alternative R&B with [[Pop music|pop]] on his second and third studio albums, ''[[Beauty Behind the Madness]]'' (2015) and ''[[Starboy (album)|Starboy]]'' (2016); both debuted atop the US [[Billboard 200|''Billboard'' 200]] and featured the [[Billboard Hot 100|''Billboard'' Hot 100]] number-one singles "[[Can't Feel My Face]]", "[[The Hills (song)|The Hills]]", "[[Starboy (song)|Starboy]]", and "[[Die for You (The Weeknd song)|Die for You]]". His debut [[extended play]] (EP), ''[[My Dear Melancholy]]'' (2018), featured the US top-ten single "[[Call Out My Name]]".

Tesfaye began an album trilogy based on three time points, starting with the [[dream pop]]– and [[new wave music|new wave]]–inspired album ''[[After Hours (The Weeknd album)|After Hours]]'' (2020), which spawned the chart-topping singles "[[Heartless (The Weeknd song)|Heartless]]" and "[[Save Your Tears]]", as well as "[[Blinding Lights]]"—the [[List of Billboard Hot 100 chart achievements and milestones#Top 10 songs of all time (1958–2021)|best-performing song in the ''Billboard'' Hot 100's history]] and the [[List of Spotify streaming records#Most-streamed songs|most-streamed song]] on [[Spotify]]. The trilogy's latter two installments, ''[[Dawn FM]]'' (2022) and ''[[Hurry Up Tomorrow]]'' (2025), featured the US top-ten singles, "[[Take My Breath]]" and "[[Timeless (The Weeknd and Playboi Carti song)|Timeless]]".

Tesfaye is one of the [[List of best-selling music artists|best-selling]] and [[List of most-streamed artists on Spotify#First 20 years|most-streamed artists of all time]]. He has amassed eight diamond-certified singles from the [[Recording Industry Association of America]] (RIAA).<ref>{{Cite web |title=Gold & Platinum |url=https://www.riaa.com/gold-platinum/ |access-date=October 30, 2025 |publisher=Recording Industry Association of America |archive-date=January 3, 2016 |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20160103172359/http://www.riaa.com/goldandplatinumdata.php?table=SEARCH |url-status=live }}</ref> The first artist to ever surpass 100 million monthly listeners on [[Spotify]] and the [[Forbes list of the world's highest-paid musicians#Highest-paid musicians by year|highest-paid musician in 2025]], he ranked among the world's [[Time 100|100 most influential people]] by ''[[Time (magazine)|Time]]'' in 2020.<ref name=Rowley>{{Cite magazine |last=Rowley |first=Glenn |date=February 27, 2023 |title=The Weeknd Becomes First Artist to Hit This Spotify Milestone |url=https://www.billboard.com/music/music-news/the-weeknd-spotify-100-million-monthly-listeners-1235263328/ |access-date=February 28, 2023 |magazine=Billboard |language=en-US |archive-date=February 28, 2023 |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20230228015429/https://www.billboard.com/music/music-news/the-weeknd-spotify-100-million-monthly-listeners-1235263328/ |url-status=live }}</ref> His [[After Hours til Dawn Tour]] (2022–2026) set the record for the highest-grossing tour by a male soloist in history.<ref>{{Cite magazine |date=December 30, 2025|title=The Weeknd's 'After Hours Til Dawn' Tour Is the Top-Grossing Male Solo Tour Ever |url=https://www.rollingstone.com/music/music-news/the-weeknd-top-grossing-male-tour-1235469507/ |access-date=February 12, 2026|magazine=Rolling Stone |language=en |archive-date=January 8, 2026 |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20260108112646/https://www.rollingstone.com/music/music-news/the-weeknd-top-grossing-male-tour-1235469507/ |url-status=live }}</ref> Tesfaye also co-created and starred in the [[HBO]] drama series ''[[The Idol (TV series)|The Idol]]'' (2023) and the film [[Hurry Up Tomorrow (film)|''Hurry Up Tomorrow'']] (2025). His other ventures include hosting the [[Apple Music 1]] radio show ''[[Memento Mori (radio show)|Memento Mori]]'' (2018–2022), being appointed [[goodwill ambassador]] for the [[World Food Programme]] in 2021, and advocacy for [[racial equality]] and [[food security]].

== Life and career ==

educated at [[West Hill Collegiate Institute]] and [[Birchmount Park Collegiate Institute]].<ref>{{Cite news |title=Scarborough native The Weeknd scores seven Grammy nominations |url=https://www.toronto.com/news-story/6163335-scarborough-native-the-weeknd-scores-seven-grammy-nominations/ |url-status=live first=Tara | last=Hatherly | date=December 7, 2015 | work=[[Metroland Media Group]]}}</ref>

At seventeen, Tesfaye dropped out of school and relocated to an apartment in the neighbourhood of [[Parkdale, Toronto|Parkdale]] with two friends, one of whom was [[La Mar Taylor]]—his best friend and now [[creative director]].<ref name="veryown">{{Cite news |last=Mistry |first=Anupa |title=Why The Weeknd is truly Toronto's very own |work=[[The Globe and Mail]] |url=https://www.theglobeandmail.com/news/toronto/why-the-weeknd-is-truly-torontos-very-own/article27053702/ |url-status=live |date=October 30, 2015 |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20210107223920/https://www.theglobeandmail.com/news/toronto/why-the-weeknd-is-truly-torontos-very-own/article27053702/ |archive-date=January 7, 2021|mode=cs2}}</ref> They lived a [[Hedonism|hedonistic]] lifestyle, and paid their $850 monthly rent using [[Social programs in Canada|welfare]] checks. Later, Tesfaye earned money by selling [[Cannabis (drug)|cannabis]] and folding clothes at an [[American Apparel]] store in downtown Toronto, where his coworkers played his early music without realizing it was his.<ref name="Biggest" /><ref>{{Cite news |title=The Weeknd Reveals the Humble Job He Had Before Becoming Famous — and How He Still Works with His Old Boss |url=https://people.com/the-weeknd-reveals-the-job-he-had-before-fame-he-still-works-with-his-old-boss-11736482 |access-date=2026-09-03 |work=People.com |language=en |url-status=live }}</ref><ref name="Know">{{Cite news |last1=Wagstaff |first1=Keith |last2=Swanson |first2=David |title=10 Things You Should Know 'Drugs were a crutch for me'|newspaper=[[The Guardian]]|date=December 3, 2016|access-date=March 11, 2024|archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20231129120504/https://www.theguardian.com/music/2016/dec/03/the-weeknd-abel-tesfaye-interview-music-tom-lamont|archive-date=November 29, 2023|url-status=live|mode=cs2|first=Tom|last=Lamont}}</ref> Tesfaye frequently engaged in drug use, including substances such as [[ketamine]], [[cocaine]], [[MDMA]], [[Psilocybin mushroom|magic mushrooms]], and [[Cold medicine|cough syrup]],<ref>{{Cite news |last=Fisher |first=Luchina |author-link=Luchina Fisher |date=December 5, 2016 |title=The Weeknd Opens Up About His 'Dark' Past |agency=[[ABC News (United States)|ABC News]] |url=https://abcnews.go.com/Entertainment/weeknd-opens-dark-past/story?id=43987671 |url-status=live |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20210107224046/https://abcnews.go.com/Entertainment/weeknd-opens-dark-past/story?id=43987671 |archive-date=January 7, 2021|mode=cs2}}</ref> stating that drugs were a "crutch" for him when he wrote music.<ref>{{Cite magazine |date=December 3, 2016 |title=The Weeknd opens up about 'heavy' drug use |magazine=[[NME]] |url=https://www.nme.com/news/music/weeknd-opens-drug-use-1888046 |url-status=live |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20210107224108/https://www.nme.com/news/music/weeknd-opens-drug-use-1888046 |archive-date=January 7, 2021|mode=cs2|first=Nick|last=Levine|access-date=March 11, 2024}}</ref> He adopted his stage name because he moved to Parkdale on a weekend; he removed the last 'e' in 'weekend' to avoid [[trademark]] issues with the Canadian [[pop rock]] band [[The Weekend (Canadian band)|the Weekend]].<ref name=Went>{{Cite news |last=Ehrlich |first=Brenna |date=September 13, 2013 |title=The Weeknd Reveals How He Got His Name... And Where The 'E' Went |publisher=MTV News |url=http://www.mtv.com/news/1714103/the-weeknd-reddit-ama/ |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20210107223909/http://www.mtv.com/news/1714103/the-weeknd-reddit-ama/ |archive-date=January 7, 2021 |url-status=dead}}</ref><ref name="Biggest" /> Before releasing music under his current stage name, he went under the alias "Kin Kane", as part of a hip-hop duo called "Bulleez n Nerdz",<ref name="Fantasy3" /> and was part of a production team called 'the Noise'.<ref>{{cite web |last=Breihan |first=Tom |date=May 25, 2011 |title=Hear Unreleased Early Tracks From the Weeknd |url=https://pitchfork.com/news/42635-hear-unreleased-early-tracks-from-the-weeknd/ |url-status=live |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20240220203713/https://pitchfork.com/news/42635-hear-unreleased-early-tracks-from-the-weeknd/ |archive-date=February 20, 2024 |access-date=February 20, 2024 |website=[[Pitchfork (website)|Pitchfork]] |mode=cs2}}</ref><ref name="Screwed">{{Cite news |last=McGuire |first=Patrick |date=March 22, 2012 |title=How the Producer of The Weeknd's Breakout Tracks Got Majorly Screwed |work=[[Vice (magazine)|Vice]] |url=https://www.vice.com/en/article/how-the-producer-of-the-weeknds-breakout-tracks-got-majorly-screwed/ |url-status=live |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20121224110035/http://www.vice.com/read/how-the-producer-of-the-weeknds-breakout-tracks-got-majorly-screwed |archive-date=December 24, 2012|mode=cs2}}</ref>

=== 2009–2014: ''Trilogy'' and ''Kiss Land'' ===
[[File:The Weeknd by David Hwang.jpg|thumb|alt=The Weeknd wearing a button-up, sleeveless shirt covering a normal black t-shirt. He is performing on a stage, singing into a microphone, with palm trees in the background.|Tesfaye performing at the [[Coachella Valley Music and Arts Festival]] in 2012]]
In August 2009, Tesfaye began anonymously releasing music on [[YouTube]].<ref>{{Cite web|date=December 27, 2019|title=10-Year High: The Weeknd's Game-Changing Decade|url=https://www.papermag.com/the-weeknd-2010s-career-2642233169.html|mode=cs2|access-date=December 20, 2021|website=[[Paper (magazine)|Paper]]|language=en|archive-date=March 8, 2022|archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20220308153108/https://www.papermag.com/the-weeknd-2010s-career-2642233169.html|url-status=live}}</ref><ref name="before" /> The following year, he met the producer Jeremy Rose at a party. Rose asked Tesfaye if he wanted to work together as a dark R&B project after hearing him freestyle over an instrumental. After creating multiple songs and parting ways due to creative differences, Tesfaye was allowed to use the songs they made together under the condition that Rose received production credits.<ref name="Screwed" /> In December 2010, Tesfaye uploaded "[[What You Need (The Weeknd song)|What You Need]]", "[[Loft Music (song)|Loft Music]]" and "[[The Morning (The Weeknd song)|The Morning]]" to YouTube under the username "xoxxxoooxo".<ref name="nytimes">{{Cite news |last=Caramanica |first=Jon | author-link=Jon Caramanica |title=Screams That Charmed, and Other Overlooked Highlights |newspaper=[[The New York Times]] |url=https://www.nytimes.com/2011/01/02/arts/music/02playlist.html |url-status=live |date=December 31, 2010 | url-access=limited |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20150210094135/http://www.nytimes.com/2011/01/02/arts/music/02playlist.html?_r=4&ref=music |archive-date=February 10, 2015|mode=cs2}}</ref><ref>{{Cite news |last=Stewart |first=Allison |date=March 14, 2011 |title=Singles File: Da Phuture, The Weeknd, Fever Ray |mode=cs2|newspaper=[[The Washington Post]] |url=https://www.washingtonpost.com/wp-dyn/content/article/2011/03/14/AR2011031403984.html |url-status=live |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20131208064202/http://www.washingtonpost.com/wp-dyn/content/article/2011/03/14/AR2011031403984.html |archive-date=December 8, 2013}}</ref> His identity remained undisclosed initially. These songs gained attention online and were later acknowledged in a blog post by the rapper [[Drake (musician)|Drake]].<ref name="Screwed" /><ref>{{Cite news |last=Shepherd |first=Julianne Escobedo |date=March 23, 2011 |title=Love and Other Drugs: The Weeknd's Altered-State R&B |url=https://www.thirteen.org/riffcity/music/love-and-other-drugs-the-weeknds-altered-state-rb/ |url-status=live |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20201129222613/https://www.thirteen.org/riffcity/music/love-and-other-drugs-the-weeknds-altered-state-rb/ |archive-date=November 29, 2020 |publisher=[[WNET]]|mode=cs2}}</ref> The songs subsequently received coverage from various media outlets, including ''[[Pitchfork (website)|Pitchfork]]'' and ''[[The New York Times]]''.<ref>{{Cite news |last=Fitzmaurice |first=Larry |title=The Weeknd – ''What You Need'' |url=https://pitchfork.com/reviews/tracks/12131-the-weeknd-what-you-need/ |url-status=live |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20110326174005/http://pitchfork.com/reviews/tracks/12131-what-you-need |archive-date=March 26, 2011 |date=March 4, 2011 | website=[[Pitchfork (website)|Pitchfork]]|mode=cs2}}</ref>

In 2011, Tesfaye met music executives [[Wassim Slaiby|Wassim "Sal" Slaiby]] and [[Amir Esmailian|Amir "Cash" Esmailian]], with whom, along with Taylor, he founded the [[XO (record label)|XO]] record label; earlier in 2009, Tesfaye and Taylor, along with friend and later photographer and collaborator Hyghly Alleyne formed a multimedia collective, She's So Lovely via [[Tumblr]].<ref name="Bain2021">{{Cite news |last=Bain |first=Katie |date=January 28, 2021 |title=The Weeknd on the Crew That Boosted Him From 'Basically Homeless' to the Super Bowl |url=https://www.billboard.com/articles/business/9516909/the-weeknd-xo-records-billboard-cover-story-interview-2021 |url-access=subscription |url-status=live |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20210128235510/https://www.billboard.com/articles/business/9516909/the-weeknd-xo-records-billboard-cover-story-interview-2021/ |archive-date=January 28, 2021 |magazine=[[Billboard (magazine)|Billboard]]}}</ref> On March 21, Tesfaye released his debut mixtape, ''[[House of Balloons]]'',<ref name=Kameir>{{Cite web |last=Kameir |first=Rawiya |last2=Hockley-Smith |first2=Sam |last3=Mistry |first3=Anupa |date=March 21, 2016 |title=How ''House of Balloons'' Changed R&B |url=https://www.thefader.com/2016/03/21/house-of-balloons-rountable-the-weeknd |website=[[The Fader]] |access-date=September 10, 2026 |archive-date=January 7, 2021 |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20210107223914/https://www.thefader.com/2016/03/21/house-of-balloons-rountable-the-weeknd |url-status=live }}</ref><ref>{{Cite news |last=Ramirez |first=Erika |title=Say Hello to The Weeknd, Drake Co-Signs |url=http://www.mtv.com/news/genre/hip-hop//2011/03/21/say-hello-to-the-weeknd-drake-co-signs/ |url-status=dead |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20151012012132/http://rapfix.mtv.com/2011/03/21/say-hello-to-the-weeknd-drake-co-signs/ |archive-date=October 12, 2015 |date=March 21, 2011 | website=[[MTV News]]|mode=cs2}}</ref><ref>{{Cite web | url=https://www.cbcmusic.ca/posts/17548/cohen-gould-buffy-25-best-canadian-debut-albums | title=25 best Canadian debut albums ever | archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20170906183203/http://www.cbcmusic.ca/posts/17548/cohen-gould-buffy-25-best-canadian-debut-albums | archive-date=September 6, 2017 | work=[[CBC Music]] | date=June 16, 2017|mode=cs2|url-status=live}}</ref> which featured production from [[Illangelo]] and [[Doc McKinney]]. The mixtape also included tracks produced by Rose, although he did not receive production credits.<ref name="Screwed" /> ''House of Balloons'' was named as one of the ten shortlisted nominees for the [[2011 Polaris Music Prize]].<ref>{{Cite web |title=Polaris Music Prize |url=https://polarismusicprize.ca/album/house-of-balloons/ |url-status=live |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20140409053023/http://www.polarismusicprize.ca/2011/ |archive-date=April 9, 2014 |website=[[Polaris Music Prize]]|mode=cs2}}</ref><ref name=":1">{{Cite news |last=Friend |first=David |date=September 17, 2011 |title=Great days for Weeknd |url=https://variety.com/2011/music/news/great-days-for-weeknd-1118042986/ |access-date=July 29, 2025 |work=Variety |archive-date=August 19, 2025 |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20250819201152/https://variety.com/2011/music/news/great-days-for-weeknd-1118042986/ |url-status=live }}</ref> Tesfaye started working with Drake in May, eventually earning a spot at the latter's [[OVO Fest]] on July 31.<ref>{{Cite news |last=Fitzmaurice |first=Larry |date=July 3, 2011 |title=The Weeknd to Play Drake's OVO Festival |url=https://pitchfork.com/news/42716-the-weeknd-to-play-drakes-ovo-festival/ |url-status=live |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20210107223855/https://pitchfork.com/news/42716-the-weeknd-to-play-drakes-ovo-festival/ |archive-date=January 7, 2021 |access-date=March 11, 2024 |website=[[Pitchfork (website)|Pitchfork]] |mode=cs2}}</ref> That month, Tesfaye held his first live performance at [[The Mod Club]] in Toronto, which received media attention for Drake being in attendance.<ref>{{Cite news |last=Ritchie |first=Kevin |date=July 25, 2011 |title=The Weeknd's perfect premiere |work=[[NOW Toronto]] |url=https://nowtoronto.com/the-weeknds-perfect-premiere |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20111222100021/http://www.nowtoronto.com/daily/music/story.cfm?content=181922 |archive-date=December 22, 2011 |access-date=March 3, 2011 |mode=cs2 |url-status=dead}}</ref><ref name=":1" />  He also participated in concerts hosted by the Black Student Association at the [[University of Toronto]].<ref name="before" /> In summer 2011, he also received media attention in the United States when his song "[[High for This|High For This]]" was featured in an ad campaign for the final season of HBO's "[[Entourage (American TV series)|Entourage]]."<ref name=":1" /> On August 18, Tesfaye released his second mixtape, ''[[Thursday (album)|Thursday]]'', which garnered usually positive reviews.<ref>{{Cite web |last=Diver |first=Mike |title=BBC - Music - Review of The Weeknd - Thursday |url=https://www.bbc.co.uk/music/reviews/jgq4/ |url-status=live |mode=cs2}}</ref> Tesfaye contributed to four songs on Drake's second studio album, ''[[Take Care (album)|Take Care]]'', released on November 15, as a songwriter, producer and a featured artist on the album's seventh single, "[[Crew Love]]".<ref>{{Cite magazine|last=Berry|first=Peter A.|date=November 16, 2017|title=Drake Sets The Record Straight On How Much The Weeknd Wrote On Take Care Album|magazine=[[XXL (magazine)|XXL]]|url=https://www.xxlmag.com/news/2017/11/drake-the-weeknd-wrote-take-care/|url-status=live|archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20210107223930/https://www.xxlmag.com/drake-the-weeknd-wrote-take-care/|archive-date=January 7, 2021|mode=cs2}}</ref> He released his third mixtape, ''[[Echoes of Silence]]'', on December 21. It was a long-listed nominee for the [[2012 Polaris Music Prize]].<ref>{{Cite news |last=Dunlevy |first=T'Cha |date=March 23, 2012 |title=365 days of Weeknd: We chart Abel Tesfaye's rise to fame a year after House of Balloons |url=https://nationalpost.com/entertainment/365-days-of-weeknd-we-chart-abel-tesfayes-rise-to-fame-a-year-after-house-of-balloons |url-status=live |archive-url=http://webarchive.loc.gov/all/20120321220120/http://arts.nationalpost.com/2012/03/20/365%2Ddays%2Dof%2Dweeknd%2Dwe%2Dchart%2Dabel%2Dtesfayes%2Drise%2Dto%2Dfame%2Da%2Dyear%2Dafter%2Dhouse%2Dof%2Dballoons/ |archive-date=March 21, 2012 |access-date=March 11, 2024 |newspaper=[[National Post]]}}</ref><ref>{{Cite news |last=Mudhar |first=Raju |date=June 14, 2012 |title=Polaris Prize long list includes lots of Toronto bands |url=https://www.thestar.com/entertainment/polaris-prize-long-list-includes-lots-of-toronto-bands/article_318d3259-7684-58cc-ba52-9154580ff77c.html |url-status=live |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20141103215956/http://www.thestar.com/entertainment/2012/06/14/polaris_prize_long_list_includes_lots_of_toronto_bands.html |archive-date=November 3, 2014 |access-date=September 28, 2021 |newspaper=[[The Toronto Star]] |language=en-CA |issn=0319-0781 |url-access=subscription |mode=cs2}}</ref><ref name=":1" />

In April 2012, Tesfaye began performing at more shows, such as the [[Coachella Valley Music and Arts Festival|Coachella Festival]],<ref name="debut">{{Cite news |last=Snapes |first=Laura |title=Abel website=[[Pitchfork (website)|Pitchfork]]|access-date=March 11, 2024|mode=cs2}}</ref> and two sold-out shows at the [[Bowery Ballroom]] in New York City.<ref name="debut" /><ref>{{Cite news |last=Krishnamurthy |first=Sowmya |author-link=Sowmya Krishnamurthy |date=April 29, 2012 |title=The Weeknd Mesmerizes at Bowery Ballroom | url-access=subscription|mode=cs2}}</ref> He also performed at various European festivals, such as [[Primavera Sound]] in Spain and Portugal<ref>{{Cite news | url=https://pitchfork.com/news/45598-the-weeknd-to-headline-pitchfork-stage-at-primavera/ | title=The Weeknd to Headline Pitchfork Stage at Primavera | first=Carrie | last=Battan | work=[[Pitchfork Festival]] in the United Kingdom.<ref>{{Cite news |last=Lough |first=Kate |date=May 31, 2012 |title=Wireless Festival 2012 |work=[[London Evening Standard]] | url-status=live|access-date=March 11, 2024|mode=cs2}}</ref> In September, Tesfaye signed with [[Republic Records]]; XO was assumed as a [[subsidiary|subsidiary label]].<ref>{{Cite magazine |last=Hampp |first=Andrew |title=The Weeknd & Reps Talk Clearing Samples, Touring For 'Trilogy' Release |archive-date=January 7, 2021 |date=November 12, 2012 |mode=cs2}}</ref> That same month, he embarked on his first concert tour, the Fall Tour, which included his own headlining shows and some opening shows for the English band [[Florence and the Machine]]. The tour was performed in North America in September to November.<ref>{{cite news|url=https://exclaim.ca/music/article/weeknd_schedules_more_canadian_dates_on_north_american_fall_tour|title=The Weeknd 2021|mode=cs2|url-status=live|magazine=[[Exclaim!]]}}</ref> On November 13, Tesfaye released ''[[Trilogy (The Weeknd album)|Trilogy]]'', a compilation album comprising re-mixed and remastered versions of his 2011 mixtapes, and three additional tracks.<ref>{{Cite news |last=Diver |first=Mike |title=The Weeknd Trilogy Review |url=https://www.bbc.co.uk/music/reviews/gr5c/ |url-status=live [[File:The Weeknd at Massey Hall October 17, 2013 amber lighting.jpg|thumb|alt=The picture depicts Tesfaye performing, with the lights giving the image an orange environment. In the background, there is a drum set.|Tesfaye performing at [[Massey Hall]] in October 2013]]
On May 17, 2013, Tesfaye released the [[Kiss Land (song)|title track]] to his debut studio album, ''[[Kiss Land]]''<ref>{{Cite magazine |last=Nostro |first=Lauren |title=Listen: The Weeknd "Kiss Land" |magazine=[[Complex Networks|Complex]] |url=https://www.complex.com/music/2013/05/listen-the-weeknd-kiss-land |url-status=live |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20201124205738/https://www.complex.com/music/2013/05/listen-the-weeknd-kiss-land |archive-date=November 24, 2020 |date=May 16, 2013|access-date=March 11, 2024|mode=cs2}}</ref> and announced the album's release date of September 10.<ref>{{Cite news |last=Williott |first=Carl |date=July 22, 2013 |title=The Weeknd Unveils 'Kiss Land' Release Date & Cover Artwork |url=https://www.idolator.com/7472491/the-weeknd-kiss-land-release-date-cover-artwork |website=[[Idolator (website)|Idolator]] |access-date=May 18, 2021 |archive-date=October 29, 2023 |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20231029160534/https://www.idolator.com/7472491/the-weeknd-kiss-land-release-date-cover-artwork |url-status=dead|mode=cs2}}</ref> Upon its release, the album debuted at number two on the [[Billboard 200|''Billboard'' 200]] with 96,000 copies<ref>{{Cite magazine | first=Keith | last=Caulfield |date=December 1, 2012 |title=Keith Urban Edges The Weeknd for No. 1 Debut on Billboard 200 |url=https://www.billboard.com/articles/news/5695587/keith-urban-edges-the-weeknd-for-no-1-debut-on-billboard-200 |url-status=live |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20130919013240/http://www.billboard.com/articles/news/5695587/keith-urban-edges-the-weeknd-for-no-1-debut-on-billboard-200 |archive-date=September 19, 2013 |magazine=[[Billboard (magazine)|Billboard]]|mode=cs2}}</ref> and received generally positive reviews from music critics.<ref>{{Cite web |title=Kiss Land Reviews |url=https://www.metacritic.com/music/kiss-land/the-weeknd |url-status=live |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20210107223922/https://www.metacritic.com/music/kiss-land/the-weeknd |archive-date=January 7, 2021 |access-date=March 11, 2024|website=[[Metacritic]]|mode=cs2}}</ref> Tesfaye further promoted the album with a fall tour that occurred in North America and England in September to November.<ref>{{Cite news | url=https://pitchfork.com/news/51422-the-weeknd-announces-big-fall-tour-kiss-land-out-late-summer/ | title=The Weeknd Announces Big Fall Tour, Kiss Land Out Late Summer | first=Carrie | last=Battan | work=[[Pitchfork (website)|Pitchfork]] | date=July 8, 2013 | access-date=May 23, 2021 | archive-date=May 23, 2021 | 2013, Tesfaye was announced as the opening act for Drake's European leg of [[Would You Like a Tour?]], lasting between February and March 2014.<ref>{{cite web |date=October 29, 2013|title=Drake Announces European Tour Dates with The Weeknd |url=http://www.rap-up.com/2013/10/29/drake-announces-european-tour-dates-with-the-weeknd/ |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20140721004624/http://www.rap-up.com/2013/10/29/drake-announces-european-tour-dates-with-the-weeknd/ |archive-date=July 21, 2014 |access-date=July 22, 2014 |work=[[Rap-Up]] |url-status=dead}}</ref> Between November 6 and 13, he served as an opening act for [[Justin Timberlake]] during [[The 20/20 Experience World Tour]].<ref>{{Cite news |last=Payne |first=Chris |date=November 4, 2013 |title=The Weeknd To Open For Justin Timberlake On '20/20 Experience' Tour |magazine=[[Billboard (magazine)|Billboard]] |url=https://www.billboard.com/music/rb-hip-hop/the-weeknd-to-open-for-justin-timberlake-on-2020-experience-tour-5778176/ |url-status=live |archive-date=January 7, 2021|mode=cs2}}</ref> He also contributed two songs to the [[The Hunger Games: Catching Fire – Original Motion Picture Soundtrack|soundtrack]] for the 2013 film ''[[The Hunger Games: Catching Fire]]'', "Devil May Cry" and the soundtrack's second single, "[[Elastic Heart]]" with [[Sia]] and [[Diplo]].<ref>{{Cite magazine |last=Hakimian |first=Rob |date=November 19, 2013 |title=Album Review: Various Artists – The Hunger Games: Catching Fire Soundtrack |url=https://consequence.net/2013/11/album-review-various-artists-the-hunger-games-catching-fire-soundtrack/ |archive-date=December 11, 2018 |magazine=[[Consequence (publication)|Consequence]]|mode=cs2}}</ref>

In February 2014, Tesfaye released a remix of "[[Drunk in Love]]" from [[Beyoncé]]'s [[Beyoncé (album)|eponymous studio album]],<ref>{{cite magazine|last=Hooton|first=Christopher|url=https://www.independent.co.uk/arts-entertainment/music/news/listen-the-weeknd-enters-beyonce-drunk-in-love-remix-battle-with-kanye-west-and-diplo-9133498.html|title=Listen: The Weeknd enters Beyonce Drunk in Love remix battle with Kanye West and Diplo|magazine=[[The Independent]]|date=February 17, 2014|access-date=May 2, 2024|archive-date=May 2, 2024|archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20240502064118/https://www.independent.co.uk/arts-entertainment/music/news/listen-the-weeknd-enters-beyonce-drunk-in-love-remix-battle-with-kanye-west-and-diplo-9133498.html|url-status=live}}</ref> and [[Ty Dolla Sign]]'s "[[Or Nah (Ty Dolla Sign song)|Or Nah]]".<ref>{{cite web|last=Pelly|first=Jen|url=https://pitchfork.com/news/54068-listen-the-weeknd-remixes-ty-dolla-igns-or-nah/|title=Listen: The Weeknd remixes Ty Dolla Sign's "Or Nah"|website=Pitchfork|date=February 21, 2014|access-date=May 2, 2024|archive-date=May 2, 2024|archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20240502064121/https://pitchfork.com/news/54068-listen-the-weeknd-remixes-ty-dolla-igns-or-nah/|url-status=live}}</ref> He announced the [[King of the Fall Tour]] in June, a 4-city tour of North America between September and October and was supported by [[Schoolboy Q]] and [[Jhené Aiko]].<ref>{{Cite web |date=June 26, 2014 |title=The Weeknd touring w/ Schoolboy Q & Jhene Aiko, playing Barclays Center (dates); SBQ played Central Park |url=https://www.brooklynvegan.com/the-weeknd-tour-1/ |url-status=dead |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20140924044926/http://www.brooklynvegan.com/archives/2014/06/the_weeknd_tour_1.html |archive-date=September 24, 2014 |website=[[BrooklynVegan]]}}</ref> In promotion of the tour, he released the songs "[[King of the Fall]]" and "[[Often]]" in July of that year.<ref>{{cite magazine|last=Bowe|first=Miles|url=https://www.stereogum.com/1693315/the-weeknd-king-of-the-fall/news/|title=The Weeknd – "King Of The Fall"|magazine=Stereogum|date=July 21, 2014|access-date=May 2, 2024|archive-date=May 2, 2024|archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20240502064118/https://www.stereogum.com/1693315/the-weeknd-king-of-the-fall/news/|url-status=live}}</ref> On August 25, Tesfaye collaborated with [[Ariana Grande]] on the song "[[Love Me Harder]]" from Grande's second studio album ''[[My Everything (Ariana Grande album)|My Everything]]''. It was later released on September 30 as the fourth single from the album, and peaked at number seven on the [[Billboard Hot 100|''Billboard'' Hot 100]].<ref>{{Cite news |date=October 3, 2014 |title=Ariana Grande and The Weeknd Team Up In 'Love Me Harder' Video |work=[[Rap-Up]] |url=https://www.rap-up.com/2014/10/03/first-look-ariana-grande-the-weeknd-love-me-harder-video/ |url-status=dead |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20141005205143/http://www.rap-up.com/2014/10/03/first-look-ariana-grande-the-weeknd-love-me-harder-video/ |archive-date=October 5, 2014}}</ref> On December 23, Tesfaye released the song "[[Earned It]]" from the [[Fifty Shades of Grey (soundtrack)|soundtrack]] for ''[[Fifty Shades of Grey (film)|Fifty Shades of Grey]]'' (2015). The single, which peaked at number three on the ''Billboard'' Hot 100, earned Tesfaye his first and only [[Academy Awards|Academy Award]] nomination for [[Academy Award for Best Original Song|Best Original Song]].<ref>{{Cite news |last=Cox |first=Jamieson |date=February 28, 2016 |title=Oscars 2016: Watch The Weeknd reenact his sultry video for Earned It |work=[[The Verge]] |url=https://www.theverge.com/2016/2/28/11130062/the-weeknd-earned-it-performance-watch-academy-awards-2016 |access-date=May 18, 2021 |archive-date=April 18, 2023 |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20230418071407/https://www.theverge.com/2016/2/28/11130062/the-weeknd-earned-it-performance-watch-academy-awards-2016 |url-status=live }}</ref> The song won [[Grammy Award for Best R&B Performance|Best R&B Performance]] and was nominated for [[Grammy Award for Best R&B Song|Best R&B Song]] and [[Grammy Award for Best Song Written for Visual Media|Best Song Written for Visual Media]] at the [[58th Annual Grammy Awards]].<ref>{{Cite web|title=Grammys 2016: The Weeknd Wins Two Awards|url=https://people.com/awards/grammys-2016-the-weeknd-wins-two-awards/|access-date=September 28, 2021|website=PEOPLE.com|language=en|archive-date=September 29, 2021|archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20210929004222/https://people.com/awards/grammys-2016-the-weeknd-wins-two-awards/|url-status=live}}</ref>

=== 2015–2016: ''Beauty Behind the Madness'' ===
[[File:The Weeknd Bumbershoot 2015.jpg|thumb|upright|Tesfaye performing at [[Bumbershoot]], 2015|alt=The Weeknd, sporting a Basquiat-like hairstyle, holds a microphone while his other hand points up as he performs.]]
On May 27, 2015, Tesfaye released the second single from ''[[Beauty Behind the Madness]]'', "[[The Hills (song)|The Hills]]".<ref>{{Cite news |last=Lockett |first=Dee |title=The Weeknd Returns With 'The Hills,' Which Probably Isn't About Lauren Conrad |url=https://www.vulture.com/2015/05/weeknd-debuts-new-song-the-hills.html |url-status=live |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20210107223930/https://www.vulture.com/2015/05/weeknd-debuts-new-song-the-hills.html |archive-date=January 7, 2021 | date=May 27, 2015 |website=[[Vulture.com]] |ref=vulture-hills}}</ref> The single debuted at number twenty on the ''Billboard'' Hot 100, and peaked at number one, becoming Tesfaye's second number-one single, following "[[Can't Feel My Face]]", which had reached the number one position before it, and by four years, was [[RIAA certification|certified diamond]] by the RIAA, marking Tesfaye's first diamond-certified record.<ref>{{Cite news |last=McIntyre |first=Hugh |date=June 28, 2019 |title=The Weeknd's No. 1 Hit 'The Hills' Becomes His First Diamond-Certified Single |work=[[Forbes]] |url=https://www.forbes.com/sites/hughmcintyre/2019/06/28/the-weeknds-no-1-hit-the-hills-becomes-his-first-diamond-certified-single/ |url-access=limited |access-date=May 18, 2021 |archive-date=May 18, 2021 |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20210518195601/https://www.forbes.com/sites/hughmcintyre/2019/06/28/the-weeknds-no-1-hit-the-hills-becomes-his-first-diamond-certified-single/ |url-status=live }}</ref><ref>{{Cite news |last=Lamare |first=Carl |date=June 28, 2019 |title=The Weeknd's 'The Hills' Certified Diamond: Exclusive |magazine=[[Billboard (magazine)|Billboard]] |url=https://www.billboard.com/music/rb-hip-hop/the-weeknd-the-hills-diamond-8517989/ |url-status=live |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20210107223924/https://www.billboard.com/articles/columns/hip-hop/8517989/the-weeknd-the-hills-diamond |archive-date=January 7, 2021}}</ref> In June 2015, after winning the Centric Award at the [[BET Awards 2015|BET Awards]], Tesfaye performed "Earned It" with [[Alicia Keys]].<ref>{{Cite news |last=Beauchemin |first=Molly |title=Janelle Monáe Performs "Yoga", The Weeknd Performs "Earned It" with Alicia Keys at the BET Awards |url=https://pitchfork.com/news/60165-janelle-monae-performs-yoga-the-weeknd-performs-earned-it-with-alicia-keys-at-the-bet-awards/ |url-status=live |archive-date=January 7, 2021 | date=June 28, 2015 |website=[[Pitchfork (website)|Pitchfork]]}}</ref> On June 8, he released the song "[[Can't Feel My Face]]" as the album's third single. The track was previously leaked in May, but was released as a single following a performance by Tesfaye at the [[Apple Worldwide Developers Conference]].<ref name="RS1">{{Cite news |last=Kreps |first=Daniel |date=June 8, 2015 |title=Hear The Weeknd's Funky New Song 'Can't Feel My Face' |magazine=[[Rolling Stone]] |url=https://www.rollingstone.com/music/news/hear-the-weeknds-funky-new-song-cant-feel-my-face-20150608 |url-status=live |url-access=limited debuted at number twenty-four on the [[Billboard Hot 100|''Billboard'' Hot 100]], and peaked at number one, making it Tesfaye's third top 10 hit and his first number-one song in the United States.<ref>{{Cite magazine |last=Trust |first=Gary |title=Wiz Khalifa No. 1 on Hot 100 'Again,' Selena Gomez Debuts at No. 9 |date=July 1, 2015 |magazine=[[Billboard (magazine)|Billboard]]}}</ref><ref>{{Cite news |last=Trust |first=Gary |title=The Weeknd Tops Hot 100 With 'Can't Feel My Face,' One Direction Debuts at No. 3 |archive-date=January 7, 2021 |date=August 10, 2015| magazine=[[Billboard (magazine)|Billboard]]}}</ref> The song was nominated for [[Grammy Award for Record of the Year|Record of the Year]] and [[Grammy Award for Best Pop Solo Performance|Best Pop Solo Performance]] at the 58th Annual Grammy Awards.<ref>{{Cite magazine|title=Grammy Nominations 2016: See the Full List of Tesfaye occupied all three slots on ''Billboard's'' [[Hot R&B/Hip-Hop Songs]] chart simultaneously with the aforementioned singles, becoming the first artist in history to accomplish this.<ref>{{Cite news | url=https://www.billboard.com/pro/weeknd-entire-top-three-hot-rb-songs-chart/ | title=The Weeknd Is First Artist to Own Entire Top Three on Hot R&B Songs Chart | first=Amaya | last=Mendizabal | Weeknd headline FVDED IN THE PARK 2015 |work=[[Daily Hive]] |url=https://dailyhive.com/vancouver/fvded-in-the-park-2015-lineup-surrey |access-date=May 18, 2021 |archive-date=May 18, 2021 |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20210518195601/https://dailyhive.com/vancouver/fvded-in-the-park-2015-lineup-surrey |url-status=live }}</ref> On June 29, Tesfaye was featured on [[Meek Mill]]'s second studio album ''[[Dreams Worth More Than Money]]'' (2015), on the track "Pullin' Up".<ref>{{Cite news |title=Meek Mill Reveals 'Dreams Worth More Than Money' Features ''[[Beauty Behind the Madness]]'', Tesfaye's second studio album, was released on August 28, 2015, and debuted atop the ''Billboard'' 200, earning 412,000 [[album-equivalent unit]]s in its first week.<ref>{{Cite news |title=The Weeknd's 'Beauty Behind the Madness' Debuts at No. 1 on Billboard 200 Chart |url=https://www.billboard.com/pro/the-weeknd-beauty-behind-the-madness-debuts-at-no-1-billboard-200-charts/ |url-status=live |archive-date=March 23, 2018 |first=Keith | last=Caulfield | date=September 6, 2015 |magazine=[[Billboard (magazine)|Billboard]]}}</ref><ref>{{Cite news |title=The Weeknd spends third week at No. 1 on Billboard 200 |url=https://www.billboard.com/pro/the-weeknd-billboard-200-third-week-no-1/ |url-status=live |first=Keith | last=Caulfield | date=September 20, 2015 |magazine=[[Billboard (magazine)|Billboard]]}}</ref> It reached the top 10 in over ten countries and reached number one in Canada, Australia, Norway, and the United Kingdom.<ref>{{Cite web |title=ARIA Albums : The Weeknd Takes Top Spot On Australian Chart last=Ryan | date=September 5, 2015 | work=[[Noise11]]}}</ref><ref>{{Cite web |title=Top 20 Albums |url=https://musiccanada.com/charts/2015-09-21/ |url-status=live headlining various summer music festivals, including [[Lollapalooza]], the [[Hard (music festival)|Hard Summer Music Festival]], and the [[Bumbershoot]] Festival.<ref>{{Cite news |title=The Weeknd's new album is coming Aug. 28 |url=https://www.usatoday.com/story/life/music/2015/07/10/weeknd-beauty-behind-the-madness-august-28-release/29961285/ |url-status=live first=Brian | last=Mansfield | author-link=Brian Mansfield | date=July 10, 2015 |website=[[USA Today]]}}</ref> He announced [[The Madness Fall Tour]], his first large-scale tour across the United States, which began in November, and concluded in December.<ref name="pitchfork.com">{{cite web |author=Gordon, Jeremy |date=August 20, 2015 |title=The Weeknd Announces "The Madness" Fall Tour |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20210107223940/https://pitchfork.com/news/60875-the-weeknd-announces-the-madness-fall-tour/ |url-status=live }}</ref><ref>{{Cite news |title=The Weeknd for [[Grammy Award for Album of the Year|Album of the Year]] at the 58th Annual Grammy Awards.<ref>{{Cite magazine|title=See All the Winners of the 2016 On September 4, 2015, Tesfaye was featured on [[Travis Scott]]'s debut album ''[[Rodeo (Travis Scott album)|Rodeo]]'', on the track "[[Pray 4 Love (Travi$ Scott song)|Pray 4 Love]]".<ref>{{Cite news === 2016–2019: ''Starboy'' and ''My Dear Melancholy'', ===
[[File:The Weeknd (253662129).jpeg|thumb|Tesfaye performing at [[Lollapalooza Chile]] during the ''[[Starboy: Legend of the Fall Tour]]'' in 2017|300x300px|alt=The Weeknd looks slightly up in a pink-lit environment during a concert]]
In September 2016, Tesfaye announced that his third studio album, ''[[Starboy (album)|Starboy]]'', would be released on November 25, and included collaborations with now-disbanded French electronic music duo [[Daft Punk]].<ref>{{Cite news|last=Mench|first=Chris|date=August 23, 2016|title=The Weeknd Is Reportedly Working with Daft Punk|website=[[Complex Networks]]|url=https://www.complex.com/music/2016/08/the-weeknd-working-with-daft-punk|url-status=live|archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20160824143608/http://www.complex.com/music/2016/08/the-weeknd-working-with-daft-punk|archive-date=August 24, 2016}}</ref><ref>{{Cite web|last=Pearce|first=Sheldon|date=September 21, 2016|title=The Weeknd Announces New Album Starboy|website=[[Pitchfork (website)|Pitchfork]]|url=https://pitchfork.com/news/67755-the-weeknd-announces-new-album-starboy/|url-status=live|archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20210107223937/https://pitchfork.com/news/67755-the-weeknd-announces-new-album-starboy/|archive-date=January 7, 2021}}</ref> He released the album's [[Starboy (song)|title track]], which featured the duo on September 21.<ref>{{Cite news|date=September 22, 2016|title=Hear the Weeknd's new track with Daft Punk, Starboy|newspaper=[[The Guardian]]|url=https://www.theguardian.com/music/2016/sep/22/hear-the-weeknds-new-track-with-daft-punk-starboy|url-status=live|archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20210301172411/https://www.theguardian.com/music/2016/sep/22/hear-the-weeknds-new-track-with-daft-punk-starboy|archive-date=March 1, 2021|issn=0261-3077}}</ref> The song debuted at number 40 on the ''Billboard'' Hot 100, and peaked at number one, making it Tesfaye's third number-one single.<ref>{{Cite magazine|date=January 20, 2021|title=The Weeknd's 'Starboy,' Featuring Daft Punk, Hits No. 1 on Hot 100 |magazine=[[Billboard (magazine)|Billboard]]|url=https://www.billboard.com/pro/hot-100-the-weeknd-starboy-daft-punk-hits-number-one/|archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20210120110659/https://www.billboard.com/articles/columns/chart-beat/7633336/hot-100-the-weeknd-starboy-daft-punk-hits-number-one|url-status=live|archive-date=January 20, 2021|access-date=September 28, 2021}}</ref> As of March 2023, the song is certified Diamond by the RIAA.<ref name="RIAAStarboy">{{Cite web|date=February 1, 2021|title=Gold & Platinum|publisher=[[Recording Industry Association of America]]|url=https://www.riaa.com/gold-platinum/?tab_active=default-award&ar=The+Weeknd&ti=Starboy|archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20210201081033/https://www.riaa.com/gold-platinum/?tab_active=default-award&ar=The+Weeknd&ti=Starboy|url-status=dead|archive-date=February 1, 2021|access-date=September 28, 2021}}</ref> Their second collaboration, "[[I Feel It Coming]]" was released on November 24. The single peaked at number four on the ''Billboard'' Hot 100.<ref>{{Cite web|last=Zwilling|first=Eric|date=September 21, 2016|title=Listen to Daft Punk's new collaboration with The Weeknd, 'Starboy'|url=https://dancingastronaut.com/2016/09/daft-punk-the-weeknd-collaboration-listen/|url-status=live|archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20201107130837/https://dancingastronaut.com/2016/09/daft-punk-the-weeknd-collaboration-listen/|archive-date=November 7, 2020|website=[[Dancing Astronaut]]}}</ref> On October 1, Tesfaye made a second appearance on ''[[Saturday Night Live]]'' as the musical guest alongside actress [[Margot Robbie]]. During the show, he performed "Starboy" and "[[False Alarm (The Weeknd song)|False Alarm]]".<ref>{{Cite news|last=Peters|first=Mitchell|date=October 2, 2016|title=Watch The Weeknd Perform 'Starboy' and 'False Alarm' on 'SNL' Season Premiere|magazine=[[Billboard (magazine)|Billboard]]|url=https://www.billboard.com/music/rb-hip-hop/the-weeknd-perform-starboy-false-alarm-on-snl-saturday-night-live-7526619/|access-date=May 25, 2021|archive-date=May 25, 2021|archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20210525140031/https://www.billboard.com/articles/columns/hip-hop/7526619/the-weeknd-perform-starboy-false-alarm-on-snl-saturday-night-live|url-status=live}}</ref> On November 23, he released the short film ''M A N I A''. Directed by Grant Singer, it featured excerpts from the album, including snippets from "[[All I Know (The Weeknd song)|All I Know]]" featuring [[Future (rapper)|Future]], "[[Sidewalks (The Weeknd song)|Sidewalks]]" featuring [[Kendrick Lamar]], "[[Secrets (The Weeknd song)|Secrets]]", and "[[Die for You (The Weeknd song)|Die for You]]".<ref>{{Cite news|date=November 23, 2016|title=Watch The Weeknd's New Short Film Mania|website=[[Pitchfork (website)|Pitchfork]]|url=https://pitchfork.com/news/69997-watch-the-weeknds-new-short-film-mania/|url-status=live|archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20161124093812/http://pitchfork.com/news/69997-watch-the-weeknds-new-short-film-mania/|archive-date=November 24, 2016}}</ref> Upon release, the album debuted at number one on the U.S. ''Billboard'' 200 with 348,000 units, making it Tesfaye's second consecutive number-one album.<ref>{{Cite magazine|date=January 30, 2021|title=The Weeknd's 'Starboy' Bows at No. 1 on Billboard 200 With Third-Biggest Debut of 2016 |magazine=[[Billboard (magazine)|Billboard]]|url=https://www.billboard.com/pro/the-weeknd-starboy-billboard-200-no-1/|archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20210130022651/https://www.billboard.com/articles/columns/chart-beat/7597375/the-weeknd-starboy-billboard-200-no-1|url-status=live|archive-date=January 30, 2021|access-date=September 29, 2021}}</ref> As of January 2019, the album is certified [[RIAA certification|triple platinum]] by the RIAA.<ref name="RIAAStarboy" /> The album won Best Urban Contemporary Album at the [[60th Annual Grammy Awards]], making it Tesfaye's second win in the category.<ref>{{Cite news|date=January 28, 2018|title=NewsAlert: Starboy Wins Best Urban Contemporary Album for the Weeknd|work=[[National Post]]|agency=[[The Canadian Press]]|url=https://nationalpost.com/pmn/entertainment-pmn/newsalert-starboy-wins-best-urban-contemporary-album-for-the-weeknd|access-date=April 16, 2019|archive-date=January 7, 2021|archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20210107223930/https://nationalpost.com/pmn/entertainment-pmn/newsalert-starboy-wins-best-urban-contemporary-album-for-the-weeknd|url-status=dead}}</ref>

=== 2019–2021: ''After Hours'' and Super Bowl LV halftime show ===
On November 24, 2019, Tesfaye teased his single "[[Blinding Lights]]" through a [[Mercedes-Benz]] commercial.<ref>{{Cite news |date=November 24, 2019 |title=The Weeknd to Release New Single 'Blinding Lights' on Black Friday |work=[[Rap-Up]] |url=https://www.revolt.tv/article/the-weeknd-new-single-blinding-lights |url-status=live |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20191128132032/https://www.rap-up.com/2019/11/24/the-weeknd-new-single-blinding-lights/ |archive-date=November 28, 2019}}</ref> On November 27, he released "[[Heartless (The Weeknd song)|Heartless]]" as the lead single from his fourth studio album. The song debuted at number thirty-two on the ''Billboard'' Hot 100 and peaked at number one, making it Tesfaye's fourth number-one single.<ref>{{Cite news |title=Five Burning Questions: Billboard Staffers Discuss The Weeknd's Top 40 Debut For 'Heartless' |url=https://www.billboard.com/articles/business/chart-beat/8545288/the-weeknd-heartless-five-burning-questions |url-status=live |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20200319225809/https://www.billboard.com/articles/business/chart-beat/8545288/the-weeknd-heartless-five-burning-questions |archive-date=March 19, 2020 | date=December 3, 2019 |magazine=[[Billboard (magazine)|Billboard]]}}</ref><ref>{{Cite news |last=Trust |first=Gary |title=The Weeknd's 'Heartless' Hits No. 1 on Hot 100, Mariah Carey's 'All I Want for Christmas Is You' Returns to No. 3 High |url=https://www.billboard.com/articles/business/chart-beat/8545778/the-weeknd-heartless-no-1-hot-100 |url-status=live |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20200116053415/https://www.billboard.com/articles/business/chart-beat/8545778/the-weeknd-heartless-no-1-hot-100 |archive-date=January 16, 2020 |date=December 9, 2019 |magazine=[[Billboard (magazine)|Billboard]]}}</ref> "Blinding Lights" was released two days after the release of "Heartless" on November 29. The single debuted at number eleven on the ''Billboard'' Hot 100 and peaked at number one, making it Tesfaye's fifth number-one single.<ref>{{Cite magazine |last=Trust |first=Gary |title=The Weeknd's 'Blinding Lights' Hits No. 1 on Billboard Hot 100, Doja Cat's 'Say So' Enters Top 10 |url=https://www.billboard.com/articles/business/chart-beat/9346375/weeknd-blinding-lights-number-one-hot-100 |url-status=live |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20200331195920/https://www.billboard.com/articles/business/chart-beat/9346375/weeknd-blinding-lights-number-one-hot-100 |archive-date=March 31, 2020 |date=March 30, 2020 |magazine=[[Billboard (magazine)|Billboard]]}}</ref> "Blinding Lights" would then go on to become the first song in the chart's history to hold a spot in the top ten for an entire year.<ref>{{Cite magazine|last=Trust|first=Gary|date=March 9, 2021|title=The Weeknd Talks Unprecedented Year in Hot 100's Top 10 With 'Blinding Lights': 'This Is Truly a Result of the Fans'|url=https://www.billboard.com/pro/the-weeknd-blinding-lights-chart-record-reaction/|access-date=November 26, 2021|magazine=Billboard|language=en-US|archive-date=November 26, 2021|archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20211126174840/https://www.billboard.com/pro/the-weeknd-blinding-lights-chart-record-reaction/|url-status=live}}</ref> It also became the [[List of Billboard Hot 100 chart achievements and milestones|longest charting song by a solo artist on the Hot 100]] at 90 weeks, ending the week of September 11, 2021.<ref>{{Cite magazine|last=Trust|first=Gary|date=August 16, 2021|title=The Weeknd's 'Blinding Lights' Spends Record-Breaking 88th Week on Billboard Hot 100|url=https://www.billboard.com/pro/the-weeknd-blinding-lights-hot-100-record-88-weeks/|access-date=November 26, 2021|magazine=Billboard|language=en-US|archive-date=November 26, 2021|archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20211126174838/https://www.billboard.com/pro/the-weeknd-blinding-lights-hot-100-record-88-weeks/|url-status=live}}</ref><ref>{{Cite web|title=The Weeknd's "Blinding Lights" Is Now the Longest-Charting Billboard Hot 100 Song of All Time|url=https://www.complex.com/music/the-weeknd-blinding-lights-longest-charting-song-billboard-hot-100|access-date=September 29, 2021|website=Complex|language=en|archive-date=September 29, 2021|archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20210929152346/https://www.complex.com/music/the-weeknd-blinding-lights-longest-charting-song-billboard-hot-100|url-status=live}}</ref> On November 23, 2021, "Blinding Lights" was ranked as the [[List of Billboard Hot 100 chart achievements and milestones#Top 10 songs of all time (19588–2021)|best-performing song in the ''Billboard'' Hot 100's history]], surpassing "[[The Twist (song)|The Twist]]" by [[Chubby Checker]].<ref>{{Cite magazine|last=Mamo|first=Heran|date=November 23, 2021|title=The Greatest Hit: The New No. 1 Song of All Time|url=https://www.billboard.com/music/features/the-weeknd-blinding-lights-billboard-cover-story-2021-interview-1235001282/|access-date=November 26, 2021|magazine=Billboard|language=en-US|archive-date=December 2, On January 1, 2023, it became the most streamed song on Spotify with 3.3 billion streams.<ref>{{Cite web|title=The Weeknd's 'Blinding Lights' becomes the most-streamed song on Spotify |date=January 4, 2023 |url=https://www.cbc.ca/music/the-weeknd-s-blinding-lights-becomes-the-most-streamed-song-on-spotify-1.6704361 |language=en |access-date=January 31, 2023 |archive-date=January 31, 2023 |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20230131185916/https://www.cbc.ca/music/the-weeknd-s-blinding-lights-becomes-the-most-streamed-song-on-spotify-1.6704361 |url-status=live }}</ref>

On February 19, 2020, Tesfaye revealed that his fourth studio album would be titled ''[[After Hours (The Weeknd album)|After Hours]]'', and would be released on March 20. He also released the album's [[After Hours (The Weeknd song)|title track]] as a promotional single.<ref>{{Cite news |last=Brandle |first=Lars |date=February 19, 2020 |title=The Weeknd Sets 'After Hours' Release Date, Shares Dark Title Track: Listen |magazine=[[Billboard (magazine)|Billboard]] |url=https://www.billboard.com/music/rb-hip-hop/the-weeknd-after-hours-release-date-title-track-release-8551359/ |url-status=live |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20200313135747/https://www.billboard.com/articles/columns/hip-hop/8551359/the-weeknd-after-hours-release-date-title-track-release |archive-date=March 13, 2020}}</ref> On March 7, he made his third appearance as a musical guest on ''Saturday Night Live'', alongside actor [[Daniel Craig]]. On the show, he starred in the skit "[[On the Couch (The Weeknd, Kenan Thompson, and Chris Redd song)|On The Couch]]" with actors [[Kenan Thompson]] and [[Chris Redd]],<ref>{{Cite web |date=March 9, 2020 |title=SNL's 'On the Couch' Song Is Brutal and Hilariously Relatable |url=https://www.fatherly.com/news/snls-on-the-couch-song-is-brutal-and-hilariously-relatable/ |url-status=live |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20210709185806/https://www.fatherly.com/news/snls-on-the-couch-song-is-brutal-and-hilariously-relatable/ |archive-date=July 9, 2021 |access-date=June 30, 2021 |website=Fatherly |language=en-US}}</ref> performed "Blinding Lights", and debuted the track "[[Scared to Live]]".<ref>{{Cite web |last=Young |first=Alex |date=March 8, 2020 |title=The Weeknd Debuts New Song, Appears in Sketch on SNL |url=https://consequence.net/2020/03/the-weeknd-snl-video/ |url-status=live |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20200320035057/https://consequenceofsound.net/2020/03/the-weeknd-snl-video/ |archive-date=March 20, 2020 |website=[[Consequence (publication)|Consequence]]}}</ref> Tesfaye released the album's third single "[[In Your Eyes (The Weeknd song)|In Your Eyes]]" on March 24. The track peaked at number sixteen on the ''Billboard'' Hot 100.<ref>{{Cite web|date=November 27, 2020|title=The Weeknd Loses His Head in Gory New "In Your Eyes" Video |website=[[Pitchfork 27, 2020|access-date=September 29, 2021}}</ref> Upon release,<ref>{{Cite news |last=Strauss |first=Matthew |title=Listen to the Weeknd's New Album After Hours |url=https://pitchfork.com/news/listen-to-the-weeknds-new-album-after-hours/ |url-status=live |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20200320145830/https://pitchfork.com/news/listen-to-the-weeknds-new-album-after-hours/ |archive-date=March 20, 2020 |date=March 20, 2020 |website=[[Pitchfork (website)|Pitchfork]]}}</ref> ''After Hours'' debuted atop the ''Billboard'' 200, earning 444,000 units, marking Tesfaye's fourth consecutive number-one album.<ref>{{Cite news |last=Caulfield |first=Keith |title=The Weeknd's 'After Hours' Debuts at No. 1 on Billboard 200 Chart With Biggest Week of 2020 |url=https://www.billboard.com/articles/business/chart-beat/9345850/the-weeknd-after-hours-billboard-200-debut-2020-biggest-week |url-status=live Weeknd Captures #1 Album and #1 on Billboard Hot 100 With After Hours and Blinding On May 24, Tesfaye performed "Save Your Tears" at the [[2021 Billboard Music Awards|''Billboard'' Music Awards]]. He was nominated for a record sixteen awards, and won ten, including [[Billboard Music Award for Top Artist|Top Artist]] and [[Billboard Music Award for Top Hot 100 Song|Top Hot 100 Song]]. When accepting his awards, Tesfaye continued to tease new music by saying "the ''After Hours'' are done, and ''The Dawn'' is coming".<ref name="Cryptic">{{cite web |last=Singh |first=Ashish |date=May 28, 2021 |title=Is The Weeknd dropping an album? === 2021–2023: ''Dawn FM'' ===
[[File:The Weeknd (cropped).jpg|thumb|Tesfaye in July 2023 during his [[After Hours til Dawn Tour]] in [[Paris]].|alt=An extreme wide shot of the Weeknd performing with a durag in white-colored fashion as he sits down and performs on the staircase of the concert's setup.]]
On August 2, 2021, Tesfaye released a snippet of new music on social media.<ref>{{cite magazine|url=https://www.billboard.com/articles/news/9608939/the-weeknd-the-dawn-is-coming-new-music|title=The On March 20, 2022, Tesfaye played in an episode of the cartoon ''[[The Simpsons]]''.<ref>{{Cite web |date=March 21, 2022 |title=Watch The Weeknd appear in new episode of 'The Simpsons' |url=https://www.nme.com/news/music/watch-the-weeknd-appear-in-new-episode-of-the-simpsons-3186557 |access-date=April 22, 2023 |work=NME |archive-date=October 29, 2023 |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20231029161517/https://www.nme.com/news/music/watch-the-weeknd-appear-in-new-episode-of-the-simpsons-3186557 |url-status=live }}</ref> On April 18, he headlined the Coachella Valley Music and Arts Festival for the second time, performing alongside Swedish House Mafia.<ref>{{Cite web |last=Aswad |first=Jem |date=April 18, 2022 |title=The Weeknd Closes Out Coachella With Hit-Filled, Tag-Team Set With Swedish House Mafia |url=https://variety.com/2022/music/news/the-weeknd-coachella-swedish-house-mafia-1235234529/ |access-date=April 20, 2022 |website=Variety |language=en-US |archive-date=October 29, 2023 |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20231029161519/https://variety.com/2022/music/news/the-weeknd-coachella-swedish-house-mafia-1235234529/ |url-status=live }}</ref><ref>{{Cite web |date=April 18, 2022 |title=Coachella 2022: The Weeknd covers Kanye, Drake as part of hits-filled headlining set |url=https://consequence.net/2022/04/coachella-2022-the-weeknd-kanye-drake-setlist/ |access-date=April 20, 2022 |website=Consequence |language=en-US |archive-date=April 20, 2022 |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20220420032453/https://consequence.net/2022/04/coachella-2022-the-weeknd-kanye-drake-setlist/ |url-status=live }}</ref> On July 8, his record of most number-one songs on the ''[[Billboard (magazine)|Billboard]]'' [[Hot R&B Songs]] chart was surpassed by fellow singer and would-be rival [[Chris Brown]]; Weeknd had 71 while Brown broke his record by 8 more.<ref>{{Cite magazine |last=Anderson |first=Trevor |date=July 8, 2022 |title=Chris Brown Breaks The Weeknd's Record for Most Hot R&B Songs Hits |url=https://www.billboard.com/pro/chris-brown-breaks-the-weeknd-record-hot-rb-songs-chart-hits/ |access-date=March 28, 2025 |magazine=Billboard |language=en-US |archive-date=June 13, 2025 |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20250613051300/https://www.billboard.com/pro/chris-brown-breaks-the-weeknd-record-hot-rb-songs-chart-hits/ |url-status=live }}</ref> On July 26, Tesfaye announced that he would host a [[Universal's Halloween Horror Nights#Haunted houses|haunted house]] at [[Universal Studios Florida]] and [[Universal Studios Hollywood|Hollywood]], as part of [[Universal's Halloween Horror Nights]] hosted every Halloween.<ref>{{Cite magazine |last=Mier |first=Tomás |date=July 26, 2022 |title=The Weeknd Brings 'After Hours' to Universal Studios' Halloween Horror Nights |url=https://www.rollingstone.com/music/music-news/the-weeknd-after-hours-nightmare-halloween-horror-nights-1388229/ |access-date=August 26, 2022 |magazine=Rolling Stone |language=en-US |archive-date=August 26, 2022 |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20220826152429/https://www.rollingstone.com/music/music-news/the-weeknd-after-hours-nightmare-halloween-horror-nights-1388229/ |url-status=live }}</ref> Tesfaye appeared on the song "[[Creepin' (Metro Boomin, the Weeknd and 21 Savage song)|Creepin']]" from Metro Boomin's album, ''[[Heroes & Villains]]'', on December 2.<ref>{{cite magazine|last=Brodsky|first=Rachel|url=https://www.stereogum.com/2207303/stream-metro-boomins-new-album-heroes-villains-feat-the-weeknd-travis-scott-young-thug-more/music/|title=Stream Metro Boomin's New Album Heroes & Villains Feat. The Weeknd, Travis Scott, Young Thug, & More|magazine=Stereogum|date=December 2, 2022|access-date=April 6, 2024|archive-date=January 14, 2024|archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20240114205921/https://www.stereogum.com/2207303/stream-metro-boomins-new-album-heroes-villains-feat-the-weeknd-travis-scott-young-thug-more/music/|url-status=live}}</ref> His song "[[Nothing Is Lost (You Give Me Strength)]]", made for the film ''[[Avatar: The Way of Water (soundtrack)|Avatar: The Way of Water]]''{{'}}s official soundtrack, was released on December 16.<ref>{{cite web |date=December 16, 2022 |title=The Weeknd Releases New Song "Nothing Is Lost (You Give Me Strength)" from "Avatar: The Way of Water" Soundtrack |url=https://www.pmstudio.com/music/20221216-18567 |access-date=December 16, 2022 |website=PM Studio |archive-date=December 17, 2022 |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20221217001641/https://www.pmstudio.com/music/20221216-18567 |url-status=live }}</ref>

On February 24, 2023, following months-long renewed interest in and virality of Tesfaye's 2016 song "Die for You", which began charting in 2022 and reached a new peak of 6 on the ''Billboard'' Hot 100 6 years after its release, [[Die for You (The Weeknd song)|a remix]] of the song featuring Ariana Grande was released. The remix marked their fourth collaboration.<ref>{{Cite web |last=Strauss |first=Matthew |date=February 24, 2023 |title=The Weeknd Enlists Ariana Grande for New "Die for You (Remix)" |url=https://pitchfork.com/news/the-weeknd-enlists-ariana-grande-for-new-die-for-you-remix-listen/ |access-date=February 27, 2023 |website=Pitchfork |language=en-US |archive-date=August 19, 2023 |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20230819222159/https://pitchfork.com/news/the-weeknd-enlists-ariana-grande-for-new-die-for-you-remix-listen/ |url-status=live }}</ref><ref>{{Cite magazine |last=Trust |first=Gary |date=February 6, 2023 |title=Miley Cyrus' 'Flowers' Tops Billboard Hot 100 for Third Week, Lil Uzi Vert's 'Just Wanna Rock' Hits Top 10 |url=https://www.billboard.com/music/chart-beat/miley-cyrus-flowers-lil-uzi-vert-just-wanna-rock-hot-100-top-10-1235213867/ |access-date=February 27, 2023 |magazine=Billboard |language=en-US |archive-date=February 7, 2023 |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20230207064022/https://www.billboard.com/music/chart-beat/miley-cyrus-flowers-lil-uzi-vert-just-wanna-rock-hot-100-top-10-1235213867/ |url-status=live }}</ref> In the ''Billboard'' Hot 100 issue dated March 11, the remix reached the top of the chart, becoming both artists' seventh number one hit.<ref>{{Cite magazine |last=Trust |first=Gary |date=March 6, 2023 |title=The Weeknd & Ariana Grande's 'Die for You' Leaps to No. 1 on Billboard Hot 100 |url=https://www.billboard.com/music/chart-beat/the-weeknd-ariana-grande-die-for-you-number-one-billboard-hot-100-1235280422/ |access-date=March 7, 2023 |magazine=Billboard |language=en-US |archive-date=March 6, 2023 |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20230306180602/https://www.billboard.com/music/chart-beat/the-weeknd-ariana-grande-die-for-you-number-one-billboard-hot-100-1235280422/ |url-status=live }}</ref> On February 27, in the wake of the remix's success, Tesfaye became the first artist to surpass 100 million monthly listeners on Spotify.<ref name=Rowley/> On March 3, Tesfaye released his first live album, titled ''[[Live at SoFi Stadium]].''<ref>{{cite magazine|url=https://www.billboard.com/music/rb-hip-hop/the-weeknd-first-live-album-live-at-sofi-stadium-1235279121/|title=The Weeknd Drops First Live Album, 'Live at SoFi Stadium'|first=Gil|last=Kaufman|magazine=Billboard|date=March 3, 2023|access-date=March 3, 2023|archive-date=March 10, 2023|archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20230310143734/https://www.billboard.com/music/rb-hip-hop/the-weeknd-first-live-album-live-at-sofi-stadium-1235279121/|url-status=live}}</ref> It featured recordings from his HBO concert film of the same name, showcasing the last concert of the North American leg of his [[After Hours til Dawn Tour]] at [[SoFi Stadium]].<ref>{{cite web |date=March 1, 2023 |title=The Weeknd's 'Live at SoFi Stadium' To Be Released As Live Album |url=https://www.udiscovermusic.com/news/the-weeknd-live-at-sofi-stadium-live-album/ |access-date=March 2, 2023 |publisher=[[Universal Music Group]] }}</ref> He subsequently featured on four songs—"Artificial Intelligence", "Defame Moi", "More Coke!!", and "Emotionless"—from [[Mike Dean (record producer)|Mike Dean]]'s album ''[[4:23]]'', released on April 29.<ref name="Complex">{{cite web |title=Stream Mike Dean's '4:23' Album Featuring 4 New Songs With The Weeknd |url=https://www.complex.com/music/mike-dean-423-the-weeknd |access-date=June 6, 2023 |website=[[Complex Networks|Complex]] }}</ref> On May 8, Tesfaye stated that he was intending to retire the moniker of "the Weeknd" in favor of performing under his birth name, or adopting a new pseudonym altogether. He explained that his upcoming album would most likely serve as his "final hurrah" under the name.<ref>{{cite news |url=https://www.thedailybeast.com/the-weeknd-doesnt-want-to-be-the-weeknd-anymore |title=The Weeknd Doesn't Want to Be The Weeknd Anymore |last=Holmes |first=Helen |website=The Daily Beast |date=May 8, 2023 |publisher=The Daily Beast Company LLC |access-date=May 8, 2023 |archive-date=May 8, 2023 |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20230508200344/https://www.thedailybeast.com/the-weeknd-doesnt-want-to-be-the-weeknd-anymore |url-status=live }}</ref> He later withdrew his decision, stating on September 18, 2026, that he was "not stepping away from anything" and that "I'm going to keep doing this for as long as I possibly can. The Weeknd isn't going anywhere."<ref>{{Cite web |last=Kaufman |first=Gil |date=2026-09-18 |title=After Threatening to ‘Retire’ The Weeknd Persona, Abel Tesfaye Hits Reverse and Says He’s ‘Not Stepping Away From Anything’ |url=https://www.billboard.com/music/rb-hip-hop/the-weeknd-not-stepping-away-from-anything-reverse-retirement-1236343278/ |access-date=2026-11-15 |website=Billboard |language=en-US}}</ref>

=== 2023–2024: ''The Idol'' ===
[[File:The Weeknd Cannes 2023.png|thumb|upright=0.7|Tesfaye at the [[2023 Cannes Film Festival]]|alt=The Weeknd, in a tuxedo, slightly looks away from the camera.]]
Tesfaye co-created the [[HBO]] drama series ''[[The Idol (TV series)|The Idol]]'' with [[Sam Levinson]], and stars in the show alongside [[Lily-Rose Depp]].<ref>{{Cite web |last=White |first=Peter |date=June 29, 2021 |title=The Weeknd To Star In & Write Pop Singer Cult Drama Series 'The Idol' With Sam Levinson In The Works At HBO |url=https://deadline.com/2021/06/the-weeknd-pop-star-cult-drama-the-idol-sam-levinson-hbo-1234783685/ |url-status=live |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20210630001750/https://deadline.com/2021/06/the-weeknd-pop-star-cult-drama-the-idol-sam-levinson-hbo-1234783685/ |archive-date=June 30, 2021 |access-date=November 23, 2021 |website=Deadline Hollywood}}</ref> The series premiered at the [[2023 Cannes Film Festival]] out of competition,<ref>{{Cite web |last1=Blyth |first1=Antonia |last2=Hipes |first2=Patrick |date=May 22, 2023 |title='The Idol' Gets Five-Minute Standing Ovation After Cannes Premiere; Sam Levinson Gets Emotional In Speech |url=https://deadline.com/2023/05/the-idol-lily-cannes-standing-ovation-sam-levinson-lily-rose-depp-the-weeknd-1235375399/ |url-status=live |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20230523000809/https://deadline.com/2023/05/the-idol-lily-cannes-standing-ovation-sam-levinson-lily-rose-depp-the-weeknd-1235375399/ |archive-date=May 23, 2023 |access-date=May 22, 2023 |website=Deadline Hollywood}}</ref> where it received significant controversy for its graphic depiction of onscreen nudity and sexual content. The series debuted to widespread negative reviews from critics. ''[[The Hollywood Reporter]]'' stated that the series confirms the allegations that "Instead of subtly skewering the misogynistic and predatory nature of the business, ''The Idol'' became a forbidden love story — the stuff of a toxic man's fantasy", and called it "regressive rather than transgressive".<ref>{{cite web|url= https://www.hollywoodreporter.com/tv/tv-reviews/the-idol-review-lily-rose-depp-and-the-weeknd-in-an-hbo-series-thats-more-regressive-than-transgressive-1235497665/|title= 'The Idol' Review: Lily-Rose Depp and The Weeknd in an HBO Series That's More Regressive Than Transgressive|website= [[The Hollywood Reporter]]|date= May 23, 2023|access-date= June 19, 2023|archive-date= May 23, 2023|archive-url= https://web.archive.org/web/20230523014216/https://www.hollywoodreporter.com/tv/tv-reviews/the-idol-review-lily-rose-depp-and-the-weeknd-in-an-hbo-series-thats-more-regressive-than-transgressive-1235497665/|url-status=live}}</ref> He received negative reviews for his acting, with critic Robert Daniels of ''The Playlist'' writing, "Tesfaye is also a terrible actor. He lacks the comfortability, the gravitas, charisma, and charm to increase the viability of Jocelyn being attracted to him. In most scenes, Tesfaye either hides under the cover of dim lighting, obtrusive coverage, or re-recorded dialogue dubbed into several scenes."<ref>{{cite web|url= https://theplaylist.net/the-idol-review-sam-levinsons-crude-provocation-with-the-weeknd-lily-rose-depp-is-gross-sexist-cannes-20230523/|title= 'The Idol' Review: Sam Levinson's Crude Provocation With The Weeknd & Lily-Rose Depp Is Gross & Sexist [Cannes]|website= The Playlist|date= May 23, 2023|access-date= June 19, 2023|archive-date= June 19, 2023|archive-url= https://web.archive.org/web/20230619064317/https://theplaylist.net/the-idol-review-sam-levinsons-crude-provocation-with-the-weeknd-lily-rose-depp-is-gross-sexist-cannes-20230523/|url-status=live}}</ref> On August 28, 2023, HBO announced it had cancelled ''The Idol'' after one season.<ref>{{Cite web |last=Campione |first=Katie |date=August 28, 2023 |title='The Idol' Canceled At HBO After One Season |url=https://deadline.com/2023/08/the-idol-canceled-at-hbo-after-one-season-1235429399/ |access-date=August 29, 2023 |website=Deadline |language=en-US |archive-date=August 29, 2023 |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20230829042346/https://deadline.com/2023/08/the-idol-canceled-at-hbo-after-one-season-1235429399/ |url-status=live }}</ref>

On June 8, 2023, Tesfaye announced a series of [[Extended Play|EP]]s featuring [[Music from The Idol|music from ''The Idol'']].<ref name="Episode 1">{{Cite magazine |last=Spanos |first=Brittany |date=June 9, 2023 |title=The Weeknd and Lily-Rose Depp Roll Out New Music From HBO's 'The Idol' |url=https://www.rollingstone.com/music/music-news/the-idol-world-class-sinner-im-a-freak-the-lure-songs-1234766947/ |url-status=live |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20230630083630/https://www.rollingstone.com/music/music-news/the-idol-world-class-sinner-im-a-freak-the-lure-songs-1234766947/ |archive-date=June 30, 2023 |access-date=June 9, 2023 |magazine=Billboard |language=en-US}}</ref> Originally intended as a [[soundtrack album]], each EP was released following the premiere of each episode of the show, which featured collaborations with [[Future (rapper)|Future]], [[Playboi Carti]], [[Madonna]], [[Lil Baby]], [[Lily-Rose Depp]], and [[Jennie (singer)|Jennie]] from the South Korean girl group [[Blackpink]].<ref>{{cite web |last1=Galindo |first1=Thomas |title=The Weeknd Announces Soundtrack for Upcoming HBO Series 'The Idol' |url=https://americansongwriter.com/the-weeknd-announces-soundtrack-for-upcoming-hbo-series-the-idol/ |website=American Songwriter |date=April 12, 2023 |access-date=June 12, 2023 |archive-date=May 18, 2023 |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20230518045900/https://americansongwriter.com/the-weeknd-announces-soundtrack-for-upcoming-hbo-series-the-idol/ |url-status=live }}</ref> These EPs yielded the hit singles "[[Popular (The Weeknd, Playboi Carti and Madonna song)|Popular]]" and "[[One of the Girls]]". Later in June he received an invitation from [[Academy of Motion Picture Arts and Sciences|the Academy]] to become a member.<ref>{{Cite web |last=Pedersen |first=Erik |date=June 28, 2023 |title=Movie Academy Invites Nearly 400 New Members Including Taylor Swift, Ke Huy Quan, The Daniels & Austin Butler |url=https://deadline.com/2023/06/oscars-academy-membership-invites-2023-list-1235426384/ |access-date=June 28, 2023 |website=Deadline |archive-date=September 13, 2023 |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20230913115533/https://deadline.com/2023/06/oscars-academy-membership-invites-2023-list-1235426384/ |url-status=live }}</ref> On July 21, Tesfaye appeared on Travis Scott's lead single "[[K-pop (Travis Scott, Bad Bunny and the Weeknd song)|K-pop]]" from his fourth studio album ''[[Utopia (Travis Scott album)|Utopia]]'',<ref>{{Cite magazine |last=Madarang |first=Charisma |date=July 21, 2023 |title=Travis Scott Releases 'K-POP,' Recruits The Weeknd and Bad Bunny |url=https://www.rollingstone.com/music/music-news/travis-scott-k-pop-the-weeknd-bad-bunny-song-1234792160/ |access-date=November 26, 2023 |magazine=Rolling Stone |language=en-US |url-status=live }}</ref> and later appeared on another track on Scott's album, "[[Circus Maximus (song)|Circus Maximus]]".<ref name="auto2">{{Cite web |last=Pavlakos |first=Louis |title=The Weeknd Performs "Circus Maximus" For The First Time In Mexico |url=https://www.complex.com/music/a/louispavlakos/the-weeknd-performs-circus-maximus-for-the-first-time-in |access-date=November 24, 2023 |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20231124051820/https://www.complex.com/music/a/louispavlakos/the-weeknd-performs-circus-maximus-for-the-first-time-in |url-status=live }}</ref> Tesfaye appeared on [[Diddy (rapper)|Diddy]]'s single "[[Another One of Me]]" on September 15, which he called his "final feature",<ref>{{Cite magazine |last=Blistein |first=Jon |date=September 15, 2023 |title=Diddy Busts Out 'In the Air Tonight' Sample for the Weeknd's Final Feature on 'Another One of Me' despite releasing multiple collaborations with [[Metro Boomin]] and [[Future (rapper)|Future]] in 2024.<ref name=":3" />

On December 2, 2023, ''[[Fortnite]]'' announced that Tesfaye would be featured as the headlining artist for its ''[[Fortnite Festival]]'' gamemode and outfits of him would become available to play with on December 9. His outfits have three different variants of himself; the red suit seen throughout the promotional material for ''After Hours'', the outfit from his 2022 Coachella performance, and two of the costumes that he was seen wearing during the After Hours til Dawn Tour.<ref>{{Cite web |date=December 9, 2023 |title=Fortnite Festival Season 1: Opening Night Features The Weeknd!
