=== 2024–present: ''Hurry Up Tomorrow'' ===
On July 17, 2024, Tesfaye announced a one-night show in São Paulo, Brazil, set to take place on September 7, 2024.<ref>{{cite web|last=Bloom|first=Madison|url=https://pitchfork.com/news/the-weeknd-announces-one-night-only-stadium-concert-in-sao-paulo/|title=The Weeknd Announces One-Night Only Stadium Concert in São Paulo|website=Pitchfork|date=July 17, 2024|access-date=July 17, 2024|archive-date=March 3, 2025|archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20250303214848/https://pitchfork.com/news/the-weeknd-announces-one-night-only-stadium-concert-in-sao-paulo/|url-status=live}}</ref> He announced the title of his upcoming sixth album, ''[[Hurry Up Tomorrow]]'', three days prior to the concert,<ref>{{Cite x |number=1831324908510470596 |user=theweeknd |title=ALBUM TITLE |first=Abel |last=Tesfaye |date=September 4, 2024 |access-date=September 4, 2024 |url-status=live}}</ref><ref>{{Cite web |last=Aswad |first=Jem |date=September 4, 2024 |title=The Weeknd Reveals Title, New Details About Next Album |url=https://variety.com/2024/music/news/the-weeknd-reveals-title-new-details-album-hurry-up-tomorrow-1236129931/ |access-date=September 4, 2024 |website=Variety |language=en-US |archive-date=September 4, 2024 |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20240904143621/https://variety.com/2024/music/news/the-weeknd-reveals-title-new-details-album-hurry-up-tomorrow-1236129931/ |url-status=live }}</ref> and on September 13, released "[[Dancing in the Flames]]", which was then intended as the debut single from the album.<ref name="SlantReview">{{Cite web |last=Camp |first=Alexa |date=September 13, 2024 |title=Love Is a Danger Zone in the Weeknd's "Dancing in the Flames" |url=https://www.slantmagazine.com/music/the-weeknd-dancing-in-the-flames-single-and-video/ |access-date=September 24, 2024 |website=[[Slant Magazine]] }}</ref> Three days later, he released an acoustic version of the song.<ref>{{Cite magazine |last=Kaufman |first=Gil |date=September 16, 2024 |title=The Weeknd Drops Haunting Unplugged Version of 'Dancing in the Flames' |url=https://www.billboard.com/music/rb-hip-hop/the-weeknd-unplugged-acoustc-version-dancing-in-the-flames-1235775822/ |access-date=September 24, 2024 |magazine=[[Billboard (magazine)|Billboard]] }}</ref> On September 27, Tesfaye released "[[Timeless (The Weeknd and Playboi Carti song)|Timeless]]", featuring previous collaborator Playboi Carti,<ref>{{Cite web |last=Andrews |first=Elias |date=September 27, 2024 |title=The Weeknd And Playboi Carti Transcend With New Single "TIMELESS" |url=https://www.hotnewhiphop.com/845996-the-weeknd-playboi-carti-timeless-stream |access-date=April 14, 2025 |website=HotNewHipHop |language=en }}</ref> peaking at number three on the ''Billboard'' Hot 100 and becoming his highest debut on that chart to date.<ref>{{Cite web |last=Samuels |first=Keithan |date=October 9, 2024|title=The Weeknd and Playboi Carti's 'Timeless' Debuts at No. 1 on Multiple Billboard Charts |url=https://ratedrnb.com/2024/10/the-weeknd-playboi-carti-timeless-debuts-at-no-1-on-multiple-billboard-charts/ |access-date=January 21, 2026|website=Rated R&B |language=en }}</ref><ref>{{Cite web |date=2024-10-08 |title=The Weeknd and Playboi Carti's "Timeless" Debuts at No. 3 |url=https://hypebeast.com/2024/10/the-weeknd-playboi-carti-timeless-no-3-debut-billboard-hot-100 |access-date=2026-08-16 |website=Hypebeast |language=en}}</ref> On October 30, Tesfaye released "[[São Paulo (song)|São Paulo]]", featuring Brazilian singer [[Anitta (singer)|Anitta]].<ref>{{Cite magazine |last=Kreps |first=Daniel |date=October 31, 2024 |title=The Weeknd and Anitta Mark Halloween With Body Horror-Inspired 'São Paulo' Video |url=https://www.rollingstone.com/music/music-news/the-weeknd-anitta-sao-paulo-video-1235148477/ |access-date=November 1, 2024 |magazine=Rolling Stone |language=en-US |archive-date=April 15, 2025 |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20250415063024/https://www.rollingstone.com/music/music-news/the-weeknd-anitta-sao-paulo-video-1235148477/ |url-status=live }}</ref> The album was released on January 31, 2025.<ref>{{Cite magazine |last=Mamo |first=Heran |date=January 31, 2025 |title=The Weeknd Ends His Final Trilogy With 'Hurry Up Tomorrow' Album: Stream It Now |url=https://www.billboard.com/music/music-news/the-weeknd-hurry-up-tomorrow-album-listen-1235887999/ |access-date=January 31, 2025 |magazine=Billboard |language=en-US |archive-date=September 8, 2025 |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20250908040901/https://www.billboard.com/music/music-news/the-weeknd-hurry-up-tomorrow-album-listen-1235887999/ |url-status=live }}</ref> Upon release, the album debuted at number one on the ''Billboard'' 200 with 490,500 units, including 359,000 [[pure album sales]], the latter figure being the highest for any male artist since 2020. With this feat, ''Hurry Up Tomorrow'' marks his fifth ''Billboard'' 200 chart-topping album and his highest opening week sales by overall units in the country. The album also marked the second largest week opener of 2025 (after [[Morgan Wallen]]'s ''[[I'm the Problem (Morgan Wallen album)|I'm the Problem]]''), and the largest for any album since Taylor Swift's ''[[The Tortured Poets Department]]'' (2024).<ref>{{Cite magazine |last=Caulfield |first=Keith |date=February 9, 2025 |title=The Weeknd Lands Fifth No. 1 Album on Billboard 200 with 'Hurry Up Tomorrow' |url=https://www.billboard.com/music/chart-beat/the-weeknd-hurry-up-tomorrow-number-one-billboard-200-chart-1235896581/ |access-date=February 9, 2025 |magazine=[[Billboard (magazine)|Billboard]] |language=en-US }}</ref> On March 14, 2025, Tesfaye appeared on the US top five single "[[Rather Lie]]" from Playboi Carti's third studio album, ''[[Music (Playboi Carti album)|Music]]''.<ref name="billboard2">{{cite magazine |author=Diaz, Angel |author2=Saponara, Michael |date=March 14, 2025 |title=Every Song From Playboi Carti's ''Music'' Album Ranked: Critic's Picks |url=https://www.billboard.com/lists/playboi-carti-i-am-music-tracks-ranked-2025/rather-lie-feat-the-weeknd/ |access-date=March 15, 2025 |magazine=Billboard |archive-date=March 17, 2025 |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20250317041151/https://www.billboard.com/lists/playboi-carti-i-am-music-tracks-ranked-2025/rather-lie-feat-the-weeknd/ |url-status=live }}</ref>

Tesfaye starred alongside [[Jenna Ortega]] in a companion film ''[[Hurry Up Tomorrow (film)|Hurry Up Tomorrow]]'' (2025) directed by [[Trey Edward Shults]],<ref>{{cite web |last=Chapman |first=Wilson |date=February 28, 2023 |title=The Weeknd, ''Waves'' Director Trey Edward Shults to Co-Write New Film |url=https://www.indiewire.com/features/general/the-weeknd-jenna-ortega-trey-edward-shults-1234814337/ |url-status=live 2023 |website=[[IndieWire]]}}</ref><ref>{{cite web |last=Barfield |first=Charles |date=February 28, 2023 |title=Jenna Ortega, Barry Keoghan & The Weeknd To Star In New Film From Director Trey Edward |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20230301030220/https://theplaylist.net/jenna-ortega-barry-keoghan-the-weeknd-to-star-in-new-film-from-director-trey-edward-shults-20230228/ |archive-date=March 1, 2023 |access-date=March 1, 2023 |website=The Playlist}}</ref> released on May 16<ref>{{Cite web |last=Saponara |first=Michael |date=April 24, 2025|title=The Weeknd Reveals 'Hurry Up Tomorrow' Album 'Didn't Exist' Before the Film |url=https://www.billboard.com/music/rb-hip-hop/the-weeknd-hurry-up-tomorrow-film-before-album-1235954794/ |access-date=January 21, 2026|website=Billboard |language=en-US |archive-date=April 25, 2025 |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20250425034257/https://www.billboard.com/music/rb-hip-hop/the-weeknd-hurry-up-tomorrow-film-before-album-1235954794/ |url-status=live }}</ref> and widely criticized by critics, bombing at the [[Box-office bomb|box office bomb]] with grossing $7.6 million from its $15 million budget.<ref>{{Cite web |title=Hurry Up Tomorrow (2025) - Financial Information |url=https://www.the-numbers.com/movie/Hurry-Up-Tomorrow-(2025) |access-date=July 10, 2025 |website=The Numbers |archive-date=August 9, 2025 |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20250809061305/https://www.the-numbers.com/movie/Hurry-Up-Tomorrow-(2025) |url-status=live }}</ref><ref>{{Cite web |title=Hurry Up Tomorrow |url=https://www.boxofficemojo.com/title/tt26927452/ |access-date=July 10, 2025 |website=Box Office Mojo |archive-date=June 8, 2025 |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20250608193020/https://www.boxofficemojo.com/title/tt26927452/ |url-status=live }}</ref> Brandon Yu of ''[[The New York Times]]'' described the psychological thriller as being "all style and no substance".<ref>{{cite web|url= https://www.nytimes.com/2025/05/15/movies/hurry-up-tomorrow-review.html|title= 'Hurry Up Tomorrow' Review: The Shadow Cast by Blinding Lights|website= [[The New York Times]]|date= May 15, 2025|access-date= May 17, 2025|last1= Yu|first1= Brandon|archive-date= May 17, 2025|archive-url= https://web.archive.org/web/20250517005125/https://www.nytimes.com/2025/05/15/movies/hurry-up-tomorrow-review.html|url-status= live}}</ref> Charles Bramesco of ''[[IndieWire]]'' criticized Tesfaye's acting and writing, describing it as "modes of imitative, hollow performance, like a bad actor's varying notions of good acting".<ref>{{cite web|url= https://www.indiewire.com/criticism/movies/hurry-up-tomorrow-review-the-weeknd-trey-edward-schults-1235122456/|title= 'Hurry Up Tomorrow' Review: The Weeknd's Emotionally Threadbare Vanity Project Is https://web.archive.org/web/20250515224233/https://www.indiewire.com/criticism/movies/hurry-up-tomorrow-review-the-weeknd-trey-edward-schults-1235122456/|url-status= live}}</ref> Jordan Hoffman of ''[[Entertainment Weekly]]'' criticized the overall film as being a "nearly plot-free movie, [that] is self-indulgent, overly serious, and, worst of all, just plain dull."<ref>{{cite magazine|url= https://ew.com/hurry-up-tomorrow-review-the-weeknd-depiction-mopey-pop-star-astonishingly-boring-11735956|title= Hurry Up Tomorrow review: The Weeknd's depiction of a mopey pop star is astonishingly https://web.archive.org/web/20250516003316/https://ew.com/hurry-up-tomorrow-review-the-weeknd-depiction-mopey-pop-star-astonishingly-boring-11735956|url-status= live}}</ref> ''[[IndieWire]]'' labeled it one of the "worst vanity projects ever made".<ref>{{cite web|url= https://www.indiewire.com/gallery/worst-vanity-projects-movies-glitter-battlefield-earth/|title= 8 of the Most Notorious Vanity Projects Ever Made, from 'Glitter' and 'Battlefield Earth' to 'Hurry Up Tomorrow'|website= [[IndieWire]]|date= May 16, 2025|access-date= May 17, 2025|archive-date= May 24, 2025|archive-url= Allen Johnson of the [[San Francisco Chronicle]] who described the film as "a risk-taking experience" and "visually marvelous as it is head-scratching", acknowledging it was imperfect, but applauding the way in which it "questions, probes and challenges viewers".<ref>{{Cite web |last=Johnson |first=G. Allen |date=May 15, 2025|title=In ‘Hurry Up Tomorrow,’ the Weeknd makes a ‘Purple Rain’ for the ‘Euphoria’ generation |url=https://www.sfchronicle.com/entertainment/article/the-weeknd-hurry-up-tomorrow-review-20322034.php |access-date=August 10, 2026|website=San Francisco Chronicle |language=en}}</ref>

Having already set a new record for the top grossing tour by a male solo artist, on December 30, 2025, Tesfaye became the first artist on Spotify to have thirty songs with more than one billion streams each.<ref>{{Cite web |last=Zee |first=Michaela |date=November 20, 2025|title=The Weeknd’s ‘After Hours Til Dawn’ Tour Surpasses $1 Billion and Becomes Top-Earning Tour by Male Solo Artist |url=https://variety.com/2025/music/news/the-weeknd-after-hours-til-dawn-tour-surpasses-1-billion-1236586696/ |access-date=August 10, 2026|website=Variety |language=en-US}}</ref><ref>{{Cite web |date=December 30, 2025|title=The Weeknd Is First Artist With 30 Songs to Earn 1 Billion Spotify Streams |url=https://www.complex.com/music/a/jaelaniturnerwilliams/weeknd-first-artist-30-songs-one-billion-spotify-streams |access-date=January 21, 2026|website=Complex |language=en }}</ref> Tesfaye continued to set new touring milestones throughout 2026, selling out a record-breaking six consecutive shows at the [[Stade de France]] and grossing more than 120 million in July alone, while his two nights in [[Jakarta]] helped generate nearly 14 million in economic activity for the city.<ref>{{Cite web |last=Areliz |first=Oscar |date=2026-07-23 |title=A Historique Run: The Weeknd Sells Out 6 Stade de France Stadium Shows & Draws More Than 500K Fans Across France This Summer - Pollstar News |url=https://news.pollstar.com/2026/07/23/a-historique-run-the-weeknd-sells-out-6-stade-de-france-stadium-shows-draws-more-than-500k-fans-across-france-this-summer/ |access-date=2026-08-28 |language=en-US}}</ref><ref>{{Cite web |last=Frankenberg |first=Eric |date=2026-08-27 |title=The Weeknd Leads Record-Breaking Monthly Boxscore Report |url=https://www.billboard.com/pro/weeknd-after-hours-til-dawn-billboard-boxscore/ |access-date=2026-08-28 |website=Billboard |language=en-US}}</ref><ref>{{Cite web |last=antaranews.com |date=2026-09-28 |title=Pestapora, The Weeknd generate US$13.8 million for Jakarta's economy |url=https://en.antaranews.com/news/433243/pestapora-the-weeknd-generate-us138-million-for-jakartas-economy |access-date=2026-09-30 |website=Antara News |language=en}}</ref> On October 31, 2026, Tesfaye released "[[Hong Kong (song)|Hong Kong]]", the track samples the [[Faye Wong]] song "Monsoon (季候風)" and had been previously premiered in concert at [[Kai Tak Stadium]].<ref>{{Cite web |last=Monroe |first=Jazz |date=2026-10-26 |title=The Weeknd Debuts “Hong Kong” in Hong Kong |url=https://pitchfork.com/story/the-weeknd-debuts-hong-kong-in-hong-kong/ |access-date=2026-10-31 |website=Pitchfork |language=en-US}}</ref>

== Artistry ==
=== Influences ===
{{Multiple image
| image1            = Michael Jackson 1984.jpg
| image2            = Prince at Coachella 001 (upres retouch).jpg
| image3            = R. Kelly in 2017 02.png
| footer            = Tesfaye cites [[Michael Jackson]], [[Prince (musician)|Prince]], and [[R. Kelly]] as his main musical inspirations.

Tesfaye cites [[Michael Jackson]], [[Prince (musician)|Prince]], and [[R. Kelly]] as his main musical inspirations.<ref name="Scott2013">{{Cite news |last=Scott |first=Damien |title=The Weeknd: Kiss And Tell (2013 Cover Story) |url=https://www.complex.com/music/2013/07/weeknd-interview-cover-story |url-status=live |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20150614024520/http://ca.complex.com/music/2013/07/weeknd-interview-cover-story |archive-date=June 14, 2015 |date=July 15, 2013 |website=[[Complex Networks]]}}</ref> He has attributed Jackson's music as key in spurring him to be a singer, referencing the lyrics to "[[Dirty Diana]]" as an example.<ref name="Fantasy3" /> His high-flying vocal style was influenced by Ethiopian singers such as [[Aster Aweke]].<ref name="Fantasy3" /> He grew up listening to a variety of music genres, including [[Soul music|soul]], [[Hip hop music|hip hop]], [[funk]], [[indie rock]], and [[post-punk]].<ref>{{Cite news |last=Roberts |first=Randall |title=Review: Abel Tesfaye, a.k.a. The Weeknd, has hall smoldering |work=[[Los Angeles Times]] |url=https://www.latimes.com/entertainment/music/la-xpm-2012-dec-17-la-et-ms-weeknd-review-20121217-story.html |url-status=live |date=December 17, 2012 | url-access=limited |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20210107223950/https://www.latimes.com/entertainment/music/la-xpm-2012-dec-17-la-et-ms-weeknd-review-20121217-story.html |archive-date=January 7, 2021}}</ref> Tesfaye is heavily influenced by [[1980s in music|1980s music]], and credits the video game ''[[Grand Theft Auto: Vice City]]'' (2002) for "opening my eyes" to the music of that era.<ref>{{Cite web|date=November 24, 2021|title=The Weeknd: 'Grand Theft Auto: Vice City' "opened my eyes to '80s music"|url=https://www.nme.com/news/music/the-weeknd-grand-theft-auto-vice-city-80s-music-3103875|access-date=February 16, 2022|website=NME|language=en-GB|archive-date=February 16, 2022|archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20220216043000/https://www.nme.com/news/music/the-weeknd-grand-theft-auto-vice-city-80s-music-3103875|url-status=live}}</ref> "I've always had an admiration for the era before I was born", he said in an interview for ''[[Billboard (magazine)|Billboard]]''. "You can hear it as far back as my first mixtape that the '80s – [[Siouxsie and the Banshees]], [[Cocteau Twins]] – play such a huge role in my sound."<ref name="Trust2020" />

Tesfaye has named [[Deftones]] as one of his influences during the making of ''[[House of Balloons]]'', ''[[Thursday (album)|Thursday]]'' and ''[[Echoes of Silence]]''.<ref name="Metal Sucks genres">{{Cite web|date=August 22, 2021|title=The Weeknd Says Deftones Inspired His Music|url=https://www.metalsucks.net/2021/08/22/the-weeknd-says-deftones-inspired-his-music/|access-date=January 8, 2022|website=MetalSucks|language=en|archive-date=January 7, 2022|archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20220107183847/https://www.metalsucks.net/2021/08/22/the-weeknd-says-deftones-inspired-his-music/|url-status=live}}</ref> He has also cited [[Lana Del Rey]],<ref>{{Cite web|last=Mistry|first=Anupa|date=August 31, 2015|title=The Dark Knight Returns: A Conversation With the Weeknd|url=https://pitchfork.com/features/interview/9711-the-dark-knight-returns-a-conversation-with-the-weeknd/|url-status=live|archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20210107223955/https://pitchfork.com/features/interview/9711-the-dark-knight-returns-a-conversation-with-the-weeknd/?page=1|archive-date=January 7, 2021|website=[[Pitchfork (website)|Pitchfork]]}}</ref> [[David Bowie]],<ref>{{Cite magazine|last=Reed|first=Ryan|date=November 2, 2016|title=The Weeknd Talks David Bowie, Prince Influences on New 2021}}</ref> [[the Smiths]], [[Bad Brains]], [[Talking Heads]], [[DeBarge]],<ref name="bruce">{{Cite web|last=Slingerland|first=Calum|date=February 6, 2016|title=The Weeknd's New Album Is Inspired by Bad Brains, Talking Heads and the === Production and songwriting ===
{{Listen
| pos = left
| filename = The Weeknd - House of Balloons.ogg
| title = "House of Balloons"
| description = A 30-second sample of one of Tesfaye's early songs, "[[House of Balloons / Glass Table Girls|House of Balloons]]", showcasing the singer's [[alternative R&B]] sound alongside a sample of [[Siouxsie and the Banshees]]' "[[Happy House]]". 
}} 
Tesfaye's songs are "built around a fogged, [[crepuscular]] production",<ref name="Hoby">{{Cite news |last=Hoby |first=Hermione |title=The Weeknd: Sounds and sensibility |work=[[The Guardian]] | author-link=Hermione Hoby |url=https://www.theguardian.com/music/2012/nov/08/the-weeknd-sense-and-sensibility |url-status=live |date=November 8, 2012 |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20131002143549/http://www.theguardian.com/music/2012/nov/08/the-weeknd-sense-and-sensibility |archive-date=October 2, 2013}}</ref> and feature slow [[tempo]]s,<ref name="Trilogy" /> rumbling bass, and forlorn echoes.<ref name="Mistry">{{Cite news |last=Mistry |first=Anupa |date=December 23, 2011 |title=Daily Disc: The Weeknd's 'Echoes Of Silence' |work=[[Toronto Standard]] |url=http://www.torontostandard.com/culture/daily-disc-the-weeknds-echoes-of-silence/ |url-status=live |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20191129110625/http://www.torontostandard.com/culture/daily-disc-the-weeknds-echoes-of-silence/ |archive-date=November 29, 2019|mode=cs2}}</ref> His music incorporates [[Sampling (music)|samples]] that are unconventional in R&B production, including [[Punk rock|punk]], [[shoegaze]], [[dream pop]] and [[alternative rock]].<ref name="thing">{{Cite news |last=Considine |first=J. D. |author-link=J. D. Considine |title=How The Weeknd became R&B's next big thing |work=[[The Globe and Mail]] |location=Toronto |url=https://www.theglobeandmail.com/arts/music/how-the-weeknd-became-rbs-next-big-thing/article5370329/ |url-status=live |date=November 17, 2012 |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20210107223955/https://www.theglobeandmail.com/arts/music/how-the-weeknd-became-rbs-next-big-thing/article5370329/ |archive-date=January 7, 2021}}</ref> [[Marc Hogan]] of ''[[Spin (magazine)|Spin]]'' says that Tesfaye's samples tend "to draw from [[Rockism and poptimism|rock critic-approved]] sources, though generally ones that already share elements of his sexual menace", sampling artists such as [[Beach House]], Siouxsie and the Banshees and [[Aaliyah]].<ref>{{Cite news |last=Hogan |first=Marc | author-link=Marc Hogan |title=The Weeknd Imbues 'Enemy' With the Smiths' Seductive Power |url=https://www.spin.com/2012/10/the-weeknd-enemy-the-smiths/ |url-status=live |location=[[New York City]] |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20210107223927/https://www.spin.com/2012/10/the-weeknd-enemy-the-smiths/ |archive-date=January 7, 2021 |date=October 26, 2012 | website=[[Spin (magazine)|Spin]]}}</ref> Tesfaye worked mostly with producers [[Illangelo]] and [[Doc McKinney]], whom Ian Cohen of ''[[Pitchfork (website)|Pitchfork]]'' credits with developing "a state-of-the-art [[Contemporary R&B|R&B]] template" with the artist.<ref name="Trilogy">{{Cite web |last=Cohen |first=Ian |title=The Weeknd: Trilogy |url=https://pitchfork.com/reviews/albums/17280-the-trilogy/ |url-status=live |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20210107223948/https://pitchfork.com/reviews/albums/17280-the-trilogy/ |archive-date=January 7, 2021 |date=November 13, 2012 |website=[[Pitchfork (website)|Pitchfork]]}}</ref> In concert, Tesfaye reappropriates his digitized productions with a [[Suite (music)|suite]]-like [[arena rock]] aesthetic.<ref name="Mistry" />

His emotional, plaintive lyrics often express feelings of hurt and deal with subject matter such as sex,<ref name="Hoby" /> drugs, and partying; this is seen especially in ''[[After Hours (The Weeknd album)|After Hours]]'' and ''House of Balloons''.<ref name="Mistry" /> [[Hermione Hoby]] of ''[[The Guardian]]'' characterizes Tesfaye's songs as "narcotised-[[slow jam]]s" and delineates their message as "partying is an existential experience, sex is fraught with alienation, and everything registers as unreal and unsettling."<ref name="Hoby" /> Paul MacInnes of ''[[The Guardian]]'' stated that he views Tesfaye's three mixtapes as "a rough trajectory of party, after-party and [[hangover]]."<ref>{{Cite news |last=MacInnes |first=Paul |title=The Weeknd: Trilogy – review |at=section G2, p. 23 |work=[[The Guardian]] |location=London |url=https://www.theguardian.com/music/2012/nov/15/the-weeknd-trilogy-review |url-status=live |date=November 15, 2012 |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20131030193412/http://www.theguardian.com/music/2012/nov/15/the-weeknd-trilogy-review |archive-date=October 30, 2013}}</ref> Tesfaye has stated that by singing vulgar, ignorant lyrics in an elegant, sexy way, he is "paying homage to R Kelly, and even Prince to a certain extent."<ref name="Scott2013" />

=== Voice and music style ===
[[File:FEQ July 2018 The Weeknd (44778856382) (cropped).jpg|thumb|Tesfaye performing in July 2018|alt=A slight worm's eye-shot of the Weeknd, with a black cap worn backwards and a blue jacket, performing while holding a microphone.]]
Tesfaye often sings in a [[falsetto]] register.<ref>{{Cite web |last=Robbins |first=Winston |date=December 7, 2011 |title=Rookie of the Year: The Weeknd |url=https://consequence.net/2011/12/rookie-of-the-year-2011/ |url-status=live |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20130112021827/http://consequenceofsound.net/2011/12/rookie-of-the-year-2011/ |archive-date=January 12, 2013 |website=[[Consequence (publication)|Consequence]]}}</ref> [[J. D. Considine]] finds his singing's "[[Tremolo|tremulous]] quality" similar to Michael Jackson, but writes that he eschews Jackson's "strong basis in the [[blues]]" for a more [[Arabic music|Arabic]]-influenced [[melisma]].<ref name="thing" /> Tesfaye possesses a wide light-lyric [[tenor]] vocal range, which spans over three octaves. His vocal range reaches its extreme low at the bass F (F<sub>2</sub>), and its peak high at the tenor G{{sharp}} (G{{sharp}}<sub>5</sub>), with a natural [[tessitura]] within the upper fourth octave.<ref>{{Cite AV media | url=https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=Wr-K4d9BbzM | title=(HD) The Weeknd's Vocal Range: D2 - C6 | work=Prettiboimico | via=[[YouTube]] | date=January 5, 2013 | access-date=May 23, 2021 | archive-date=May 23, 2021 | archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20210523145647/https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=Wr-K4d9BbzM | url-status=live }}</ref><ref name="range">{{Cite news | url=https://www.classicfm.com/discover-music/is-the-weeknd-good-singer-vocal-range/ | title=Is The Weeknd a good singer, and what is his vocal range? | first=Kyle | last=Macdonald | work=[[Classic FM (UK)|Classic FM]] | date=March 22, 2021 | access-date=May 23, 2021 | archive-date=May 23, 2021 | archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20210523145638/https://www.classicfm.com/discover-music/is-the-weeknd-good-singer-vocal-range/ | url-status=live }}</ref> Tesfaye often makes use of his [[head voice]] to build resonance to belt out strong high notes within the fifth octave.<ref name="range" /> Tesfaye's vocals have a recognizable [[Music of Ethiopia|Ethiopian]] characteristic. Hannah Giogis of ''[[Pitchfork (website)|Pitchfork]]'' notes that "his trademark [[vibrato]], the characteristically pained whine that pervades much of Tesfaye's music, draws from a long Ethiopian musical legacy of tortured pining. Imbuing our voices with the shaky pain of loss—romantic or otherwise—is a hallmark of Ethiopian musical tradition. Tesfaye, with his [[staccato]] wails and aching nostalgia, is a young, North American addition to a dynasty of [[melodrama]]tic Ethiopian singers."<ref>{{cite news |last1=Giorgis |first1=Hannah |title=The Weeknd's East African Roots |url=https://pitchfork.com/thepitch/793-the-weeknds-east-african-roots/ |work=Pitchfork |date=June 11, 2015 |access-date=March 4, 2022 |archive-date=January 30, 2022 |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20220130004835/https://pitchfork.com/thepitch/793-the-weeknds-east-african-roots/ |url-status=live }}</ref> In 2023, ''[[Rolling Stone]]'' ranked the Weeknd at number 110 on its list of the 200 Greatest Singers of All Time.<ref>{{Cite magazine|date=January 1, 2023|title=The 200 Greatest Singers of All Time|url=https://www.rollingstone.com/music/music-lists/best-singers-all-time-1234642307/the-weeknd-12-1234643026/|access-date=May 24, 2023|magazine=Rolling Stone|language=en-US|archive-date=May 24, 2023|archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20230524221408/https://www.rollingstone.com/music/music-lists/best-singers-all-time-1234642307/the-weeknd-12-1234643026/|url-status=live}}</ref>

Tesfaye's discography spans [[Pop music|pop]], [[Contemporary R&B|R&B]], [[hip-hop]], and [[Dance music|dance]] genres.<ref>{{Cite web|last=Aswad|first=Jem|date=October 1, 2020|title=Is the Weeknd Pop, R&B or Hip-Hop? Why the Distinction Matters at the Grammys|url=https://variety.com/2020/music/news/weeknd-grammys-pop-hip-hop-category-1234789560/|access-date=February 15, 2022|website=Variety|language=en-US|archive-date=February 15, 2022|archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20220215133202/https://variety.com/2020/music/news/weeknd-grammys-pop-hip-hop-category-1234789560/|url-status=live}}</ref> His earlier work is generally categorized as [[alternative R&B]], due to its contributions in broadening the genre's musical palette to incorporate [[Electronic music|electronic]] and [[Indie rock|indie]] styles, while his later work gravitates towards [[electropop]].<ref name="Preview">{{Cite news|date=February 13, 2016|title=Grammys 2016 Preview: The Weeknd, D'Angelo and More Soulful Singers Nominated for Best R&B Song and Best Urban Contemporary Album|magazine=[[Billboard (magazine)|Billboard]]|url=https://www.billboard.com/articles/news/grammys/6813596/grammys-2016-preview-best-r-and-b-song-best-urban-contemporary-album-nominees-the-weeknd-dangelo-more|url-status=live|archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20210107223949/https://www.billboard.com/articles/news/grammys/6813596/grammys-2016-preview-best-r-and-b-song-best-urban-contemporary-album-nominees-the-weeknd-dangelo-more|archive-date=January 7, 2021}}</ref><ref name="allmusicbio">{{Cite web|last=Kellman|first=Andy|title=The Weeknd – Music Biography, Credits and Discography|url=https://www.allmusic.com/artist/the-weeknd-mn0002674162|archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20210107223953/https://www.allmusic.com/artist/the-weeknd-mn0002674162|archive-date=January 7, 2021|website=[[AllMusic]]|url-status=live|mode=cs2}}</ref> Critics have credited his early work with helping shape the development of alternative R&B.<ref>{{cite magazine |last=Shikhan |first=Amani Bin |title='Starboy' Unravels The Weeknd's Mystery and Brings Abel Tesfaye into Focus |url=https://www.vice.com/en/article/starboy-unravels-the-weeknds-mystery-and-brings-abel-tesfaye-into-focus/ |magazine=Vice |date=November 28, 2016 |access-date=May 30, 2026 |archive-date=May 20, 2026 |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20260520113257/https://www.vice.com/en/article/starboy-unravels-the-weeknds-mystery-and-brings-abel-tesfaye-into-focus/ |url-status=live }}</ref> Tesfaye shared his thoughts on the primary label during an interview with ''[[Time (magazine)|Time]]'' in 2015, stating: "Alternative R&B is in my soul. It's not going anywhere. When I put out songs from ''House of Balloons'' in 2010, people said I made R&B cool again. I'm assuming that's when the label was created. I feel honored that a good part of today's music is inspired by it, consciously or subconsciously. The only way I could have done that was to be ambitious and grand."<ref>{{Cite magazine|title=The Rise of the Weeknd: 'I Want to Make Pop Cool Again'|url=https://time.com/4011859/the-weeknd-interview-beauty-behind-themadness/|access-date=February 15, 2022|magazine=Time|language=en|archive-date=February 15, 2022|archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20220215203439/https://time.com/4011859/the-weeknd-interview-beauty-behind-themadness/|url-status=live}}</ref> Tesfaye's first three mixtapes; ''House of Balloons'', ''Thursday'' and ''Echoes of Silence'', are alternative R&B projects that draw on dream pop, post-punk and [[trip hop]], amongst others.<ref>{{Cite magazine|last=Conteh|first=Mankaprr|date=March 22, 2021|title=The Weeknd's 'The Party & the After Party' Paved the Way for our Genre-less Future|url=https://www.rollingstone.com/music/music-features/house-of-balloons-the-weeknd-best-track-1145332/|access-date=December 9, 2021|magazine=Rolling Stone|language=en-US|quote=The Weeknd's massive career was built on a foundation of dream pop and post-punk.|archive-date=December 9, 2021|archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20211209055045/https://www.rollingstone.com/music/music-features/house-of-balloons-the-weeknd-best-track-1145332/|url-status=live}}</ref><ref>{{Cite magazine|last=Scarano|first=Ross|date=November 21, 2019|title=Songs That Defined the Decade: The Weeknd's 'House of Balloons/Glass Table Girls'|url=https://www.billboard.com/music/music-news/the-weeknd-house-of-balloons-songs-that-defined-the-decade-8544245/|access-date=February 15, 2022|magazine=Billboard|language=en-US|archive-date=February 15, 2022|archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20220215180545/https://www.billboard.com/music/music-news/the-weeknd-house-of-balloons-songs-that-defined-the-decade-8544245/|url-status=live}}</ref> His debut studio album ''[[Kiss Land]]'' is categorized as R&B and [[dark wave]].<ref>{{Cite web|last=Charlton|first=Lauretta|title=How the Weeknd Went From Underground Anonymity to Superstar in 5 Years, According to His Collaborators|url=https://www.vulture.com/2015/08/the-weeknd-collaborators-chronicle-his-rise.html|access-date=December 9, 2021|website=Vulture|date=September 7, 2015|language=en-us|quote=It sounded like he was merging goth, dark-wave, and R&B with a punk-rock sensibility. It sounded like something completely new.|archive-date=December 9, 2021|archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20211209055039/https://www.vulture.com/2015/08/the-weeknd-collaborators-chronicle-his-rise.html|url-status=live}}</ref> His next three albums, ''[[Beauty Behind the Madness]]'', ''[[Starboy (album)|Starboy]]'' and ''After Hours'', are considered R&B and pop; with ''Starboy'' utilizing heavy [[Trap music|trap]] influences,<ref>{{Cite web|last=Berry|first=Peter A.|title=The Weeknd Shines Brightly on 'Starboy' - XXL|url=https://www.xxlmag.com/the-weeknd-starboy-album-review/|access-date=February 15, 2022|website=XXL Mag|date=December 5, 2016|language=en|archive-date=February 15, 2022|archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20220215133205/https://www.xxlmag.com/the-weeknd-starboy-album-review/|url-status=live}}</ref><ref>{{Cite web|last=Feeney|first=Nolan|date=November 29, 2016|title=The Weeknd's Starboy: EW Review|url=https://ew.com/article/2016/11/29/weeknd-starboy-ew-review/|access-date=February 15, 2022|website=EW.com|language=en|archive-date=February 15, 2022|archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20220215133202/https://ew.com/article/2016/11/29/weeknd-starboy-ew-review/|url-status=live}}</ref> and ''After Hours'' drawing on new wave and dream pop influences.<ref name="Consequence AH">{{Cite news |last=McDuffie |first=Candace |date=March 24, 2020 |title=The Weeknd – After Hours |url=https://consequence.net/2020/03/album-review-the-weeknd-after-hours/ |url-status=live |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20200324135956/https://consequenceofsound.net/2020/03/album-review-the-weeknd-after-hours/ |archive-date=March 24, 2020 |website=[[Consequence (publication)|Consequence]]}}</ref><ref>{{Cite web|last=Herrera|first=Isabella|date=March 24, 2020|title=The Weeknd: After Hours|url=https://pitchfork.com/reviews/albums/the-weeknd-after-hours/|access-date=February 15, 2022|website=Pitchfork|language=en-US|archive-date=March 24, 2020|archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20200324073404/https://pitchfork.com/reviews/albums/the-weeknd-after-hours/|url-status=live}}</ref> Tesfaye's fifth studio album ''[[Dawn FM]]'' explores [[dance-pop]] and [[synth-pop]] genres.<ref>{{Cite web|last=DeVille|first=Chris|date=January 7, 2022|title=Review: The Weeknd's 'Dawn FM' Is An Art-Damaged Dancefloor Opus Inspired By Pandemic Purgatory|url=https://www.stereogum.com/2171899/the-weeknd-dawn-fm/reviews/premature-evaluation/|access-date=February 15, 2022|website=Stereogum|language=en|archive-date=January 8, 2022|archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20220108204719/https://www.stereogum.com/2171899/the-weeknd-dawn-fm/reviews/premature-evaluation/|url-status=live}}</ref><ref>{{Cite web|last=Mitchell|first=Matt|date=January 7, 2022|title=Dawn FM Is The Weeknd's Grandest Synth-Pop Gesture and a Thoughtful Rumination on Mortality|url=https://www.pastemagazine.com/music/the-weeknd/dawn-fm-album-review|access-date=February 15, 2022|website=[[Paste (magazine)|Paste]]|language=en|archive-date=January 12, 2022|archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20220112024904/https://www.pastemagazine.com/music/the-weeknd/dawn-fm-album-review/|url-status=live}}</ref>

==Legacy==

===Influence on R&B and pop===

Music journalists have credited Tesfaye's early work, particularly his 2011 mixtape ''[[House of Balloons]]'', with helping reshape contemporary R&B and pop. In a 2016 roundtable, ''[[The Fader]]'' writers described the mixtape as having a lasting effect on contemporary pop music and as presenting a new framework for music discovery; they also characterized it as a blueprint that prompted widespread imitation.<ref name="TwFaderHoB">{{cite web |last1=Kameir |first1=Rawiya |last2=Hockley-Smith |first2=Sam |last3=Mistry |first3=Anupa |title=How House of Balloons Changed R&B |url=https://www.thefader.com/2016/03/21/house-of-balloons-rountable-the-weeknd |website=[[The Fader]] |date=March 21, 2016 |access-date=October 12, 2026}}</ref> Writing for ''[[Exclaim!]]'' in 2021, A. Harmony said that its dark production, explicit subject matter and anonymous release helped establish a space for alternative R&B. Harmony identified [[Bryson Tiller]] and [[6lack]] among artists influenced by the mixtape, and [[H.E.R.]] and [[Sault (band)|Sault]] among acts that adopted elements of its early mystique.<ref name="TwExclaimHoB">{{cite web |last=Harmony |first=A. |title=House of Balloons Turns 10: How the Weeknd Beat the Odds and Turned R&B on Its Head |url=https://exclaim.ca/music/article/the_weeknd_house_of_balloons_10th_anniversary |website=[[Exclaim!]] |date=March 18, 2021 |access-date=October 12, 2026}}</ref>

Critics have particularly emphasized the way Tesfaye combined R&B with electronic, indie-rock and dream-pop textures. ''[[Rolling Stone]]'' wrote that ''House of Balloons'' fused hip-hop, rock and pop, and singled out [[Usher (musician)|Usher]] and [[Rihanna]] as artists who found inspiration in the Weeknd after its release.<ref name="TwRSParty">{{cite web |last=Conteh |first=Mankaprr |title=The Weeknd's 'The Party & the After Party' Paved the Way for Our Genre-less Future |url=https://au.rollingstone.com/music/music-features/house-of-balloons-the-weeknd-best-track-24377/ |website=[[Rolling Stone]] |date=March 22, 2021 |access-date=October 12, 2026}}</ref> In 2015, the magazine published a survey of songs it considered indebted to his sound, including recordings by [[Miguel (singer)|Miguel]], Usher, [[Dawn Richard]], [[Justin Bieber]], [[Jhené Aiko]] and [[Drake (musician)|Drake]].<ref name="TwRS12Songs">{{cite web |last=Exposito |first=Suzy |title=12 Great Songs That Wouldn't Exist Without the Weeknd |url=https://au.rollingstone.com/music/music-news/12-great-songs-that-wouldnt-exist-without-the-weeknd-847/ |website=[[Rolling Stone]] |date=October 21, 2015 |access-date=October 12, 2026}}</ref>

In a 2016 ''[[Vogue (magazine)|Vogue]]'' profile, [[Taylor Swift]] said that contemporary radio was "peppered with hints of production elements and vocal stylings" likely inspired by the Weeknd; the article also described the work of [[Zayn Malik|Zayn]] and [[Nick Jonas]] as indebted to him.<ref name="TwVogue2016">{{cite magazine |last=Guiducci |first=Mark |title=The Weeknd on Going From Indie R&B Enigma to the Pacesetting Prince of Pop |url=https://www.vogue.com/article/the-weeknd-musician-profile-met-gala-performance |magazine=[[Vogue (magazine)|Vogue]] |date=May 3, 2016 |access-date=October 12, 2026}}</ref> [[Tove Lo]] likewise cited the Weeknd as "a big inspiration", saying that his work could be heard in the melodic character of some of her songs.<ref name="TwToveLo">{{cite web |last=Lindberg |first=Cailey |title=The Weeknd Inspired Tove Lo's 'Queen of the Clouds' Follow-Up |url=https://www.musictimes.com/articles/58565/20151217/weeknd-tove-lo-queen-clouds-follow-up-new-album.htm |website=Music Times |date=December 17, 2015 |access-date=October 12, 2026}}</ref> In 2020, [[Diplo]] said the production of Usher's 2012 single "[[Climax (Usher song)|Climax]]" drew on the ''House of Balloons'' era, citing its "dark edges".<ref name="TwPitchforkClimax">{{cite web |last=Minsker |first=Evan |title=The Weeknd and Usher "Climax" Debacle, Summarized |url=https://pitchfork.com/news/the-weeknd-and-usher-climax-debacle-summarized/ |website=[[Pitchfork (website)|Pitchfork]] |date=April 9, 2020 |access-date=October 12, 2026}}</ref>

Tesfaye's influence continued into the 2020s through the retro-pop sound of ''[[After Hours (The Weeknd album)|After Hours]]'' and ''[[Dawn FM]]''. ''[[Los 40 Principales|LOS40]]'' credited ''After Hours'' with helping popularize a renewed interest in 1980s pop that was subsequently pursued by artists including [[Dua Lipa]] and [[Miley Cyrus]].<ref name="TwLOS40Revival">{{cite web |title=De The Weeknd a Dua Lipa pasando por Aitana o Pablo Alborán: Los números 1 de LOS40 de 2020 |url=https://los40.com/los40/2020/12/18/radio/1608299390_545034.html |website=[[Los 40 Principales|LOS40]] |date=December 29, 2020 |access-date=October 12, 2026 |language=es}}</ref> ''Blinding Lights'' became a prominent example of this retro-modern approach; ''MusicRadar'' described it as the “ultimate ’80s-sounding hit” and noted that it synthesized multiple 1980s influences into an original contemporary-pop sound.<ref name="TwMusicRadarBlinding">{{cite web |title=How The Weeknd Created an Authentic '80s Sound in 'Blinding Lights' |url=https://www.musicradar.com/how-to/how-the-weeknd-recreated-80s-sound-blinding-lights |website=MusicRadar |date=October 14, 2021 |access-date=October 12, 2026}}</ref> ''Dawn FM'' broadened that palette through synth-pop, 1980s R&B and Japanese city pop, while retaining the darker vocal style critics associated with Tesfaye's earlier work.<ref name="TwNewYorkerDawnFM">{{cite magazine |title=The Weeknd: ''Dawn FM'' |url=https://www.newyorker.com/goings-on-about-town/night-life/the-weeknd-dawn-fm |magazine=[[The New Yorker]] |date=January 17, 2022 |access-date=October 12, 2026}}</ref>

Critics also connected later pop releases directly to the sound of ''After Hours''. Reviewing [[Ed Sheeran]]'s 2021 single "[[Bad Habits (Ed Sheeran song)|Bad Habits]]", ''[[The Guardian]]'' wrote that its primary influence appeared to be ''After Hours'', citing its glossy update of 1980s dance-pop and its lyrical treatment of compulsive hedonism.<ref name="TwGuardianBadHabits">{{cite news |last=Petridis |first=Alexis |title=Ed Sheeran: Bad Habits Review – a Certain Smash That’s Ready for the Weeknd |url=https://www.theguardian.com/music/2021/jun/25/ed-sheeran-bad-habits-review-a-certain-smash-thats-ready-for-the-weeknd |newspaper=[[The Guardian]] |date=June 25, 2021 |access-date=October 12, 2026}}</ref> Reviewing [[Tory Lanez]]'s 2021 album ''[[Alone at Prom]]'', [[AllMusic]]'s Andy Kellman similarly described its synthesizer-driven retro-contemporary style as being "in the vein of" ''After Hours''.<ref name="TwAllMusicAloneAtProm">{{cite web |last=Kellman |first=Andy |title=''Alone at Prom'' – Tory Lanez |url=https://www.allmusic.com/album/alone-at-prom-mw0003636424 |website=[[AllMusic]] |access-date=October 12, 2026}}</ref>

Critics also used "Blinding Lights" as a reference point for subsequent pop singles. ''[[Stereogum]]'' described [[the Kid Laroi]] and [[Justin Bieber]]'s 2021 single "[[Stay (The Kid Laroi and Justin Bieber song)|Stay]]" as effectively recasting "Blinding Lights" with a more overtly heart-on-sleeve style.<ref name="TwStereogumStay">{{cite web |last=Breihan |first=Tom |title=The Kid LAROI Situation Is Escalating Rapidly |url=https://stereogum.com/2154368/the-kid-laroi-stay-without-you/columns/the-week-in-pop |website=[[Stereogum]] |date=July 14, 2021 |access-date=October 12, 2026}}</ref> Reviewing [[Harry Styles]]' 2022 single "[[As It Was]]", ''[[The Guardian]]'' likewise compared its 1980s-inflected melody with the explicit neon-pop aesthetic of "Blinding Lights", while distinguishing Styles's song as a broader blend of influences.<ref name="TwGuardianAsItWas">{{cite news |last=Petridis |first=Alexis |title=Harry Styles: As It Was Review – a Pop Star in His Own Sunlit Lane |url=https://www.theguardian.com/music/2022/apr/01/harry-styles-as-it-was-review-a-pop-star-in-his-own-sunlit-lane |newspaper=[[The Guardian]] |date=April 1, 2022 |access-date=October 12, 2026}}</ref>

===Persona and visual world-building===

The Weeknd's early anonymity formed part of his artistic identity. ''The Fader'' wrote in 2020 that the construction of the Weeknd persona was as important as the music itself, tracing the persona's continuing hold over audiences to the world established on ''House of Balloons''.<ref name="TwFaderPersona">{{cite web |title=The Remarkable Sameness of The Weeknd |url=https://www.thefader.com/2020/03/23/the-weeknd-after-hours-essay-review |website=[[The Fader]] |date=March 23, 2020 |access-date=October 12, 2026}}</ref> Tesfaye said that he initially concealed his identity because he did not consider himself to have the conventional image of an R&B singer and wanted listeners to judge the music without preconceptions; he later said that the resulting mystery and mythology became part of the project's appeal.<ref name="Interview2023" /> The project was initially released with little public information about Tesfaye, while its black-and-white cover, photographed by his creative director and XO co-founder [[La Mar Taylor]], established an image that Taylor described as matching the project's "moody, debaucherous R&B sound".<ref name="TwBillboardTaylor">{{cite web |title=XO Records Co-Founder La Mar Taylor Becomes Billboard Canada's Inaugural 40 Under 40 Visionary: Interview |url=https://ca.billboard.com/business/record-labels/la-mar-taylor-xo-40-under-40-interview |website=[[Billboard (magazine)|Billboard Canada]] |access-date=October 12, 2026}}</ref>

''[[Kiss Land]]'' marked an early expansion of this approach. Tesfaye described the 2013 album to ''[[Complex (magazine)|Complex]]'' as a world he had created in his head to symbolize touring life, an unfamiliar experience after he had spent his life in Toronto.<ref name="TwComplexKissLand">{{cite web |last=Scott |first=Damien |title=The Weeknd's First-Ever Interview: Kiss and Tell |url=https://www.complex.com/music/a/damien-scott/weeknd-interview-cover-story |website=[[Complex (magazine)|Complex]] |date=July 10, 2013 |access-date=October 12, 2026}}</ref> He characterized its narrative as foreign and Asian-inspired, with Japan serving as a central reference point.<ref name="TwKissLandAsia">{{cite web |title=The Weeknd Speaks: How ''Kiss Land'' Tells the Story of His “Second Chapter” |url=https://wild1067.com/the-weeknd-speaks-how-ikiss-landi-tells-the-story-of-his-second-chapter/ |website=Wild 106.7 |access-date=October 12, 2026}}</ref> In a later interview with ''[[GQ]]'', he recalled that touring and visiting Tokyo had exposed him to "all this new information".<ref name="TwGQKissLand">{{cite magazine |last=Gordon |first=Jeremy |title=The Weeknd on His Dark Persona, Quitting Drugs, and His Grammys Snub |url=https://www.gq.com/story/the-weeknd-september-cover-interview |magazine=[[GQ]] |date=August 3, 2021 |access-date=October 12, 2026}}</ref> Tesfaye also cited filmmakers [[John Carpenter]], [[David Cronenberg]] and [[Ridley Scott]] as influences, describing ''Kiss Land'' as akin to a horror film; in 2021, he told ''[[GQ]]'' that cinema had been his "first passion".<ref name="TwComplexKissLand" /><ref name="TwGQKissLand" />

Tesfaye has also used changes in hairstyle, colour and costuming to distinguish creative eras. His tall, sculptural dreadlocks had become a defining part of his public image during the ''[[Beauty Behind the Madness]]'' period.<ref name="TwLATimesHair">{{cite news |title=How the Weeknd Got His Revenge and Became One of the Biggest Pop Stars |url=https://www.latimes.com/entertainment/music/la-ca-ms-weeknd-grammys-conversation-20160211-htmlstory.html |newspaper=[[Los Angeles Times]] |date=February 11, 2016 |access-date=October 12, 2026}}</ref> For the rollout of ''[[Starboy (album)|Starboy]]'' in 2016, he replaced them with a closely cropped cut. ''Vogue'' described the new album portrait's red backdrop and altered appearance as signaling a new creative turn.<ref name="TwVogueStarboy">{{cite magazine |last=Wagoner |first=Mackenzie |title=The Weeknd Debuts a Bold New Haircut on His ''Starboy'' Album Cover |url=https://www.vogue.com/article/the-weeknd-new-album-starboy-haircut-abel-makkonen-tesfaye |magazine=[[Vogue (magazine)|Vogue]] |date=September 21, 2016 |access-date=October 12, 2026}}</ref> Tesfaye later said that ''Starboy'' had "no real story", describing it instead as an attempt to expand on the success of ''Beauty Behind the Madness''.<ref name="TwGuardian2025">{{cite news |last=Beaumont-Thomas |first=Ben |title=‘Men Run Away from Vulnerability’: The Weeknd on Blinding Success, Panic Attacks and Why ''The Idol'' Was ‘Half-Baked’ |url=https://www.theguardian.com/music/2025/may/16/the-weeknd-abel-tesfaye-inteview-hurry-up-tomorrow-and-why-the-idol-was-half-baked |newspaper=[[The Guardian]] |date=May 16, 2025 |access-date=October 12, 2026}}</ref> Writing in 2016, ''[[The Guardian]]'' characterized the colour palette of his videos as cold blue and purple, and their visual motifs as drawing on horror cinema and softcore imagery.<ref name="TwGuardianVisuals">{{cite web |last=Reynolds |first=Simon |title=Music for the Stilted Generation: the Weeknd's Deconstruction of Modern Life |url=https://www.theguardian.com/music/2016/nov/25/the-weeknd-low-life-abel-tesfaye |website=[[The Guardian]] |date=November 25, 2016 |access-date=October 12, 2026}}</ref>

During the ''[[After Hours (The Weeknd album)|After Hours]]'' era, Tesfaye appeared in a red blazer and progressively bloodied, bandaged makeup across music videos and televised performances. The visual campaign drew explicitly on cinema: in a 2020 ''[[GQ]]'' interview, Tesfaye cited films including [[Possession (1981 film)|''Possession'']], ''[[Fear and Loathing in Las Vegas (film)|Fear and Loathing in Las Vegas]]'' and [[Claire Denis]]'s ''[[Trouble Every Day (film)|Trouble Every Day]]'', as well as thrillers from the 1970s and 1980s, as touchstones for the videos.<ref name="TwGQAfterHours">{{cite magazine |last=Lyons |first=Patrick |title=When The Weeknd Called, Anton Tammi Answered |url=https://www.gq.com/gallery/anton-tammi-the-weeknd-after-hours-interview-2020 |magazine=[[GQ]] |date=April 3, 2020 |access-date=October 12, 2026}}</ref> Director Alex Lill told ''Pitchfork'' that Tesfaye developed overarching album narratives and sought to connect individual appearances—including late-night television performances, awards shows and the [[Super Bowl LV halftime show]]—into them.<ref name="TwPitchforkLill">{{cite web |last=Strauss |first=Matthew |title=The Weeknd's Director Alex Lill Unpacks the Singer's Eccentric String of ''After Hours'' TV Performances |url=https://pitchfork.com/thepitch/the-weeknd-director-alex-lill-unpacks-the-singer-eccentric-string-of-after-hours-tv-performances/ |website=[[Pitchfork (website)|Pitchfork]] |date=May 28, 2021 |access-date=October 12, 2026}}</ref> ''[[Dawn FM]]'' extended this approach through an album-length radio-station concept: ''The Guardian'' described it as a broadcast from purgatory, with [[Jim Carrey]] serving as host and advertisements contributing to its fictional setting.<ref name="TwGuardianDawnFM">{{cite web |title=Sex Stories, Purgatory and Adele's Tears: the Album Skit Is Back |url=https://www.theguardian.com/music/2022/feb/14/sex-stories-purgatory-and-adeles-tears-the-album-skit-is-back |website=[[The Guardian]] |date=February 14, 2022 |access-date=October 12, 2026}}</ref> Taylor has said that the creative team translates Tesfaye's ideas across recordings, stadium shows, film and televised performances, while ''Billboard Canada'' described the [[After Hours til Dawn Tour]] as a narrative-driven extension of ''After Hours'', ''Dawn FM'' and ''[[Hurry Up Tomorrow]]''.<ref name="TwBillboardTaylor" /> While promoting ''[[Hurry Up Tomorrow (film)|Hurry Up Tomorrow]]'' in 2025, Tesfaye described the Weeknd project as a "15-year film" and said that the film enabled him to give the persona a visual ending.<ref name="TwGuardian2025"/>

===Critical reception and cultural footprint===

Tesfaye was named to the 2020 [[Time 100]]. In an accompanying tribute, [[Elton John]] praised his "incredible imagination", describing the shifting personae of his music and presentation as central to his work and commending the range of influences he incorporated into a distinct artistic vision.<ref name="TwTime100">{{cite magazine |last=John |first=Elton |title=The Weeknd: The 100 Most Influential People of 2020 |url=https://time.com/collections/100-most-influential-people-2020/5888500/the-weeknd/ |magazine=[[Time (magazine)|Time]] |date=September 22, 2020 |access-date=October 12, 2026}}</ref>

''House of Balloons'' has received substantial retrospective recognition. It appeared on decade-end album lists by ''Pitchfork'', ''Vice'' and ''[[Slant Magazine]]'', and in 2025, ''Rolling Stone'' included it in its list of the 250 greatest albums of the 21st century so far; the magazine's entry described the mixtape as the standout in Tesfaye's catalogue despite his subsequent commercial success.<ref name="TwPitchfork2010s">{{cite magazine |last=Zoladz |first=Lindsay |title=The 200 Best Albums of the 2010s |url=https://pitchfork.com/features/lists-and-guides/the-200-best-albums-of-the-2010s/ |magazine=[[Pitchfork (website)|Pitchfork]] |date=October 8, 2019 |access-date=October 12, 2026}}</ref><ref name="TwVice2010s">{{cite web |title=The 100 Best Albums of the 2010s |url=https://www.vice.com/en/article/the-100-best-albums-of-the-2010s/ |website=[[Vice (magazine)|Vice]] |access-date=October 12, 2026}}</ref><ref name="TwSlant2010s">{{cite magazine |title=The 100 Best Albums of the 2010s |url=https://www.slantmagazine.com/music/the-100-best-albums-of-the-2010s/ |magazine=[[Slant Magazine]] |access-date=October 12, 2026}}</ref><ref name="TwRS250Albums">{{cite web |title=The Weeknd, 'House of Balloons' |url=https://au.rollingstone.com/music/music-lists/250-greatest-albums-21st-century-so-far-list-72621/the-weeknd-house-of-balloons-3-72773/ |website=[[Rolling Stone]] |date=January 10, 2025 |access-date=October 12, 2026}}</ref>

Tesfaye's visual work has also been adapted beyond recorded music and concert performance. In 2022, [[Universal Parks & Resorts]] presented ''The Weeknd: After Hours Nightmare'', a haunted-house attraction at [[Universal Orlando Resort]] and [[Universal Studios Hollywood]] based on the music and imagery of ''After Hours''.<ref name="TwUniversalHHN2022">{{cite web |title=Final Halloween Horror Nights House Just Announced! |url=https://universalparksblog.com/2022/07/26/final-halloween-horror-nights-house-just-announced/ |website=Universal Parks Blog |date=July 26, 2022 |access-date=October 12, 2026}}</ref> The following year, Universal Studios Singapore presented an expanded ''After Hours Til Dawn Nightmare'' attraction drawing on both ''After Hours'' and ''Dawn FM''.<ref name="TwUniversalHHN2023">{{cite press release |title=Global Music Phenomenon, The Weeknd, Collaborates with Universal Studios Singapore Halloween Horror Nights 11 on Haunted House Inspired by 'After Hours' and 'Dawn FM' Albums |url=https://www.nbcuniversal.com/article/global-music-phenomenon-weeknd-collaborates-universal-studios-singapore-halloween-horror-nights-11 |publisher=[[NBCUniversal]] |access-date=October 12, 2026}}</ref>

In March 2023, [[Guinness World Records]] described Tesfaye as the world's most popular artist after he set its record for the most monthly listeners on [[Spotify]], with 111.4 million listeners at the time. Guinness also recognized him as the first artist to reach 100 million monthly listeners on the platform.<ref name="TwGuinnessPopular">{{cite web |last=Atwal |first=Sanj |title=The Weeknd Is Officially the World's Most Popular Artist |url=https://www.guinnessworldrecords.com/news/2023/3/the-weeknd-is-officially-the-worlds-most-popular-artist-741774 |website=[[Guinness World Records]] |date=March 20, 2023 |access-date=October 12, 2026}}</ref> "[[Blinding Lights]]" received separate retrospective recognition in November 2021, when ''[[Billboard (magazine)|Billboard]]'' named it the number-one song on its Greatest of All Time [[Billboard Hot 100|Hot 100]] Songs chart.<ref name="TwBillboardBlinding">{{cite web |title=The Weeknd's 'Blinding Lights' Is the New No. 1 Billboard Hot 100 Song of All Time |url=https://www.billboard.com/music/chart-beat/the-weeknd-blinding-lights-greatest-hot-100-song-all-time-1235001770/ |website=[[Billboard (magazine)|Billboard]] |date=November 23, 2021 |access-date=October 12, 2026}}</ref> In 2024, Spotify hosted  its inaugural ''Billions Club'' event in [[Santa Monica, California]], an intimate performance commemorating Tesfaye's achievement of 21 songs earning 1 billion streams.<ref>{{cite web|title=The Weeknd Celebrates Billions Club Milestone With LA Concert|url=https://www.complex.com/music/a/alex-ocho/the-weeknd-celebrates-billions-club-streams-with-la-concert |website=Complex |date=December 18, 2024 |access-date=October 11, 2026}}</ref>

The [[City of Toronto]] awarded Tesfaye a Key to the City in July 2025, recognizing his artistic achievements and philanthropic impact. The city cited his connection to Scarborough, support for local institutions and co-founding of the Toronto creative hub HXOUSE; it also proclaimed July 26 and 27 "Weeknd Weekend".<ref name="TwTorontoKey">{{cite web |title=Abel "The Weeknd" Tesfaye Honoured with a Key to the City |url=https://www.toronto.ca/news/abel-the-weeknd-tesfaye-honoured-with-a-key-to-the-city/ |website=[[City of Toronto]] |date=July 26, 2025 |access-date=October 12, 2026}}</ref>

== Controversies ==
=== Plagiarism allegations ===
In December 2015, Tesfaye was sued by Cutting Edge Music, which alleged that the [[bassline]] for "[[The Hills (song)|The Hills]]" had been taken from a composition featured in the score for the 2013 [[science fiction]] film ''[[The Machine (2013 film)|The Machine]]''.<ref>{{Cite news |date=December 9, 2015 |title=The Weeknd Sued over 'The Hills' |website=[[TMZ]] |url=https://www.tmz.com/2015/12/09/the-weeknd-the-hills-lawsuit/ |url-status=live |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20210107224110/https://www.tmz.com/2015/12/09/the-weeknd-the-hills-lawsuit/ |archive-date=January 7, 2021}}</ref> One of the producers of the song was alleged to have sent a private [[Twitter]] message to Tom Raybould, the composer of the film's score, stating that he had sampled the composition.<ref>{{Cite news |last=Renshaw |first=David |date=December 10, 2015 |title=The Weeknd sued over alleged similarities between 'The Hills' and sci-fi movie score |work=[[NME]] |url=https://www.nme.com/news/music/the-weeknd-18-1203899 |url-status=live |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20210107224138/https://www.nme.com/news/music/the-weeknd-18-1203899 |archive-date=January 7, 2021}}</ref> The lawsuit was dismissed in March 2016, with legal reporting indicating that the parties appeared to have reached a settlement.<ref>{{Cite web |date=March 11, 2016 |title=Singers Chris Brown and The Weeknd Resolve Copyright Disputes |website=Lexology |url=https://www.lexology.com/library/detail.aspx?g=ff3f8318-73d3-4c8c-8609-0653c28b77f6 |access-date=September 10, 2026 |archive-date=January 7, 2017 |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20170107152304/http://www.lexology.com/library/detail.aspx?g=ff3f8318-73d3-4c8c-8609-0653c28b77f6 |url-status=live }}</ref> In April 2025, Cutting Edge filed another copyright infringement lawsuit against Tesfaye and several of his collaborators and music companies, again alleging that "The Hills" copied elements of the score for ''The Machine''.<ref>{{Cite news |date=April 23, 2025 |title=The Weeknd Boosted From Brits, Firm Says |work=Courthouse News Service |url=https://www.courthousenews.com/the-weeknd-boosted-from-brits-firm-says/ |archive-date=August 30, 2026 |access-date=September 10, 2026 |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20260830203652/https://www.courthousenews.com/the-weeknd-boosted-from-brits-firm-says/ |url-status=live }}</ref>

In September 2018, Tesfaye and [[Daft Punk]] were sued by Ethiopian-American singer and songwriter Yasmin Mohamed, who alleged that "[[Starboy (song)|Starboy]]" infringed on her work.<ref>{{Cite news |date=September 18, 2018 |title=The Weeknd and Daft Punk Sued for Stealing 'Starboy' |website=[[TMZ]] |url=https://www.tmz.com/2018/09/18/the-weeknd-daft-punk-lawsuit-starboy-stolen/ |url-status=live |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20210107224051/https://www.tmz.com/2018/09/18/the-weeknd-daft-punk-lawsuit-starboy-stolen/ |archive-date=January 7, 2021}}</ref> Tesfaye denied the allegations.<ref>{{Cite news |date=May 18, 2019 |title=The Weeknd Denies Stealing "Starboy" Beat From Somali-American Singer |url=https://www.hotnewhiphop.com/211462-the-weeknd-denies-stealing-starboy-beat-from-somali-american-singer-news |url-status=live |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20210107224110/https://www.hotnewhiphop.com/the-weeknd-denies-stealing-starboy-beat-from-somali-american-singer-news.80542.html |archive-date=January 7, 2021}}</ref>

In April 2019, Tesfaye was sued by British songwriters William Smith, Brian Clover, and Scott McCulloch, who alleged that his song "[[A Lonely Night]]" infringed on their song "I Need to Love".<ref>{{Cite news |last=O'Connor |first=Roisin |date=April 4, 2019 |title=The Weeknd sued by British songwriters over 'A Lonely Night' plagiarism accusations |website=[[The Independent]] |url=https://www.independent.co.uk/arts-entertainment/music/news/the-weeknd-a-lonely-night-copied-plagiarism-lawsuit-belly-rapper-a8854351.html |url-status=dead |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20210107224103/https://www.independent.co.uk/arts-entertainment/music/news/weeknd-lonely-night-copied-plagiarism-lawsuit-belly-rapper-a8854351.html |archive-date=January 7, 2021}}</ref> In July 2020, a federal court granted summary judgment in favor of Tesfaye and his collaborators, finding that the plaintiffs had failed to establish a genuine dispute concerning the defendants' access to their song or substantial similarity between the protectable elements of the two works.<ref>{{Cite news |last=Brittain |first=Blake |date=July 24, 2020 |title=The Weeknd Defeats Copyright Claims Over 'A Lonely Night' |publisher=[[Bloomberg Law]] |url=https://news.bloomberglaw.com/legal-ethics/the-weeknd-defeats-copyright-claims-over-a-lonely-night |archive-date=August 30, 2026 |access-date=September 10, 2026 |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20260830202856/https://news.bloomberglaw.com/legal-ethics/the-weeknd-defeats-copyright-claims-over-a-lonely-night |url-status=live }}</ref> In October 2021, the [[United States Court of Appeals for the Ninth Circuit]] affirmed the decision.<ref>{{Cite web |title=Brian Clover v. Abel Tesfaye |date=October 8, 2021 |website=Justia |url=https://law.justia.com/cases/federal/appellate-courts/ca9/20-55861/20-55861-2021-10-08.html |access-date=2026-09-10 |archive-date=2026-09-01 |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20260901043617/https://law.justia.com/cases/federal/appellate-courts/ca9/20-55861/20-55861-2021-10-08.html |url-status=live }}</ref>

In February 2020, Tesfaye and [[Kendrick Lamar]] were sued by the now-defunct indie band [[Yeasayer]], which alleged that "[[Pray for Me (The Weeknd and Kendrick Lamar song)|Pray for Me]]" included an unauthorized sample of their song "Sunrise".<ref>{{Cite news |last=Blistein |first=Jon |date=February 24, 2020 |title=Yeasayer Sue the Weeknd, Kendrick Lamar Over 'Black Panther' Song |magazine=[[Rolling Stone]] |url=https://www.rollingstone.com/music/music-news/yeasayer-the-weeknd-kendrick-lamar-black-panther-song-lawsuit-957606/ |access-date=July 20, 2021 |archive-date=July 20, 2021 |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20210720093908/https://www.rollingstone.com/music/music-news/yeasayer-the-weeknd-kendrick-lamar-black-panther-song-lawsuit-957606/ |url-status=live}}</ref> Yeasayer voluntarily dismissed the lawsuit later that year.<ref>{{Cite news |last=Strauss |first=Matthew |date=July 10, 2020 |title=Yeasayer Drop Lawsuit Against the Weeknd |work=[[Pitchfork (website)|Pitchfork]] |url=https://pitchfork.com/news/yeasayer-drop-lawsuit-against-the-weeknd/ |access-date=July 20, 2021 |archive-date=September 17, 2021 |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20210917101716/https://pitchfork.com/news/yeasayer-drop-lawsuit-against-the-weeknd/ |url-status=live}}</ref>

In September 2021, Tesfaye, [[Nicolás Jaar]], and [[Frank Dukes]] were sued for copyright infringement by producers Suniel Fox and Henry Strange, who alleged that "[[Call Out My Name]]" was "strikingly [or] substantially similar, if not identical" to their song "Vibeking".<ref>{{Cite web |last=Bloom |first=Madison |date=September 22, 2021 |title=The Weeknd and Nicolás Jaar Sued for Copyright Infringement Over "Call Out My Name" |url=https://pitchfork.com/news/the-weeknd-and-nicolas-jaar-sued-for-copyright-infringement-over-call-out-my-name/ |website=Pitchfork |language=en-US |archive-date=October 28, 2021 |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20211028214607/https://pitchfork.com/news/the-weeknd-and-nicolas-jaar-sued-for-copyright-infringement-over-call-out-my-name/ |url-status=live}}</ref><ref>{{Cite magazine |last=Robinson |first=Kristin |date=September 22, 2021 |title=The Weeknd Accused of Plagiarizing 2018 Song 'Call Out My Name' |url=https://www.billboard.com/articles/business/legal-and-management/9633774/the-weeknd-copyright-lawsuit-call-out-my-name |magazine=Billboard |language=en |archive-date=October 28, 2021 |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20211028025152/https://www.billboard.com/articles/business/legal-and-management/9633774/the-weeknd-copyright-lawsuit-call-out-my-name/ |url-status=live}}</ref> In March 2023, the parties reached a settlement, the terms of which were not publicly disclosed.<ref>{{Cite web |last=Minsker |first=Evan |date=March 19, 2023 |title=The Weeknd Settles Copyright Lawsuit Over "Call Out My Name" |website=Pitchfork |url=https://pitchfork.com/news/the-weeknd-settles-copyright-lawsuit-over-call-out-my-name/ |access-date=September 10, 2026 |archive-date=August 30, 2026 |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20260830224139/https://pitchfork.com/news/the-weeknd-settles-copyright-lawsuit-over-call-out-my-name/ |url-status=live }}</ref>

=== Allegations of homophobic lyrics ===
In January 2019, Tesfaye faced criticism over lyrics in his and [[Gesaffelstein]]'s single "[[Lost in the Fire]]". Critics and social media users described lines in the song's second verse as homophobic or biphobic, arguing that they dismissed same-sex attraction as a "phase", fetishized relationships between women, and invoked the idea that a person's sexual orientation could be changed through sex.<ref>{{Cite news |last=Whitehead |first=Joanna |date=January 14, 2019 |title=The Weeknd's lyrics were homophobic – who cares if it's 'sensitive' to point that out? |work=[[The Independent]] |url=https://www.independent.co.uk/voices/weeknd-lost-fire-homophobia-snowflakes-metoo-music-lgbtq-abuse-a8727666.html |archive-url=https://ghostarchive.org/archive/20220620/https://www.independent.co.uk/voices/weeknd-lost-fire-homophobia-snowflakes-metoo-music-lgbtq-abuse-a8727666.html |archive-date=June 20, 2022 |url-access=subscription |url-status=live}}</ref><ref>{{Cite news |last=Dodgson |first=Lindsay |date=January 14, 2019 |title=People are calling out The Weeknd for a 'homophobic' new song that suggests lesbianism is a 'phase' and he can 'f— a girl straight' |work=[[Business Insider]] |url=https://www.insider.com/the-weeknd-called-out-for-homophobic-lyrics-lost-in-the-fire-2019-1 |access-date=August 31, 2026 |archive-date=October 29, 2023 |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20231029160607/https://www.insider.com/the-weeknd-called-out-for-homophobic-lyrics-lost-in-the-fire-2019-1 |url-status=live}}</ref>

The criticism focused particularly on the lyric "While I fuck you straight", which commentators interpreted as invoking the idea that lesbian or bisexual women could be "turned straight".<ref name="auto8">{{Cite news |last=Cooper |first=Mariah |date=January 14, 2019 |title=The Weeknd's 'Lost in the Fire' lyrics branded homophobic |url=https://www.washingtonblade.com/2019/01/14/the-weeknds-lost-in-the-fire-lyrics-branded-homophobic/ |work=[[Washington Blade]] |access-date=August 31, 2026 |archive-date=February 10, 2026 |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20260210155358/https://www.washingtonblade.com/2019/01/14/the-weeknds-lost-in-the-fire-lyrics-branded-homophobic/ |url-status=live }}</ref> While Tesfaye did not respond directly to the criticism at the time, many speculate that he later referenced the controversy in the lyrics to his song "[[Snowchild]]", saying, "Every month, another accusation. Only thing I'm phobic of is failing".<ref name="auto8"/>

=== Public disputes ===

==== Drake ====
Tesfaye and [[Drake (musician)|Drake]] were early collaborators from Toronto. In 2010, Drake's [[October's Very Own]] blog promoted several of Tesfaye's early songs, and the two subsequently collaborated on Tesfaye's mixtape ''[[Thursday (album)|Thursday]]'' and Drake's 2011 album ''[[Take Care (album)|Take Care]]''.<ref name="auto10">{{Cite web |last=Mench |first=Chris |title=How the Weeknd Went From Drake's Discovery to His Competition |url=https://www.complex.com/music/a/chris-mench/timeline-of-drake-the-weeknd-complicated-relationship |website=Complex |access-date=August 30, 2026 |archive-date=January 21, 2026 |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20260121145041/https://www.complex.com/music/a/chris-mench/timeline-of-drake-the-weeknd-complicated-relationship |url-status=live }}</ref> Tesfaye received writing and vocal credits on several songs from ''Take Care''.<ref>{{Cite web |last=Price |first=Joe |title=Noah '40' Shebib on Weeknd's 'Take Care' Contributions: 'I Saw Abel Maybe Two Days. I Was in There for Like a Year' |date=June 9, 2020 |url=https://www.complex.com/music/a/backwoodsaltar/40-talks-the-weeknd-drake-take-care |website=Complex |access-date=August 30, 2026 |archive-date=March 30, 2024 |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20240330000336/https://www.complex.com/music/a/backwoodsaltar/40-talks-the-weeknd-drake-take-care |url-status=live }}</ref>

In 2012, Drake indicated that he was interested in signing Tesfaye to his [[OVO Sound]] imprint, although Tesfaye ultimately signed with [[Republic Records]].<ref name="auto10"/> The decision prompted media speculation about a rift between the two, although Drake later denied that several of his songs were directed at Tesfaye and stated that he had no hard feelings about Tesfaye's decision.<ref name="auto10"/> In a 2013 interview, Tesfaye discussed contributing material to ''Take Care'' that had originally been intended for his own music, while also describing Drake as his closest friend in the music industry at the time and expressing gratitude for his early support.<ref>{{Cite web |last=Scott |first=Damien |title=The Weeknd: Kiss And Tell |date=July 15, 2013 |url=https://www.complex.com/music/a/damien-scott/weeknd-interview-cover-story |website=Complex |access-date=August 30, 2026 |archive-date=August 30, 2026 |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20260830220309/https://www.complex.com/music/a/damien-scott/weeknd-interview-cover-story |url-status=live }}</ref><ref>{{Cite web |title=The Weeknd's Dark Twisted Fantasy |url=https://au.rollingstone.com/music/music-news/the-weeknds-dark-twisted-fantasy-927/ |website=Rolling Stone Australia |access-date=August 30, 2026}}</ref>

The two continued to collaborate publicly during the following years. In 2017, Drake brought Tesfaye out during [[OVO Fest]], where they performed together and Drake expressed interest in a future collaborative OVOXO project.<ref>{{Cite web |title=Here’s Everything That Happened At Drake’s 8th Annual OVO Fest |date=August 7, 2017 |url=https://www.thefader.com/2017/08/07/drake-eighth-annual-ovo-fest |website=The Fader |access-date=August 30, 2026 |archive-date=August 31, 2026 |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20260831222554/https://www.thefader.com/2017/08/07/drake-eighth-annual-ovo-fest |url-status=live }}</ref> Later that year, reports emerged of possible tension between the two amid rumors that Drake had been romantically involved with Tesfaye's former girlfriend, model [[Bella Hadid]].<ref>{{Cite web |last=Rearick |first=Lauren |date=October 26, 2017 |title=There's Reportedly Some Tension Between Drake and The Weeknd Over Bella Hadid |url=https://www.teenvogue.com/story/bella-hadid-drake-the-weeknd-tension |website=Teen Vogue |access-date=August 30, 2026 |archive-date=April 21, 2025 |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20250421053229/https://www.teenvogue.com/story/bella-hadid-drake-the-weeknd-tension |url-status=live }}</ref>

In January 2019, Tesfaye appeared on [[Gesaffelstein]]'s song "[[Lost in the Fire (song)|Lost in the Fire]]". Media outlets interpreted lyrics from the song as possible references to Drake's son and to the rumors concerning Drake and Hadid.<ref>{{Cite web |last=Park |first=Andrea |date=January 11, 2019 |title=The Weeknd Maybe Disses Drake for Bella Hadid Rumors on "Lost in the Fire" |url=https://www.wmagazine.com/story/weeknd-lost-in-the-fire-drake-bella |website=W |access-date=August 30, 2026 |archive-date=August 31, 2026 |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20260831121640/https://www.wmagazine.com/story/weeknd-lost-in-the-fire-drake-bella |url-status=live }}</ref><ref>{{Cite web |date=January 11, 2019 |title=All the Bella Hadid and Drake References in The Weeknd's 'Lost in the Fire' Lyrics |url=https://www.elle.com/culture/music/a25856471/the-weeknd-lost-in-the-fire-lyrics-bella-hadid-drake/ |website=Elle |access-date=August 30, 2026 |archive-date=April 21, 2025 |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20250421140014/https://www.elle.com/culture/music/a25856471/the-weeknd-lost-in-the-fire-lyrics-bella-hadid-drake/ |url-status=live }}</ref> Later that year, Drake released the song "[[War (Drake song)|War]]", whose lyrics were widely interpreted as indicating that the two had resolved their differences.<ref>{{Cite web |date=December 24, 2019 |title=Drake Confirms Reconciliation With The Weeknd in New Song 'War' |url=https://thesource.com/2019/12/24/drake-weeknd-war/ |website=The Source |access-date=August 30, 2026 |archive-date=August 30, 2026 |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20260830215057/https://thesource.com/2019/12/24/drake-weeknd-war/ |url-status=live }}</ref>

In November 2020, Drake publicly defended Tesfaye following his exclusion from the nominations for the [[63rd Annual Grammy Awards]].<ref>{{Cite news |last=Snapes |first=Laura |date=November 26, 2020 |title=Drake calls for Grammys to be replaced after snubbing Black artists |url=https://www.theguardian.com/music/2020/nov/26/drake-replace-grammys-black-artists-snubbed-the-weeknd-lil-uzi-vert |work=The Guardian |access-date=August 30, 2026}}</ref> The two appeared publicly together again in February 2022, when Drake attended Tesfaye's birthday celebration and referred to their relationship as a reunited "family".<ref>{{Cite web |last=Krol |first=Charlotte |date=February 21, 2022 |title=Watch Drake give heartfelt toast at The Weeknd's 32nd birthday |url=https://www.nme.com/news/music/watch-drake-give-heartfelt-toast-the-weeknd-32nd-birthday-3166289 |website=NME |access-date=August 30, 2026 |archive-date=August 30, 2026 |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20260830211235/https://www.nme.com/news/music/watch-drake-give-heartfelt-toast-the-weeknd-32nd-birthday-3166289 |url-status=live }}</ref> However, Drake then appeared to direct a subliminal at Tesfaye on "All the Parties," a track from his album [[For All the Dogs]] in 2023.<ref>{{Cite web |last=Alexandria |first=Lavender |date=2024-02-28 |title=Drake Name Drops The Weeknd On New Song That Sparks Diss Debate |url=https://www.hotnewhiphop.com/721399-drake-the-weeknd-reference-diss-debate-hip-hop-news |access-date=2026-10-29 |website=HotNewHipHop |language=en}}</ref>

Their apparent tensions escalated in 2024 amid Drake's wider conflicts with [[Future (rapper)|Future]], [[Metro Boomin]], and [[Kendrick Lamar]]. Tesfaye appeared on Future and Metro Boomin's collaborative album ''[[We Still Don't Trust You]]'', released on April 12, on the song "All to Myself". Media outlets interpreted the song's lyrics as references to Drake and Tesfaye's previous decision not to sign with OVO Sound.<ref>{{Cite web |last=Price |first=Joe |date=April 12, 2024 |title=The Weeknd Appears to Shade Drake and OVO on "All to Myself" |url=https://www.complex.com/music/a/backwoodsaltar/the-weeknd-shade-drake-ovo-on-all-to-myself |website=Complex |access-date=August 30, 2026 |archive-date=May 2, 2024 |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20240502020712/https://www.complex.com/music/a/backwoodsaltar/the-weeknd-shade-drake-ovo-on-all-to-myself |url-status=live }}</ref><ref>{{Cite web |last=Rose |first=Jordan |date=April 12, 2024 |title=All of the Subs Aimed at Drake on 'We Still Don't Trust You' |url=https://www.complex.com/music/a/j-rose/all-of-the-subs-aimed-at-drake-on-we-still-dont-trust-you |website=Complex |access-date=August 30, 2026}}</ref>

Drake subsequently addressed Tesfaye on the diss track "[[Push Ups (song)|Push Ups]]", which also targeted several other artists involved in the broader conflict.<ref>{{Cite web |last=Monroe |first=Jazz |date=April 19, 2024 |title=Drake Officially Releases His Kendrick Lamar Diss Response, "Push Ups": Listen |url=https://pitchfork.com/news/drake-officially-releases-his-kendrick-lamar-diss-response-push-ups-listen/ |website=Pitchfork |access-date=August 30, 2026 |archive-date=April 25, 2024 |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20240425003608/https://pitchfork.com/news/drake-officially-releases-his-kendrick-lamar-diss-response-push-ups-listen/ |url-status=live }}</ref> He addressed Tesfaye again on "[[Family Matters (Drake song)|Family Matters]]".<ref>{{Cite web |date=June 20, 2024 |title=The Weeknd Attends Kendrick Lamar's 'Pop-Out' Concert After Trading Disses With Drake |url=https://www.complex.com/music/a/cmplxtara-mahadevan/the-weeknd-kendrick-lamar-pop-out-concert |website=Complex |access-date=August 30, 2026 |archive-date=August 31, 2026 |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20260831162636/https://www.complex.com/music/a/cmplxtara-mahadevan/the-weeknd-kendrick-lamar-pop-out-concert |url-status=live }}</ref> Tesfaye subsequently attended [[The Pop Out: Ken & Friends]] concert in June 2024.<ref>{{Cite web |title=The Weeknd Spotted at Kendrick Lamar's 'Pop Out' Concert in Los Angeles |url=https://ca.billboard.com/music/rb-hip-hop/the-weeknd-kendrick-lamar-pop-out |website=Billboard Canada |access-date=August 30, 2026 |archive-date=August 30, 2026 |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20260830190222/https://ca.billboard.com/music/rb-hip-hop/the-weeknd-kendrick-lamar-pop-out |url-status=live }}</ref> In November 2024, Drake publicly criticized Tesfaye during a livestream, asking for his song "[[Starboy (song)|Starboy]]" to be turned off and stating that "real 6ixers" did not listen to it.<ref>{{Cite web |title=Drake Tells xQc to Switch off The Weeknd During Live Kick Stream: 'We're Real 6ixers, We Don't Listen To That' |url=https://ca.billboard.com/music/rb-hip-hop/drake-xqc-weeknd |website=Billboard Canada |access-date=August 30, 2026 |archive-date=March 6, 2026 |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20260306232217/https://ca.billboard.com/music/rb-hip-hop/drake-xqc-weeknd |url-status=live }}</ref>

==== Justin Bieber ====
