In January 2017, a public dispute emerged between Tesfaye and [[Justin Bieber]] after Tesfaye began dating Bieber's former girlfriend, singer [[Selena Gomez]].<ref name="auto11">{{Cite web |last=Elizabeth |first=De |date=January 21, 2017 |title=Justin Bieber Says He Can't Listen to The Weeknd's Music |url=https://www.teenvogue.com/story/justin-bieber-says-he-cant-listen-the-weeknds-music |website=Teen Vogue |access-date=August 30, 2026 |archive-date=September 1, 2026 |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20260901122318/https://www.teenvogue.com/story/justin-bieber-says-he-cant-listen-the-weeknds-music |url-status=live }}</ref> When asked whether he listened to Tesfaye's music, Bieber responded that he did not and described it as "whack".<ref name="auto11"/> The following month, Bieber appeared to mock Tesfaye's song "[[Starboy (song)|Starboy]]" during an Instagram Live broadcast.<ref>{{Cite web |last=Duboff |first=Josh |date=February 13, 2017 |title=Justin Bieber Playfully Mocks The Weeknd While the Grammys Air |url=https://www.vanityfair.com/style/2017/02/justin-bieber-the-weeknd-selena-gomez-grammys-starboy |website=Vanity Fair |access-date=August 30, 2026 |archive-date=September 1, 2026 |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20260901121730/https://www.vanityfair.com/style/2017/02/justin-bieber-the-weeknd-selena-gomez-grammys-starboy |url-status=live }}</ref>

Tesfaye subsequently appeared on Canadian rapper [[Nav (rapper)|Nav]]'s song "[[Some Way]]". Media outlets interpreted several lyrics from the song as a response to Bieber and his relationship with Gomez.<ref>{{Cite web |last=Shea |first=Courtney |date=February 21, 2017 |title=A blow-by-blow breakdown of The Weeknd's war with Justin Bieber |url=https://torontolife.com/culture/the-weeknd-justin-bieber-selena-gomez-beef/ |website=Toronto Life |access-date=August 30, 2026 |archive-date=September 7, 2025 |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20250907122256/https://torontolife.com/culture/the-weeknd-justin-bieber-selena-gomez-beef/ |url-status=live }}</ref> The public dispute subsequently received little further attention.

==== Usher ====
In April 2020, Tesfaye discussed the influence of his 2011 mixtape ''[[House of Balloons]]'' on contemporary music during an interview with ''Variety''. Referring to [[Usher (musician)|Usher]]'s 2012 single "[[Climax (Usher song)|Climax]]", Tesfaye stated that he initially thought the song was "a Weeknd song" and said that he had been both flattered and angered by its perceived similarity to his early work.<ref name="auto3">{{Cite web |last=Minsker |first=Evan |date=April 9, 2020 |title=The Weeknd and Usher "Climax" Debacle, Summarized |url=https://pitchfork.com/news/the-weeknd-and-usher-climax-debacle-summarized/ |website=Pitchfork |access-date=August 30, 2026 |archive-date=August 30, 2026 |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20260830212356/https://pitchfork.com/news/the-weeknd-and-usher-climax-debacle-summarized/ |url-status=live }}</ref>

Usher's collaborator [[Eric Bellinger]], subsequently promoted the "#ClimaxChallenge" on social media, challenging Tesfaye to perform the song to demonstrate whether he could reproduce its high notes, while Usher posted videos of himself singing "Climax".<ref name="auto3"/> Usher also appeared to respond indirectly to the controversy by tweeting, "Have you ever seen the moon bark back at the dog?" Media outlets interpreted the message as a response to Tesfaye's remarks.<ref>{{Cite web |last=Breihan |first=Tom |date=April 10, 2020 |title=The Weeknd Claims That Usher Ripped Off His Style With "Climax"; Usher Responds With #ClimaxChallenge |url=https://stereogum.com/2079808/the-weeknd-claims-that-usher-ripped-off-his-style-with-climax-usher-responds-with-climaxchallenge/news |website=Stereogum |access-date=August 30, 2026 |archive-date=August 31, 2026 |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20260831050354/https://stereogum.com/2079808/the-weeknd-claims-that-usher-ripped-off-his-style-with-climax-usher-responds-with-climaxchallenge/news |url-status=live }}</ref>

[[Diplo]], who produced "Climax", subsequently stated that Tesfaye's early music had influenced his approach to the song's production.<ref name="auto3"/> However, the song's co-producer and co-writer [[Ariel Rechtshaid]] disputed that "Climax" had been influenced by Tesfaye's music.<ref name="auto3"/>

Tesfaye subsequently clarified that his comments had been taken out of context, describing Usher as "a King and always an inspiration".<ref>{{Cite web |last=Mamo |first=Heran |date=August 25, 2020 |title=The Weeknd Says There's 'No Feud' With Usher, Cites Him as 'Why I Make Music' |url=https://www.billboard.com/music/rb-hip-hop/the-weeknd-no-feud-usher-interivew-9439623/ |website=Billboard |access-date=August 30, 2026 |archive-date=March 16, 2023 |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20230316053036/https://www.billboard.com/music/rb-hip-hop/the-weeknd-no-feud-usher-interivew-9439623/ |url-status=live }}</ref> In August 2020, Tesfaye stated that he had contacted Usher to apologize for the misunderstanding and denied that a feud had existed between them.<ref>{{Cite web |last=Bruney |first=Gabrielle |date=August 25, 2020 |title=The Weeknd Set the Record Straight On His So-Called Usher 'Feud' |url=https://www.esquire.com/entertainment/music/a33778371/the-weeknd-usher-climax-feud-explained/ |website=Esquire |access-date=August 30, 2026 |archive-date=August 30, 2026 |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20260830184347/https://www.esquire.com/entertainment/music/a33778371/the-weeknd-usher-climax-feud-explained/ |url-status=live }}</ref> In 2026, Bellinger questioned the Weeknd's ability to perform the high notes in Usher's song, saying, "I ain't never heard you up there, champ."<ref>{{Cite web|url=https://www.complex.com/music/a/jaelaniturnerwilliams/eric-bellinger-weeknd-billy-goat-cam-newton-podcast|title=Eric Bellinger Says The Weeknd Is for Those Who 'Like the Billy Goat Vibe'|first=Jaelani|last=Turner-Williams|date=July 13, 2026|website=Complex.com|access-date=September 6, 2026|archive-date=September 1, 2026|archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20260901105613/https://www.complex.com/music/a/jaelaniturnerwilliams/eric-bellinger-weeknd-billy-goat-cam-newton-podcast|url-status=live}}</ref>

=== 2021 Grammys snub ===
Following the announcement of the nominations for the [[63rd Annual Grammy Awards]] in November 2020, Tesfaye received no nominations despite the commercial and critical success of his album ''[[After Hours (The Weeknd album)|After Hours]]'' and its single "[[Blinding Lights]]". The absence of any nominations was widely described by music critics and publications as one of the ceremony's most notable snubs.<ref>{{Cite web |date=November 25, 2020 |title=Why Did the 2021 Grammys Snub the Weeknd? |url=https://au.rollingstone.com/music/music-news/2021-grammys-snubs-weeknd-19886/ |website=[[Rolling Stone]] |access-date=August 31, 2026 |archive-date=August 31, 2026 |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20260831015601/https://au.rollingstone.com/music/music-news/2021-grammys-snubs-weeknd-19886/ |url-status=live }}</ref> Tesfaye subsequently accused the [[Grammy Awards|Grammys]] of corruption, writing on Twitter, "The Grammys remain corrupt. You owe me, my fans and the industry transparency...".<ref name="auto">{{Cite web |date=November 25, 2020 |title=The Weeknd Responds to 2021 Grammys Snub: 'The Grammys Remain Corrupt' |url=https://au.rollingstone.com/music/music-news/weeknd-grammys-snub-19924/ |website=[[Rolling Stone]] |access-date=August 31, 2026 |archive-date=August 30, 2026 |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20260830205505/https://au.rollingstone.com/music/music-news/weeknd-grammys-snub-19924/ |url-status=live }}</ref>

Reports subsequently indicated that Tesfaye's team and the [[Recording Academy]] had held discussions about a possible performance at the ceremony while he was also preparing to perform at the [[Super Bowl LV halftime show]]. A source close to Tesfaye's team alleged that disagreements over the scheduling of the two performances had complicated the negotiations.<ref name="auto"/> [[Harvey Mason Jr.]], the Recording Academy's chair and interim president and chief executive officer at the time, denied that Tesfaye's Super Bowl performance could have affected the nomination process, stating that voting had concluded before the performance was announced.<ref name="auto"/>

In January 2021, Tesfaye described the snub as a "sucker punch" and said that he had been confused by the outcome after being widely told that his work was expected to receive nominations. He questioned the Recording Academy's record regarding Black artists in the [[Grammy Award for Album of the Year|Album of the Year]] category, while stating that he did not want to make the controversy solely about himself.<ref>{{Cite web |date=January 29, 2021 |title=The Weeknd Likens 2021 Grammys Snub to 'Sucker Punch' in New Interview |url=https://rollingstoneindia.com/the-weeknd-likens-2021-grammys-snub-to-sucker-punch-in-new-interview/ |website=[[Rolling Stone]] |access-date=August 31, 2026 |archive-date=August 31, 2026 |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20260831173439/https://rollingstoneindia.com/the-weeknd-likens-2021-grammys-snub-to-sucker-punch-in-new-interview/ |url-status=live }}</ref> He later stated that his three Grammy Awards "mean nothing to me now" and said that he was willing to face criticism if doing so could help prevent a similar situation from occurring again.<ref>{{Cite news |date=January 29, 2021 |title=The Weeknd: Grammys 'mean nothing to me' after nominations snub |work=[[BBC News]] |url=https://www.bbc.com/news/entertainment-arts-55858548 |access-date=August 31, 2026 |archive-date=April 22, 2021 |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20210422152329/https://www.bbc.com/news/entertainment-arts-55858548 |url-status=live }}</ref>

In March 2021, Tesfaye announced that he would boycott the Grammy Awards and would no longer allow his record label to submit his music for consideration, citing the Academy's anonymous nomination review committees, which he referred to as "secret committees".<ref>{{Cite web |date=March 11, 2021 |title=The Weeknd Says He Will Boycott the Grammys Going Forward After 2021 Nominations Snub |url=https://pitchfork.com/news/the-weeknd-says-he-will-boycott-the-grammys-going-forward-after-2021-nominations-snub/ |website=[[Pitchfork (website)|Pitchfork]] |access-date=August 31, 2026 |archive-date=August 31, 2026 |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20260831013701/https://pitchfork.com/news/the-weeknd-says-he-will-boycott-the-grammys-going-forward-after-2021-nominations-snub/ |url-status=live }}</ref>

The following month, the Recording Academy announced changes to its nomination process, including the elimination of the nomination review committees for most categories and a shift toward nominations determined by votes from the Academy's wider voting membership.<ref name="auto5">{{Cite web |date=May 1, 2021 |title=Grammys eliminate secretive nominating committees |url=https://www.latimes.com/entertainment-arts/music/story/2021-05-01/grammys-eliminate-secret-nominating-committees-weeknd |website=[[Los Angeles Times]] |access-date=August 31, 2026 |archive-date=August 30, 2026 |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20260830185824/https://www.latimes.com/entertainment-arts/music/story/2021-05-01/grammys-eliminate-secret-nominating-committees-weeknd |url-status=live }}</ref> The Academy stated that the changes had been under consideration before the controversy surrounding Tesfaye's exclusion and were not a direct response to his boycott.<ref name="auto5"/>

Tesfaye described the elimination of the committees as "an important start" but announced that he would continue his boycott, stating that trust between artists and the Grammy organization had been broken for too long and that he remained uninterested in submitting his music for consideration.<ref>{{Cite web |date=May 3, 2021 |title=The Weeknd Says He's Still Boycotting the Grammys Despite Rule Changes |url=https://pitchfork.com/news/the-weeknd-says-hes-still-boycotting-the-grammys-despite-rule-changes/ |website=[[Pitchfork (website)|Pitchfork]] |access-date=August 31, 2026 |archive-date=August 30, 2026 |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20260830185011/https://pitchfork.com/news/the-weeknd-says-hes-still-boycotting-the-grammys-despite-rule-changes/ |url-status=live }}</ref>

In February 2025, Tesfaye ended his years-long boycott of the Grammy Awards with a surprise performance at the [[67th Annual Grammy Awards]]. Before introducing him, Recording Academy chief executive officer [[Harvey Mason Jr.]] acknowledged Tesfaye's previous criticism of the organization and highlighted changes that the Academy had made to its membership and voting procedures. Tesfaye subsequently performed "Cry for Me" and "[[Timeless (The Weeknd song)|Timeless]]", marking his first appearance at the ceremony since 2017.<ref>{{Cite web |date=February 3, 2025 |title=2025 GRAMMYs: The Weeknd Makes Triumphant Return To GRAMMY Stage |url=https://www.grammy.com/news/the-weeknd-2025-grammys-performance/ |website=[[Grammy Awards]] |access-date=August 31, 2026 |archive-date=August 31, 2026 |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20260831023630/https://www.grammy.com/news/the-weeknd-2025-grammys-performance/ |url-status=live }}</ref><ref>{{Cite web |date=February 2, 2025 |title=The Weeknd ends yearslong Grammys boycott with surprise performance |url=https://www.cbsnews.com/news/the-weeknd-grammys-2025-performance/ |website=[[CBS News]] |access-date=August 31, 2026 |archive-date=February 5, 2025 |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20250205224040/https://www.cbsnews.com/news/the-weeknd-grammys-2025-performance/ |url-status=live }}</ref>

Later that year, Tesfaye's record label resumed submitting his music for Grammy consideration. His album ''[[Hurry Up Tomorrow (album)|Hurry Up Tomorrow]]'' and several of its songs were submitted in multiple categories for the [[68th Annual Grammy Awards]], marking his first Grammy submissions since announcing his boycott in 2021. Despite the submissions, he received no nominations.<ref>{{Cite news |title=K-pop, The Weeknd, Timothée Chalamet, Billie Eilish: All the 2026 Grammy nom snubs and surprises |url=https://apnews.com/article/71fc9a830662602d17d6caa14e1a8c56 |website=[[Associated Press]] |access-date=August 31, 2026}}</ref>

=== ''The Idol'' allegations ===
In March 2023, ''[[Rolling Stone (magazine)|Rolling Stone]]'' published a report based on interviews with 13 sources connected to the production of the [[HBO]] drama series ''[[The Idol (TV series)|The Idol]]'', which Tesfaye co-created, co-wrote, and starred in alongside [[Sam Levinson]].<ref name="auto7">{{Cite web |last=Roundtree |first=Cheyenne |date=March 1, 2023 |title='The Idol': How HBO's Next 'Euphoria' Became Twisted 'Torture Porn' |url=https://www.rollingstone.com/tv-movies/tv-movie-features/the-idol-hbo-next-euphoria-torture-porn-the-weeknd-sam-levinson-lily-rose-depp-blackpink-jennie-1234688754/ |website=[[Rolling Stone (magazine)|Rolling Stone]] |access-date=August 30, 2026 |archive-date=March 1, 2023 |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20230301220933/https://www.rollingstone.com/tv-movies/tv-movie-features/the-idol-hbo-next-euphoria-torture-porn-the-weeknd-sam-levinson-lily-rose-depp-blackpink-jennie-1234688754/ |url-status=live }}</ref> The report described alleged production difficulties following the departure of the series' original director, [[Amy Seimetz]], and a subsequent creative overhaul. Sources interviewed by the magazine alleged that the revised version placed greater emphasis on explicit sexual content and included material they characterized as resembling "sexual torture porn" and a "rape fantasy".<ref name="auto7"/> The report also cited sources who alleged that Tesfaye had been dissatisfied with the original direction of the series, including its perceived "female perspective".<ref name="auto7"/>

HBO disputed the report's characterization of the production, stating that the series' initial approach had not met the network's standards and that the subsequent creative changes had been made in the interests of the production and its cast and crew. The network stated that the creative team had remained committed to maintaining a "safe, collaborative and mutually respectful working environment".<ref name="auto7"/> Actress [[Lily-Rose Depp]], who starred in the series, similarly defended the production, stating that she had felt supported and that her input and opinions had been valued during the creative process.<ref name="auto7"/>

Tesfaye responded to the report by posting a clip from ''The Idol'' in which his character described ''Rolling Stone'' as "a little irrelevant", accompanying it with the caption "@RollingStone did we upset you?"<ref>{{Cite web |last=Miranda |first=Shauna |date=March 2, 2023 |title=The Weeknd mocked online for his Rolling Stone response |url=https://www.latimes.com/entertainment-arts/music/story/2023-03-02/the-weeknd-rolling-stone-the-icon |website=[[Los Angeles Times]] |access-date=August 30, 2026 |archive-date=June 18, 2023 |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20230618131211/https://www.latimes.com/entertainment-arts/music/story/2023-03-02/the-weeknd-rolling-stone-the-icon |url-status=live }}</ref> In a subsequent interview with ''[[Vanity Fair (magazine)|Vanity Fair]]'', he said that he considered the report "ridiculous" and had intended his response to be similarly "ridiculous".<ref name="auto9">{{Cite magazine |last=Adler |first=Dan |date=May 16, 2023 |title=The Weeknd Remade Pop Music. Will ''The Idol'' Remake The Weeknd? |url=https://www.vanityfair.com/style/2023/05/the-weeknd-remade-pop-music-will-the-idol-remake-the-weeknd |magazine=[[Vanity Fair (magazine)|Vanity Fair]] |access-date=August 30, 2026 |archive-date=June 18, 2023 |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20230618173531/https://www.vanityfair.com/style/2023/05/the-weeknd-remade-pop-music-will-the-idol-remake-the-weeknd |url-status=live }}</ref>

Tesfaye disputed the characterization of Seimetz's departure and the creative overhaul. He stated that he had enjoyed working with Seimetz and attributed the changes to logistical difficulties involving production timelines and her schedule, as well as his desire not to rush his first television series.<ref name="auto9"/> Addressing speculation that the overhaul had been motivated by a desire for a more prominent role, he maintained that the series remained centered on Depp's character and said that he had initially been reluctant to play Tedros.<ref name="auto9"/>

During the series' release, Tesfaye also rejected interpretations that his character, Tedros, was intended to be romanticized. He described the character as a "pathetic scumbag" and stated that he wanted audiences to be disgusted by him rather than view him as seductive or aspirational.<ref>{{Cite web |last=Rodriguez |first=Karla |date=June 14, 2023 |title=Abel 'The Weeknd' Tesfaye Wants ''The Idol'' Fans to Be 'Disgusted' by His 'Scumbag' Character Tedros |url=https://www.complex.com/pop-culture/a/karla-rodriguez/the-weeknd-talks-the-idol-interview |website=[[Complex Networks|Complex]] |access-date=August 30, 2026 |archive-date=August 13, 2026 |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20260813162309/https://www.complex.com/pop-culture/a/karla-rodriguez/the-weeknd-talks-the-idol-interview |url-status=live }}</ref>

In later interviews, Tesfaye reflected more critically on the series. In January 2025, he said that he remained proud of the cast and crew while acknowledging that the story of ''The Idol'' "could have been told in a different way".<ref>{{Cite web |title=The Weeknd Says He's 'Proud' of ''The Idol'' but Admits It 'Could Have Been Told in a Different Way' |url=https://people.com/the-weeknd-admits-the-idol-could-have-been-told-in-different-way-8772640 |website=People |date=January 10, 2025 |access-date=August 30, 2026 |archive-date=May 15, 2025 |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20250515014015/https://people.com/the-weeknd-admits-the-idol-could-have-been-told-in-different-way-8772640 |url-status=live }}</ref> In May 2025, Tesfaye said that criticism of the series "makes a lot of sense" and described the production as having "too many cooks in the kitchen".<ref name="auto6">{{Cite web |last=Beaumont-Thomas |first=Ben |date=May 16, 2025 |title='Men run away from vulnerability': The Weeknd on blinding success, panic attacks and why ''The Idol'' was 'half-baked' |url=https://www.theguardian.com/music/2025/may/16/the-weeknd-abel-tesfaye-inteview-hurry-up-tomorrow-and-why-the-idol-was-half-baked |website=[[The Guardian]] |access-date=August 30, 2026}}</ref> He attributed some of the project's difficulties to repeated changes in its format and to competing creative voices, stating that the production had reached a point where everyone was attempting to "get to the finish line" and that "you can't force something".<ref name="auto6"/> He described the resulting series as "half-baked" and said that, despite being involved in the writing and production, he had been reluctant to exert greater creative influence because of concerns about being perceived as "difficult" in Hollywood.<ref name="auto6"/> Tesfaye nevertheless said that he remained proud of the "leap of faith" taken by those involved in the series.<ref name="auto6"/>

masters' rights over the catalog."<ref>{{Cite news |last=Marshall |first=Elizabeth D. |date=December 17, 2025 |title=The Novel Financing That Powered The Weeknd & Lyric Capital's Billion-Dollar Deal 2026 |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20260206202759/https://ca.billboard.com/business/publishing/the-weeknd-publishing-deal-partners-group |url-status=live }}</ref> On January 22, 2026, HXOUSE announced a partnership with [[Point Park University]] to launch their "Creative Direction Credential Program", providing education for young artists in [[Pittsburgh]], which began in March.<ref>{{Cite web |date=January 23, 2026 |title=The Weeknd and Point Park University Link for Creative Direction Program |url=https://www.complex.com/music/a/andrew-w/the-weeknd-hxouse-point-park-university-creative-direction-program |access-date=January 26, 2026 |website=Complex |language=en |archive-date=January 26, 2026 |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20260126080551/https://www.complex.com/music/a/andrew-w/the-weeknd-hxouse-point-park-university-creative-direction-program |url-status=live }}</ref>

==== XO ====
[[File:XO Records logo.png|200px|thumb|The logo for Tesfaye's record label [[XO (record label)|XO]]|alt=The text "XO" is roughly written and the heart tops between the letters.]]
In 2011, Tesfaye co-founded with managers [[Wassim Slaiby]] and [[Amir Esmailian]], as well as creative friend [[La Mar Taylor]], the [[XO (record label)|XO]] record label. Its motto is "XO 'Til We Overdose" and has been frequently associated with [[OVO Sound]], [[Drake (musician)|Drake]]'s record label, of which the partnership has been sometimes labelled as "OVOXO". "XO", sometimes used as an emoticon for [[hugs and kisses]], may refer to Tesfaye's recreational use of [[ecstasy (drug)|ecstasy]] and [[oxycodone]].<ref name="Hoby" /><ref name="Know" /><ref name="capital xtra">{{Cite news |title=Why is The Weeknd's crew called 'XO' |publisher=[[Capital Xtra]] |url=https://www.capitalxtra.com/features/lists/the-weeknd-facts/why-is-his-crew-called-xo/ |archive-date=2022-08-26 |access-date=2026-09-10 |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20220826202327/https://www.capitalxtra.com/features/lists/the-weeknd-facts/why-is-his-crew-called-xo/ |url-status=live }}</ref> Tesfaye would later use the word as a suffix for his given name on social media (AbelXO).<ref name="Hoby" /> Artists signed to XO include the Weeknd himself, [[Belly (rapper)|Belly]],<ref name=":4">{{Cite web |date=February 16, 2017 |title=Nav Signs Deal With The Weeknd's XO Records |url=https://www.xxlmag.com/nav-signs-with-xo-records/ |access-date=January 21, 2026 |website=XXL Mag |language=en |archive-date=January 19, 2026 |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20260119174008/https://www.xxlmag.com/nav-signs-with-xo-records/ |url-status=live }}</ref> [[Nav (rapper)|Nav]],<ref name=":4" /> and [[Chxrry]],<ref>{{Cite web |last=Lane |first=Kwase |title=With a co-sign from the Weeknd, rising singer Chxrry22 is one to watch in alt-R&B |url=https://www.altpress.com/chxrry22-the-other-side-interview/ |access-date=January 21, 2026 |website=Alternative Press Magazine |language=en-US |archive-date=February 1, 2024 |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20240201111425/https://www.altpress.com/chxrry22-the-other-side-interview/ |url-status=live }}</ref> while [[88Glam]]<ref name="Independent">{{cite web |last1=Gheciu |first1=Alex Nino |title=88GLAM Talk 'New Mania,' Leaving XO, and That 'Independent Flex' |url=https://www.complex.com/music/2020/06/88glam-talk-new-ep-leaving-xo-going-independent |url-status=dead |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20200629084452/https://www.complex.com/music/2020/06/88glam-talk-new-ep-leaving-xo-going-independent |archive-date=June 29, 2020 |access-date=June 27, 2020 |website=Complex |language=en}}</ref> and [[Black Atlass]]<ref>{{Cite web |last=A |first=Aron |date=March 9, 2021|title=Black Atlass: From Bedroom Records To Signing With The Weeknd |url=https://www.hotnewhiphop.com/125184-black-atlass-from-bedroom-records-to-signing-with-the-weeknd-news |access-date=January 21, 2026|website=HotNewHipHop |language=en }}</ref> were previously signed to the label. As of 2025, the label has eight number-one albums on the [[Billboard 200|''Billboard'' 200]]: six albums by the Weeknd and two albums by Nav.<ref>{{Cite magazine|url=https://www.billboard.com/artist/the-weeknd/chart-history/tlp/|title=The Weeknd Chart History (''Billboard'' 200)|access-date=August 13, 2025|magazine=[[Billboard (magazine)|Billboard]]|archive-date=November 17, 2021|archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20211117192515/https://www.billboard.com/artist/the-weeknd/chart-history/tlp/|url-status=live}}</ref><ref>{{Cite magazine|url=https://www.billboard.com/artist/nav/chart-history/tlp/|title=Nav Chart History (''Billboard'' 200)|access-date=August 13, 2025|magazine=[[Billboard (magazine)|Billboard]]|archive-date=January 5, 2026|archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20260105004710/https://www.billboard.com/artist/nav/chart-history/tlp/|url-status=live}}</ref>

==== Memento Mori ====
In June 2018, Tesfaye created the radio show [[Memento Mori (radio show)|Memento Mori]] under [[Apple Music 1]] (then known as Beats 1).<ref name="Penrose2018" /> The show, which featured numerous guest artists, featured songs that inspired Tesfaye's album during recording processes. The guests would also make curated playlists and host their own episodes.<ref>{{Cite web|title=The Weeknd announces Memento Mori radio show on Beats 1|url=https://www.thefader.com/2018/06/06/the-weeknd-memento-mori-beats-1|access-date=October 26, 2021|website=The FADER|date=June 6, 2018|language=en|archive-date=October 26, 2021|archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20211026204016/https://www.thefader.com/2018/06/06/the-weeknd-memento-mori-beats-1|url-status=live}}</ref> The first episode, aired on June 8, featured songs that were inspired by "some very memorable nights." Tesfaye was at the time living in Paris when airing the first episode.<ref name="Penrose2018" /> Campaigns for ''After Hours'' and ''Dawn FM'' were also aired on Memento Mori.<ref>{{Cite web|title=Stream The Weeknd's 'Memento Mori' Episode 7 on Beats 1 Radio (UPDATE)|url=https://www.complex.com/music/2019/11/the-weeknd-memento-mori-beats-1-show-episode-7|access-date=October 26, 2021|website=Complex|language=en|archive-date=September 17, 2020|archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20200917040430/https://www.complex.com/music/2019/11/the-weeknd-memento-mori-beats-1-show-episode-7|url-status=live}}</ref><ref>{{Cite web|last=Haylock|first=Zoe|date=November 27, 2019|title=The Weeknd's New Song Is Anti-Cuffing Season|url=https://www.vulture.com/2019/11/the-weeknd-drops-new-single-heartless-listen.html|access-date=October 26, 2021|website=Vulture|language=en-us|archive-date=October 26, 2021|archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20211026204015/https://www.vulture.com/2019/11/the-weeknd-drops-new-single-heartless-listen.html|url-status=live}}</ref><ref>{{Cite tweet|number=1423057369697710085|user=theweeknd|title=memento mori episode 14 💀 tomorrow}}</ref> For the former campaign, the ninth episode that aired on August 13, 2020, was made to celebrate Tesfaye's debut album ''Kiss Land'' (2013).<ref>{{Cite web|date=August 16, 2020|title=The Weeknd Shares 'Kiss Land' Demos and Lana Del Rey Remix|url=https://hypebeast.com/2020/8/the-weeknd-memento-mori-episode-9-kiss-land-lana-del-rey-remix|access-date=October 26, 2021|website=Hypebeast|archive-date=October 26, 2021|archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20211026211118/https://hypebeast.com/2020/8/the-weeknd-memento-mori-episode-9-kiss-land-lana-del-rey-remix|url-status=live}}</ref> These include unreleased tracks that did not make part of the final release and a [[Lana Del Rey]] collaboration, as well as a track that would later be released as "[[Another One of Me]]" with [[Sean Combs|Diddy]].<ref>{{Cite web|date=August 15, 2020|title=The Weeknd shares 'Kiss Land' demos, including Lana Del Rey remix|url=https://www.nme.com/news/music/the-weeknd-shares-kiss-land-demos-including-lana-del-rey-remix-2728475|access-date=October 26, 2021|website=NME|language=en-GB|archive-date=October 26, == Philanthropy ==
After being presented with a Bikila Award for Professional Excellence in 2014, Tesfaye donated CA$50,000 to the [[University of Toronto]] to fund a new course on [[Ge'ez]], the classic language of Ethiopia.<ref>{{Cite news|last=Carissimo|first=Justin|date=August 7, 2016|title=The Weeknd donates $50,000 to language class at the University of Toronto|work=[[The Independent]]|url=https://www.independent.co.uk/news/people/the-weeknd-donates-50000-to-the-university-of-toronto-to-help-start-an-ethiopic-studies-program-a7177606.html|url-status=live|archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20210107224216/https://www.independent.co.uk/news/people/weeknd-donates-50-000-university-toronto-help-start-ethiopic-studies-program-a7177606.html|archive-date=January 7, 2021}}</ref> In May 2016, during [[Orthodox Easter]], Tesfaye donated CA$50,000 to the St. Mary [[Ethiopian Orthodox Tewahedo]] Cathedral in Toronto, Canada, a church he attended growing up.<ref>{{Cite news|last=Min|first=Lilian|date=August 7, 2016|title=The Weeknd donated $50,000 to a cause dear to his heart|work=[[HelloGiggles]]|url=https://hellogiggles.com/reviews-coverage/weeknd-donated-50000-cause-dear-heart/|archive-date=July 6, 2022|access-date=May 18, 2021|archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20220706210909/https://hellogiggles.com/reviews-coverage/weeknd-donated-50000-cause-dear-heart/|url-status=dead}}</ref><ref>{{Cite news|date=May 2, 2016|title=The Weeknd Donates $50,000 to St. Mary Ethiopian Orthodox Church in Toronto|url=https://www.ethiosports.com/2016/05/02/the-weeknd-donates-50000-to-st-mary-ethiopian-orthodox-church-in-toronto/|url-status=live|archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20210218171133/https://www.ethiosports.com/2016/05/02/the-weeknd-donates-50000-to-st-mary-ethiopian-orthodox-church-in-toronto/|archive-date=February 18, 2021}}</ref> In August 2016, he funded a new [[Ethiopian studies]] program at the University of Toronto.<ref name="blm250">{{Cite news|last=Daly|first=Rhian|date=August 14, 2016|title=The Weeknd has donated $250,000 to Black Lives Matter|magazine=[[Time (magazine)|Time]]|url=https://www.nme.com/news/music/the-weeknd-6-1194305|url-status=live|archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20210107224054/https://www.nme.com/news/music/the-weeknd-6-1194305|archive-date=January news|last=Melas|first=Chloe|date=August 11, 2016|title=The Weeknd donates $250,000 to Black Lives Matter|publisher=[[CNN]]|url=https://money.cnn.com/2016/08/11/media/the-weeknd-250000-black-lives-matter/|archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20160812184641/http://money.cnn.com/2016/08/11/media/the-weeknd-250000-black-lives-matter/|url-status=dead|archive-date=August 12, 2016}}</ref><ref name="blm250" /><ref>{{Cite news|date=August 11, 2016|title=The Weeknd gives $250,000 to Black Lives Matter|website=[[The Guardian]]|url=https://www.theguardian.com/music/2016/aug/11/the-weeknd-black-lives-matter-beyonce-jay-z-drake|url-status=live|archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20210107224112/https://www.theguardian.com/music/2016/aug/11/the-weeknd-black-lives-matter-beyonce-jay-z-drake|archive-date=January He was inspired to support the center after learning of [[French Montana]]'s work with [[Global Citizen (organization)|Global Citizen]] and Mama Hope to help raise awareness for Suubi and the people of Uganda.<ref>{{Cite news |last=Malkin |first=Marc |title=The Weeknd Donates $100,000 to Health Center in Uganda |url=https://www.eonline.com/news/858296/the-weeknd-donates-100-000-to-health-center-in-uganda |url-status=live |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20210107224216/https://www.eonline.com/news/858296/the-weeknd-donates-100-000-to-health-center-in-uganda |archive-date=January 7, 2021 |publisher=[[E!]]}}</ref>

In April 2020, Tesfaye launched a line of non-medical [[Face masks during the COVID-19 pandemic|face masks]] with all of the proceeds going to the [[MusiCares COVID-19 Relief Fund]], a campaign launched by [[the Recording Academy]] to help musicians affected by the [[COVID-19 pandemic]].<ref>{{Cite news |date=April 23, 2020 |title=The Weeknd Brings Back XO Face Mask For COVID-19 Relief |archive-date=January 7, 2021 |website=Hypebae}}</ref> In addition, Tesfaye donated US$500,000 to [[MusiCares]] and CA$500,000 to the [[Scarborough Health Network]].<ref>{{Cite web |last=Aswad |first=Jem |date=June 29, 2020 |title=The Weeknd Donates $1 Million to MusiCares' and His Hometown's Coronavirus Relief (magazine)|Variety]]}}</ref> In May 2020, in response to the [[murder of George Floyd]] and the [[George Floyd protests|protests]] that followed, Tesfaye donated US$500,000 to the Black Lives Matter Global Network Foundation, [[Colin Kaepernick]]'s Know Your Rights Camp, and the National Bail Out.<ref>{{Cite magazine|date=June 2, 2020|title=The Weeknd Donated $500K to Racial Justice Initiatives: On August 7, 2020, Tesfaye held the "Weeknd Experience", an interactive [[virtual concert]] on the social media platform [[TikTok]] that drew two million total viewers, including 275,000 concurrent viewers. The concert raised over US$350,000 for the [[Equal Justice Initiative]]. === WFP ambassadorship ===
On April 4, 2021, Tesfaye announced a US$1,000,000 donation through the [[United Nations]] [[World Food Programme]] (WFP) to relief efforts in [[Ethiopia]] for people affected by the [[Tigray War]].<ref>{{Cite web|last=Shafer|first=Ellise|date=April 4, 2021|title=The Weeknd Donates $1 Million to Relief Efforts in Ethiopia|url=https://variety.com/2021/music/news/the-weeknd-1-million-hunger-relief-ethiopia-1234943921/|website=[[Variety (magazine)|Variety]]|access-date=September 10, 2026|archive-date=March 26, 2022|archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20220326175003/https://variety.com/2021/music/news/the-weeknd-1-million-hunger-relief-ethiopia-1234943921/|url-status=live}}</ref> On June 9, he met with the administrator of the [[United States Agency for International Development]], [[Samantha Power]], to discuss the humanitarian crisis of the Tigray War.<ref>{{Cite web|date=June 9, 2021|title=USAID Administrator Power Held Urgent Meetings on Famine and Atrocities in Tigray, Ethiopia, including Ministerial Convening with Nine Countries and Conversation with The Weeknd, And Announced $181 Million in New USAID Assistance|url=https://usaid.gov/news-information/press-releases/jun-9-2021-usaid-administrator-power-held-urgent-meetings-famine-atrocities-tigray|archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20210610075842/https://www.usaid.gov/news-information/press-releases/jun-9-2021-usaid-administrator-power-held-urgent-meetings-famine-atrocities-tigray|url-status=dead|archive-date=June 10, 2021|access-date=June 10, 2021|website=[[USAID]]}}</ref> Tesfaye was appointed as a UN [[Goodwill ambassador|Goodwill Ambassador]] for the World Food Programme on October 7.<ref>{{Cite web|title=It's The Weeknd! Superstar singer becomes World Food Programme Goodwill Ambassador|url=https://www.wfp.org/stories/its-weeknd-superstar-singer-becomes-world-food-programme-goodwill-ambassador|access-date=October 8, 2021|website=Wfp.org|date=October 8, 2021|language=en|archive-date=May 20, 2022|archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20220520062009/https://www.wfp.org/stories/its-weeknd-superstar-singer-becomes-world-food-programme-goodwill-ambassador|url-status=live}}</ref><ref>{{Cite web|title=The Weeknd Becomes U.N. Goodwill Ambassador for World Food Programme|url=https://www.wfpusa.org/theweeknd/|access-date=October 8, 2021|website=World Food Program USA|language=en-US|archive-date=July 6, 2022|archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20220706091727/https://www.wfpusa.org/theweeknd/|url-status=live}}</ref>

On March 3, 2022, he partnered with the WFP to launch the XO Humanitarian Fund. Through the fund, Tesfaye was to donate US$1 from every ticket sold at his [[After Hours til Dawn Tour]], in addition to a US$500,000 donation, to the WFP.<ref>{{Cite web |last=Aswad |first=Jem |date=March 3, 2022 |title=The Weeknd Unveils Stadium Tour Dates, Launches XO Humanitarian Fund With Combined $1.5 Million Donation |url=https://variety.com/2022/music/news/the-weeknd-stadium-tour-dates-un-world-food-programme-1235195142/ |access-date=March 3, 2022 |website=Variety |language=en-US |archive-date=August 21, 2022 |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20220821204644/https://variety.com/2022/music/news/the-weeknd-stadium-tour-dates-un-world-food-programme-1235195142/ |url-status=live }}</ref> $2,500,000 from the XO Humanitarian Fund was directed toward WFP's humanitarian response efforts in the [[Gaza Strip]], providing four million emergency meals for those affected by the [[Israeli invasion of the Gaza Strip]].<ref>{{Cite web |last=Jones |first=Abby |date=December 1, 2023 |title=The Weeknd Donates $2.5 Million to Gaza Food Deliveries |url=https://consequence.net/2023/12/the-weeknd-donates-emergency-food-deliveries-gaza/ |access-date=December 1, 2023 |website=Consequence of Sound |language=en-US |archive-date=March 8, 2025 |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20250308091328/https://consequence.net/2023/12/the-weeknd-donates-emergency-food-deliveries-gaza/ |url-status=live }}</ref><ref>{{Cite web |last=Cho |first=Regina |date=December 1, 2023 |title=The Weeknd To Provide 4 Million Emergency Meals To Humanitarian Efforts In Gaza |url=https://www.vibe.com/news/entertainment/the-weeknd-provide-4-million-emergency-meals-to-gaza-1234824577/ |access-date=December 1, 2023 |website=Vibe |language=en-US}}</ref> On April 29, 2024, Tesfaye announced another $2 million donation to the WFP to provide loaves of bread in Gaza.<ref>{{cite magazine |title=The Weeknd Donates $2 Million to Provide 18 Million Loaves of Bread to Gaza Families |magazine=[[Rolling Stone]] |date=April 29, 2024 |url=https://www.rollingstone.com/music/music-news/the-weeknd-2-million-dollar-donation-gaza-food-aid-1235011969/ |access-date=April 29, 2024 |archive-date=July 29, 2025 |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20250729111130/https://www.rollingstone.com/music/music-news/the-weeknd-2-million-dollar-donation-gaza-food-aid-1235011969/ |url-status=live }}</ref> On January 17, 2025, it was announced that Tesfaye had pledged a further $1 million to support relief efforts in the [[January 2025 Southern California wildfires|Southern California wildfires]].<ref>{{Cite web |last=Horowitz |first=Steven J. |date=January 17, 2025 |title=The Weeknd Donates $1 Million to Los Angeles Wildfire Relief Efforts |url=https://variety.com/2025/music/news/the-weeknd-donate-1-million-los-angeles-wildfire-relief-1236277462/ |access-date=May 25, 2026 |website=Variety |language=en-US |archive-date=August 9, 2026 |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20260809151656/https://variety.com/2025/music/news/the-weeknd-donate-1-million-los-angeles-wildfire-relief-1236277462/ |url-status=live }}</ref> In November 2025, he announced a donation of $350,000 in support of the WFP's response to [[Hurricane Melissa]].<ref>{{Cite web |last=Samuels |first=Keithan |date=November 15, 2025 |title=The Weeknd Donates $350,000 for Hurricane Melissa Relief in Jamaica |url=https://ratedrnb.com/2025/11/the-weeknd-donates-350000-for-hurricane-melissa-relief-in-jamaica/ |access-date=November 17, 2025 |website=Rated R&B |language=en |archive-date=November 15, 2025 |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20251115053741/https://ratedrnb.com/2025/11/the-weeknd-donates-350000-for-hurricane-melissa-relief-in-jamaica/ |url-status=live }}</ref> In 2026 it was announced that Tesfaye had once again partnered with WFP and would be directing a portion of the proceeds from his tour legs in Latin America and Europe to the XO Humanitarian Fund.<ref>{{Cite web |title=The Weeknd Announces 2026 Extension of Stadium Tour |url=https://wfpusa.org/news/the-weeknd-2026-extension-after-hours-til-dawn-tour/ |access-date=August 13, 2026 |website=World Food Program USA |language=en-US |archive-date=August 13, 2026 |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20260813124715/https://wfpusa.org/news/the-weeknd-2026-extension-after-hours-til-dawn-tour/ |url-status=live }}</ref>

== Personal life ==

Tesfaye has generally sought to keep his personal life out of the public eye. In a 2018 interview with ''[[Time (magazine)|Time]]'', he attributed his reluctance to give interviews to nervousness, saying that he would feel uncomfortable appearing in a live television interview. He also described feeling as though someone was watching him, which contributed to his decision to live in a more secluded area of Los Angeles.<ref name="Time2018">{{cite magazine |last=Brown |first=Kara |title=The Weeknd on Fame, Love and 'Melancholy' |url=https://time.com/collections/next-generation-leaders/5277969/the-weeknd-next-generation-leaders/ |magazine=Time |date=17 May 2018}}</ref> In May 2025, Tesfaye told ''[[Variety (magazine)|Variety]]'' that fame could be a "full-time job", adding that he had no interest in treating it as one.<ref>{{cite web |title=The Weeknd Says Fame Can Be a 'Full-Time' Job: 'I Have No Interest in That' |url=https://variety.com/2025/scene/news/the-weeknd-fame-full-time-job-no-interest-1236397537/ |website=Variety |date=14 May 2025}}</ref>

Tesfaye's father left the family when he was very young. In a 2015 interview with ''[[Rolling Stone]]'', Tesfaye said that he had seen his father only twice during his childhood, first briefly when he was about six and again when he was 11 or 12, by which time his father had a new family. He said that he did not resent his father, but described him as having been absent from his upbringing.<ref>{{cite magazine |title=The Weeknd's Dark Twisted Fantasy |url=https://au.rollingstone.com/music/music-news/the-weeknds-dark-twisted-fantasy-927/ |magazine=Rolling Stone Australia |date=21 October 2015}}</ref> He said in 2025 that he had found father figures in friendships and in a stepfather who entered his life later.<ref name="TwGuardian2025"/>

Tesfaye was raised in the [[Ethiopian Orthodox Tewahedo Church]], grew up listening to Ethiopian music at home, and spoke [[Amharic]] as his first language. He has said that the tone and rhythm of Amharic, as well as Ethiopian music, influenced his vocal style and vibrato.<ref name="Interview2023" /> In a 2020 interview, he said that he had been raised very religiously but expressed uncertainty about whether he remained religious as an adult.<ref>{{cite news |last=Lamont |first=Tom |title=The Weeknd: 'Drugs were a crutch for me' |url=https://www.theguardian.com/music/2016/dec/03/the-weeknd-abel-tesfaye-interview-music-tom-lamont |work=The Guardian |date=3 December 2016}}</ref><ref>{{cite web |title=The Weeknd Goes Track-by-Track (Almost) on 'After Hours' |url=https://www.yahoo.com/entertainment/weeknd-goes-track-track-almost-133030178.html |website=Yahoo Entertainment |date=20 March 2020}}</ref>

Tesfaye has described cinema as a major personal interest. In a 2023 interview with ''[[Interview (magazine)|Interview]]'', he discussed the importance of film to his artistic interests and the influence of films on his music videos. In the same interview, he publicly disclosed that he has attention deficit hyperactivity disorder (ADHD).<ref name="Interview2023">{{cite magazine |last=O. Harris |first=Jeremy |title=The Weeknd Introduces Us to Abel Tesfaye |url=https://www.interviewmagazine.com/music/the-weeknd-introduces-us-to-abel-tesfaye |magazine=Interview |date=14 April 2023}}</ref>

Tesfaye is an avid video game enthusiast. He has cited the influence of ''[[Grand Theft Auto: Vice City]]'' on his musical tastes, recalling that playing the game as a child introduced him to music by artists such as [[Hall & Oates]] and Michael Jackson.<ref>{{cite web |last=Shutler |first=Ali |title=The Weeknd: 'Grand Theft Auto: Vice City' "opened my eyes to ’80s music" |url=https://www.nme.com/news/music/the-weeknd-grand-theft-auto-vice-city-3103875 |website=NME |date=3 December 2021}}</ref> In 2026, he discussed his interest in games including ''[[Elden Ring]]'' and the ''[[Persona (franchise)|Persona]]'' series, and met with the developers of the independent game ''About Fishing'' to discuss its design.<ref>{{cite news |last=Park |first=Gene |title=The Weeknd is busy gaming — and he wants everyone to play this indie gem |url=https://www.washingtonpost.com/entertainment/video-games/2026/08/09/weeknd-is-busy-gaming-he-wants-everyone-play-about-fishing/ |newspaper=The Washington Post |date=9 August 2026}}</ref>

Tesfaye's relationships have attracted substantial media and paparazzi attention.<ref name="Time2018" /><ref>{{cite magazine |title=The Weeknd on Going From Indie R&B to Prince of Pop |url=https://www.vogue.com/article/the-weeknd-musician-profile-met-gala-performance |magazine=[[Vogue (magazine)|Vogue]] |date=May 3, 2016}}</ref> From April 2015 to August 2019, Tesfaye was in an on-again, off-again relationship with American model [[Bella Hadid]]. Hadid appeared in the music video for his 2015 single "[[In the Night (The Weeknd song)|In the Night]]".<ref>{{cite web |last=Lindig |first=Sarah |title=Bella Hadid Spends Her Birthday Weekend with the Weeknd |url=https://www.elle.com/culture/celebrities/news/a30635/bella-hadid-the-weeknd-relationship/ |website=Elle |date=October 10, 2015}}</ref><ref>{{cite web |title=Why Bella Hadid and The Weeknd Reportedly Broke Up for the Second Time |url=https://www.elle.com/culture/celebrities/a28607262/bella-hadid-the-weeknd-breakup/ |website=Elle |date=August 6, 2019}}</ref> Tesfaye dated American singer and actress [[Selena Gomez]] from January to October 2017.<ref>{{cite web |last=Yagoda |first=Maria |title=Everything We Know About Selena Gomez and The Weeknd's Whirlwind Romance |url=https://people.com/music/everything-we-know-about-selena-gomez-and-the-weeknds-whirlwind-romance/ |website=People |date=May 25, 2017}}</ref><ref>{{cite web |last1=Chiu |first1=Melody |last2=Adams |first2=Char |title=Selena Gomez and The Weeknd Split After 10 Months Together |url=https://people.com/music/selena-gomez-the-weeknd-split/ |website=People |date=October 30, 2017}}</ref> The release of his 2018 EP ''[[My Dear Melancholy,]]'' prompted media speculation that its lyrics referred to his relationships with Gomez and Hadid; ''[[The Washington Post]]'' and ''[[Vanity Fair (magazine)|Vanity Fair]]'' identified lyrics they interpreted as alluding to the two women.<ref>{{cite news |title=The Weeknd Dropped an EP That Seems to Reference His Exes Selena Gomez and Bella Hadid |url=https://www.washingtonpost.com/news/arts-and-entertainment/wp/2018/03/30/the-weeknd-dropped-his-ep-my-dear-melancholy-and-it-is-unsurprisingly-full-of-sadness/ |newspaper=[[The Washington Post]] |date=March 30, 2018}}</ref><ref>{{cite magazine |last=Duboff |first=Josh |title=The Weeknd Seemingly References Exes Selena Gomez and Bella Hadid on His New Album |url=https://www.vanityfair.com/style/2018/03/the-weeknd-selena-gomez-bella-hadid-lyrics-my-dear-melancholy |magazine=[[Vanity Fair (magazine)|Vanity Fair]] |date=March 30, 2018}}</ref> In 2025, Tesfaye said that the EP had felt "more personal again" and allowed him to focus on "the heartbreak side, the vulnerable side", but declined to identify a particular breakup as its inspiration.<ref>{{cite news |last=Beaumont-Thomas |first=Ben |title='Men Run Away from Vulnerability': The Weeknd on Blinding Success, Panic Attacks and Why ''The Idol'' Was 'Half-Baked' |url=https://www.theguardian.com/music/2025/may/16/the-weeknd-abel-tesfaye-inteview-hurry-up-tomorrow-and-why-the-idol-was-half-baked |newspaper=[[The Guardian]] |date=May 16, 2025}}</ref> In a 2018 interview with ''Time'', Tesfaye declined to discuss the specifics of his relationships, saying that he did not want to "open that Pandora's box".<ref name="Time2018" /> In 2022, he was publicly linked to Saudi-born DJ Simi Khadra, with whom he was photographed at public events in subsequent years.<ref>{{cite web |last=Lutkin |first=Aimée |title=All About The Weeknd's Girlfriend, Simi Khadra |url=https://www.elle.com/culture/celebrities/a43611543/who-is-simi-khadra-the-weeknd-girlfriend/ |website=Elle |date=March 31, 2025}}</ref>

In January 2015, Tesfaye was arrested in [[Las Vegas]] after police alleged that he punched an officer who was intervening in a fight. In October 2015, he pleaded no contest to a misdemeanor battery charge and avoided jail time under an agreement that required him to complete community service and fulfil other conditions.<ref>{{cite news |title=The Weeknd avoids jail time after punching police officer |url=https://www.theguardian.com/music/2015/oct/23/the-weeknd-avoids-jail-time-after-punching-police-officer |work=The Guardian |date=23 October 2015}}</ref> In a 2020 interview, he described the period surrounding his early rise to fame as a "real rock-star era" that he was "not really proud of". He also connected the sirens at the end of his song "[[Faith (The Weeknd song)|Faith]]" to the arrest.<ref>{{cite web |last=Willman |first=Chris |title=The Weeknd Opens Up About His Past, Turning 30 and Getting Vulnerable on 'After Hours' |url=https://variety.com/2020/music/news/the-weeknd-after-hours-track-by-track-1203540782/ |website=Variety |date=20 March 2020}}</ref>

In August 2022, Tesfaye cancelled a concert at [[SoFi Stadium]] in [[Inglewood, California]], after losing his voice during the performance of his opening song. In a 2025 interview with ''[[The Guardian]]'', he said that he later learned he had been experiencing a panic attack rather than a physical injury. The concert was rescheduled, and he attributed the episode in part to the demands of touring while simultaneously filming the television series ''[[The Idol (TV series)|The Idol]]''.<ref>{{cite news |last=Beaumont-Thomas |first=Ben |title='Men run away from vulnerability': The Weeknd on blinding success, panic attacks and why The Idol was 'half-baked' |url=https://www.theguardian.com/music/2025/may/16/the-weeknd-abel-tesfaye-inteview-hurry-up-tomorrow-and-why-the-idol-was-half-baked |work=The Guardian |date=16 May 2025}}</ref>

Tesfaye has discussed changes in his substance use over the course of his career. In a 2021 interview with ''[[GQ]]'', he described himself as "sober lite", saying that he had stopped using hard drugs while continuing to use marijuana and drink alcohol occasionally.<ref>{{cite magazine |last=Green |first=Mark Anthony |title=The Weeknd vs. Abel Tesfaye |url=https://www.gq.com/story/the-weeknd-september-cover-interview |magazine=GQ |date=2 August 2021}}</ref> In 2025, he said that earlier in his career he had sometimes relied on alcohol to manage nerves and anxiety about performing.<ref name="TwGuardian2025" />

== Accolades ==
{{see also|List of awards and nominations received by the Weeknd}}
Tesfaye has won four [[Grammy Awards]], a [[Latin Grammy Awards|Latin Grammy Award]], twenty [[Billboard Music Awards|''Billboard'' Music Awards]], six [[American Music Award]]s, three [[MTV Video Music Awards]] and twenty-two [[Juno Award]]s.<ref>{{Cite news |last=Bell |first=David |title=The Weeknd really cleaned up this Juno Awards weekend |url=https://www.cbc.ca/news/canada/calgary/junos-sunday-night-1.3517082 |url-status=live |date=April 3, 2016 |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20210107224133/https://www.cbc.ca/news/canada/calgary/junos-sunday-night-1.3517082 |archive-date=January 7, 2021 |website=[[CBC News]]}}</ref> He has also received nominations for an [[Academy Awards|Academy Award]] and a [[Primetime Emmy Awards|Primetime Emmy Award]].<ref name="grammy2">{{Cite web|title=The Weeknd|url=https://www.grammy.com/artists/The-Weeknd/24884|url-status=live|archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20170419192514/https://www.grammy.com/artist/the-weeknd|archive-date=April 19, 2017|publisher=[[Grammy Awards]]}}</ref>

In October 2014, Tesfaye was awarded the [[Allan Slaight]] Honour by [[Canada's Walk of Fame]] for "making a positive impact in the fields of music, film, literature, visual or performing arts, sports, innovation or philanthropy".<ref name="slaight">{{Cite press release |title=The Weeknd honoured with Allan Slaight Award from Canada's Walk of Fame |date=February 14, 2015 |publisher=[[Canada's Walk of Fame]] |via=[[Cision]] |url=https://www.newswire.ca/news-releases/the-weeknd-honoured-with-allan-slaight-award-from-canadas-walk-of-fame-515623821.html |url-status=live |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20150214052542/http://www.canadaswalkoffame.com/news/story/the-weeknd-honoured-with-allan-slaight-award-from-canadas-walk-of-fame |archive-date=February 14, 2015}}</ref> [[Toronto]] mayor [[John Tory]] announced that the city would observe February 7, 2021, as the Weeknd Day to commemorate Tesfaye's [[Super Bowl LV halftime show|Super Bowl halftime performance]].<ref>{{Cite magazine|last=Peters|first=Mitchell|date=February 7, 2021|title=Toronto Declares 'The Weeknd Day' to Honor Singer's Super Bowl Performance|url=https://www.billboard.com/music/music-news/toronto-the-weeknd-day-super-bowl-9522634/|access-date=December 3, 2021|magazine=Billboard|language=en-US|archive-date=February 11, 2025|archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20250211082209/https://www.billboard.com/music/music-news/toronto-the-weeknd-day-super-bowl-9522634/|url-status=live}}</ref><ref>{{Cite web|date=February 7, 2021|title=The Weeknd has been given his own day by Toronto mayor|url=https://www.nme.com/news/music/the-weeknd-given-own-day-toronto-mayor-2874646|access-date=December 3, 2021|website=NME|language=en-GB|archive-date=April 14, 2025|archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20250414094113/https://www.nme.com/news/music/the-weeknd-given-own-day-toronto-mayor-2874646|url-status=live}}</ref>
